LG1860	1	FREE, fresh, savage,
LG1860	2	Fluent, luxuriant, self-content, fond of persons and places,
LG1860	3	Fond of fish-shape Paumanok, where I was born,
LG1860	4	Fond of the sea—lusty-begotten and various,
LG1860	5	Boy of the Mannahatta, the city of ships, my city,
LG1860	6	Or raised inland, or of the south savannas,
LG1860	7	Or full-breath'd on Californian air, or Texan or Cuban air,
LG1860	8	Tallying, vocalizing all—resounding Niagara—resounding Missouri,
LG1860	9	Or rude in my home in Kanuck woods,
LG1860	10	Or wandering and hunting, my drink water, my diet meat,
LG1860	11	Or withdrawn to muse and meditate in some deep recess,
LG1860	12	Far from the clank of crowds, an interval passing,rapt and happy,
LG1860	13	Stars, vapor, snow, the hills, rocks, the Fifth Month flowers, my amaze, my love,
LG1860	14	Aware of the buffalo, the peace-herds, the bull,strong-breasted and hairy,
LG1860	15	Aware of the mocking-bird of the wilds at day-breakdaybreak,
LG1860	16	Solitary, singing in the west, I strike up for a new world.
LG1860	17	Victory, union, faith, identity, time, the Soul, your-selfyourself, the present and future lands, the indisso-lubleindissoluble compacts, riches, mystery, eternal progress,the kosmos, and the modern reports.
LG1860	18	This then is life,
LG1860	19	Here is what has come to the surface after so many throes and convulsions.
LG1860	20	How curious! How real!
LG1860	21	Underfoot the divine soil—Overhead the sun.
LG1860	22	See, revolving,
LG1860	23	The globe—the ancestor-continents, away, grouped together,
LG1860	24	The present and future continents, north and south,with the isthmus between.
LG1860	25	See, vast, trackless spaces,
LG1860	26	As in a dream, they change, they swiftly fill,
LG1860	27	Countless masses debouch upon them,
LG1860	28	They are now covered with the foremost people, arts,institutions known.
LG1860	29	See projected, through time,
LG1860	30	For me, an audience interminable.
LG1860	31	With firm and regular step they wend—they never stop,
LG1860	32	Successions of men, Americanos, a hundred millions,
LG1860	33	One generation playing its part and passing on,
LG1860	34	And another generation playing its part and passing on in its turn,
LG1860	35	With faces turned sideways or backward toward me to listen,
LG1860	36	With eyes retrospective toward me.
LG1860	37	Americanos! Masters!
LG1860	38	Marches humanitarian! Foremost!
LG1860	39	Century marches! Libertad! Masses!
LG1860	40	For you a programme of chants.
LG1860	41	Chants of the prairies,
LG1860	42	Chants of the long-running Mississippi,
LG1860	43	Chants of Ohio, Indiana, Illinois, Wisconsin, Iowa,and Minnesota,
LG1860	44	Inland chants—chants of Kanzas,
LG1860	45	Chants away down to Mexico, and up north to Oregon—Kanadian chants,
LG1860	46	Chants of teeming and turbulent cities—chants of mechanics,
LG1860	47	Yankee chants—Pennsylvanian chants—chants of Kentucky and Tennessee,
LG1860	48	Chants of dim-lit mines—chants of mountain-tops,
LG1860	49	Chants of sailors—chants of the Eastern Sea and the Western Sea,
LG1860	50	Chants of the Mannahatta, the place of my dearest love, the place surrounded by hurried and sparkling currents,
LG1860	51	Health chants—joy chants—robust chants of young men,
LG1860	52	Chants inclusive—wide reverberating chants,
LG1860	53	Chants of the Many In One.
LG1860	54	In the Year 80 of The States,
LG1860	55	My tongue, every atom of my blood, formed from this soil, this air,
LG1860	56	Born here of parents born here,
LG1860	57	From parents the same, and their parents' parents the same,
LG1860	58	I, now thirty-six years old, in perfect health,begin,
LG1860	59	Hoping to cease not till death.
LG1860	60	Creeds and schools in abeyance,
LG1860	61	Retiring back a while, sufficed at what they are, but never forgotten,
LG1860	62	With accumulations, now coming forward in front,
LG1860	63	Arrived again, I harbor, for good or bad—I permit to speak,
LG1860	64	Nature, without check, with original energy.
LG1860	65	Take my leaves, America!
LG1860	66	Make welcome for them everywhere, for they are your own offspring;
LG1860	67	Surround them, East and West! for they would surround you,
LG1860	68	And you precedents! connect lovingly with them, for they connect lovingly with you.
LG1860	69	I conned old times,
LG1860	70	I sat studying at the feet of the great masters;
LG1860	71	Now, if eligible, O that the great masters might return and study me!
LG1860	72	In the name of These States, shall I scorn the antique?
LG1860	73	Why These are the children of the antique, to justify it.
LG1860	74	Dead poets, philosophs, priests,
LG1860	75	Martyrs, artists, inventors, governments long since,
LG1860	76	Language-shapers, on other shores,
LG1860	77	Nations once powerful, now reduced, withdrawn, or desolate,
LG1860	78	I dare not proceed till I respectfully credit what you have left, wafted hither,
LG1860	79	I have perused it—I own it is admirable,
LG1860	80	I think nothing can ever be greater—Nothing can ever deserve more than it deserves;
LG1860	81	I regard it all intently a long while,
LG1860	82	Then take my place for good with my own day and race here.
LG1860	83	Here lands female and male,
LG1860	84	Here the heirship and heiress-ship of the world—Here the flame of materials,
LG1860	85	Here Spirituality, the translatress, the openly-avowed,
LG1860	86	The ever-tending, the finale of visible forms,
LG1860	87	The satisfier, after due long-waiting, now advancing,
LG1860	88	Yes, here comes the mistress, the Soul.
LG1860	89	The SOUL!
LG1860	90	Forever and forever—Longer than soil is brown and solid—Longer than water ebbs and flows.
LG1860	91	I will make the poems of materials, for I think they are to be the most spiritual poems,
LG1860	92	And I will make the poems of my body and of mortality,
LG1860	93	For I think I shall then supply myself with the poems of my Soul and of immortality.
LG1860	94	I will make a song for These States, that no one State may under any circumstances be subjected to another State,
LG1860	95	And I will make a song that there shall be comity by day and by night between all The States, and between any two of them,
LG1860	96	And I will make a song of the organic bargains of These States—And a shrill song of curses on him who would dissever the Union;
LG1860	97	And I will make a song for the ears of the President,full of weapons with menacing points,
LG1860	98	And behind the weapons countless dissatisfied faces.
LG1860	99	I will acknowledge contemporary lands,
LG1860	100	I will trail the whole geography of the globe, and salute courteously every city large and small;
LG1860	101	And employments! I will put in my poems, that with you is heroism, upon land and sea—And I will report all heroism from an American point of view;
LG1860	102	And sexual organs and acts! do you concentrate in me—For I am determined to tell you with courageous clear voice, to prove you illustrious.
LG1860	103	I will sing the song of companionship,
LG1860	104	I will show what alone must compact These,
LG1860	105	I believe These are to found their own ideal of manly love, indicating it in me;
LG1860	106	I will therefore let flame from me the burning fires that were threatening to consume me,
LG1860	107	I will lift what has too long kept down those smoul-deringsmouldering fires,
LG1860	108	I will give them complete abandonment,
LG1860	109	I will write the evangel-poem of comrades and of love,
LG1860	110	(For who but I should understand love, with all its sorrow and joy?
LG1860	111	And who but I should be the poet of comrades?)
LG1860	112	I am the credulous man of qualities, ages, races,
LG1860	113	I advance from the people en-masse in their own spirit,
LG1860	114	Here is what sings unrestricted faith.
LG1860	115	Omnes! Omnes!
LG1860	116	Let others ignore what they may,
LG1860	117	I make the poem of evil also—I commemorate that part also,
LG1860	118	I am myself just as much evil as good—And I say there is in fact no evil,
LG1860	119	Or if there is, I say it is just as important to you, to the earth, or to me, as anything else.
LG1860	120	I too, following many, and followed by many, inau-gurateinaugurate a Religion—I too go to the wars,
LG1860	121	It may be I am destined to utter the loudest cries thereof, the conqueror's shouts,
LG1860	122	They may rise from me yet, and soar above every thing.
LG1860	123	Each is not for its own sake,
LG1860	124	I say the whole earth, and all the stars in the sky, are for Religion's sake.
LG1860	125	I say no man has ever been half devout enough,
LG1860	126	None has ever adored or worship'd half enough,
LG1860	127	None has begun to think how divine he himself is,and how certain the future is.
LG1860	128	I specifically announce that the real and perma-nentpermanent grandeur of These States must be their Religion,
LG1860	129	Otherwise there is no real and permanent grandeur.
LG1860	130	What are you doing, young man?
LG1860	131	Are you so earnest—so given up to literature,science, art, amours?
LG1860	132	These ostensible realities, materials, points?
LG1860	133	Your ambition or business, whatever it may be?
LG1860	134	It is well—Against such I say not a word—I am their poet also;
LG1860	135	But behold! such swiftly subside—burnt up for Religion's sake,
LG1860	136	For not all matter is fuel to heat, impalpable flame,the essential life of the earth,
LG1860	137	Any more than such are to Religion.
LG1860	138	What do you seek, so pensive and silent?
LG1860	139	What do you need, comrade?
LG1860	140	Mon cher! do you think it is love?
LG1860	141	Proceed, comrade,
LG1860	142	It is a painful thing to love a man or woman to excess—yet it satisfies—it is great,
LG1860	143	But there is something else very great—it makes the whole coincide,
LG1860	144	It, magnificent, beyond materials, with continuous hands, sweeps and provides for all.
LG1860	145	O I see the following poems are indeed to drop in the earth the germs of a greater Religion.
LG1860	146	My comrade!
LG1860	147	For you, to share with me, two greatnesses—And a third one, rising inclusive and more resplendent,
LG1860	148	The greatness of Love and Democracy—and the greatness of Religion.
LG1860	149	Melange mine!
LG1860	150	Mysterious ocean where the streams empty,
LG1860	151	Prophetic spirit of materials shifting and flickering around me,
LG1860	152	Wondrous interplay between the seen and unseen,
LG1860	153	Living beings, identities, now doubtless near us, in the air, that we know not of,
LG1860	154	Extasy everywhere touching and thrilling me,
LG1860	155	Contact daily and hourly that will not release me,
LG1860	156	These selecting—These, in hints, demanded of me.
LG1860	157	Not he, adhesive, kissing me so long with his daily kiss,
LG1860	158	Has winded and twisted around me that which holds me to him,
LG1860	159	Any more than I am held to the heavens, to the spiritual world,
LG1860	160	And to the identities of the Gods, my unknown lovers,
LG1860	161	After what they have done to me, suggesting such themes.
LG1860	162	O such themes! Equalities!
LG1860	163	O amazement of things! O divine average!
LG1860	164	O warblings under the sun—ushered, as now, or at noon, or setting!
LG1860	165	O strain, musical, flowing through ages—now reaching hither,
LG1860	166	I take to your reckless and composite chords—I add to them, and cheerfully pass them forward.
LG1860	167	As I have walked in Alabama my morning walk,
LG1860	168	I have seen where the she-bird, the mocking-bird, sat on her nest in the briers, hatching her brood.
LG1860	169	I have seen the he-bird also,
LG1860	170	I have paused to hear him, near at hand, inflating his throat, and joyfully singing.
LG1860	171	And while I paused, it came to me that what he really sang for was not there only,
LG1860	172	Nor for his mate nor himself only, nor all sent back by the echoes,
LG1860	173	But subtle, clandestine, away beyond,
LG1860	174	A charge transmitted, and gift occult, for those being born.
LG1860	175	Democracy!
LG1860	176	Near at hand to you a throat is now inflating itself and joyfully singing.
LG1860	177	Ma femme!
LG1860	178	For the brood beyond us and of us,
LG1860	179	For those who belong here, and those to come,
LG1860	180	I, exultant, to be ready for them, will now shake out carols stronger and haughtier than have ever yet been heard upon the earth.
LG1860	181	I will make the songs of passions, to give them their way,
LG1860	182	And your songs, offenders—for I scan you with kindred eyes, and carry you with me the same as any.
LG1860	183	I will make the true poem of riches,
LG1860	184	Namely, to earn for the body and the mind, what adheres, and goes forward, and is not dropt by death.
LG1860	185	I will effuse egotism, and show it underlying all—And I will be the bard of Personality;
LG1860	186	And I will show of male and female that either is but the equal of the other,
LG1860	187	And I will show that there is no imperfection in male or female, or in the earth, or in the present—and can be none in the future,
LG1860	188	And I will show that whatever happens to anybody, it may be turned to beautiful results—And I will show that nothing can happen more beautiful than death;
LG1860	189	And I will thread a thread through my poems that no one thing in the universe is inferior to another thing,
LG1860	190	And that all the things of the universe are perfect miracles, each as profound as any.
LG1860	191	I will not make poems with reference to parts,
LG1860	192	But I will make leaves, poems, poemets, songs, says,thoughts, with reference to ensemble;
LG1860	193	And I will not sing with reference to a day, but with reference to all days,
LG1860	194	And I will not make a poem, nor the least part of a poem, but has reference to the Soul,
LG1860	195	Because, having looked at the objects of the universe,I find there is no one, nor any particle of one,but has reference to the Soul.
LG1860	196	Was somebody asking to see the Soul?
LG1860	197	See! your own shape and countenance—persons,substances, beasts, the trees, the running rivers,the rocks and sands.
LG1860	198	All hold spiritual joys, and afterward loosen them,
LG1860	199	How can the real body ever die, and be buried?
LG1860	200	Of your real body, and any man's or woman's real body, item for item, it will elude the hands of the corpse-cleaners, and pass to fitting spheres,carrying what has accrued to it from the moment of birth to the moment of death.
LG1860	201	Not the types set up by the printer return their im-pressionimpression, the meaning, the main concern, any more than a man's substance and life, or a woman's substance and life, return in the body and the Soul, indifferently before death and after death.
LG1860	202	Behold! the body includes and is the meaning, the main concern—and includes and is the Soul;
LG1860	203	Whoever you are! how superb and how divine is your body, or any part of it.
LG1860	204	Whoever you are! to you endless announcements.
LG1860	205	Daughter of the lands, did you wait for your poet?
LG1860	206	Did you wait for one with a flowing mouth and indicative hand?
LG1860	207	Toward the male of The States, and toward the female of The States,
LG1860	208	Toward the President, the Congress, the diverse Gov-ernorsGovernors, the new Judiciary,
LG1860	209	Live words—words to the lands.
LG1860	210	O the lands!
LG1860	211	Lands scorning invaders! Interlinked, food-yielding lands!
LG1860	212	Land of coal and iron! Land of gold! Lands of cotton, sugar, rice!
LG1860	213	Odorous and sunny land! Floridian land!
LG1860	214	Land of the spinal river, the Mississippi! Land of the Alleghanies! Ohio's land!
LG1860	215	Land of wheat, beef, pork! Land of wool and hemp!Land of the potato, the apple, and the grape!
LG1860	216	Land of the pastoral plains, the grass-fields of the world! Land of those sweet-aired interminable plateaus! Land there of the herd, the garden,the healthy house of adobie! Land there of rapt thought, and of the realization of the stars!Land of simple, holy, untamed lives!
LG1860	217	Lands where the northwest Columbia winds, and where the southwest Colorado winds!
LG1860	218	Land of the Chesapeake! Land of the Delaware!
LG1860	219	Land of Ontario, Erie, Huron, Michigan!
LG1860	220	Land of the Old Thirteen! Massachusetts land!Land of Vermont and Connecticut!
LG1860	221	Land of many oceans! Land of sierras and peaks!
LG1860	222	Land of boatmen and sailors! Fishermen's land!
LG1860	223	Inextricable lands! the clutched together! the passionate lovers!
LG1860	224	The side by side! the elder and younger brothers!the bony-limbed!
LG1860	225	The great women's land! the feminine! the ex-periencedexperienced sisters and the inexperienced sisters!
LG1860	226	Far breath'd land! Arctic braced! Mexican breezed!the diverse! the compact!
LG1860	227	The Pennsylvanian! the Virginian! the double Carolinian!
LG1860	228	O all and each well-loved by me! my intrepid nations!O I cannot be discharged from you!
LG1860	229	O Death! O for all that, I am yet of you, unseen,this hour, with irrepressible love,
LG1860	230	Walking New England, a friend, a traveller,
LG1860	231	Splashing my bare feet in the edge of the summer ripples, on Paumanok's sands,
LG1860	232	Crossing the prairies—dwelling again in Chicago—dwelling in many towns,
LG1860	233	Observing shows, births, improvements, structures,arts,
LG1860	234	Listening to the orators and the oratresses in public halls,
LG1860	235	Of and through The States, as during life—each man and woman my neighbor,
LG1860	236	The Louisianian, the Georgian, as near to me, and I as near to him and her,
LG1860	237	The Mississippian and Arkansian—the woman and man of Utah, Dakotah, Nebraska, yet with me —and I yet with any of them,
LG1860	238	Yet upon the plains west of the spinal river—yet in my house of adobie,
LG1860	239	Yet returning eastward—yet in the Sea-Side State,or in Maryland,
LG1860	240	Yet a child of the North—yet Kanadian, cheerily braving the winter—the snow and ice welcome to me,
LG1860	241	Yet a true son either of Maine, or of the Granite State, or of the Narragansett Bay State, or of the Empire State,
LG1860	242	Yet sailing to other shores to annex the same—yet welcoming every new brother,
LG1860	243	Hereby applying these leaves to the new ones, from the hour they unite with the old ones,
LG1860	244	Coming among the new ones myself, to be their companion—coming personally to you now,
LG1860	245	Enjoining you to acts, characters, spectacles, with me.
LG1860	246	With me, with firm holding—yet haste, haste on.
LG1860	247	For your life, adhere to me,
LG1860	248	Of all the men of the earth, I only can unloose you and toughen you,
LG1860	249	I may have to be persuaded many times before I consent to give myself to you—but what of that?
LG1860	250	Must not Nature be persuaded many times?
LG1860	251	No dainty dolce affettuoso I;
LG1860	252	Bearded, sunburnt, gray-necked, forbidding, I have arrived,
LG1860	253	To be wrestled with as I pass, for the solid prizes of the universe,
LG1860	254	For such I afford whoever can persevere to win them.
LG1860	255	On my way a moment I pause,
LG1860	256	Here for you! And here for America!
LG1860	257	Still the Present I raise aloft—Still the Future of The States I harbinge, glad and sublime,
LG1860	258	And for the Past I pronounce what the air holds of the red aborigines.
LG1860	259	The red aborigines!
LG1860	260	Leaving natural breaths, sounds of rain and winds,calls as of birds and animals in the woods,syllabled to us for names,
LG1860	261	Okonee, Koosa, Ottawa, Monongahela, Sauk, Natchez,Chattahoochee, Kaqueta, Oronoco.
LG1860	262	Wabash, Miami, Saginaw, Chippewa, Oshkosh, Walla-WallaWalla Walla,
LG1860	263	Leaving such to The States, they melt, they depart,charging the water and the land with names.
LG1860	264	O expanding and swift! O henceforth,
LG1860	265	Elements, breeds, adjustments, turbulent, quick, and audacious,
LG1860	266	A world primal again—Vistas of glory, incessant and branching,
LG1860	267	A new race, dominating previous ones, and grander far,
LG1860	268	New politics—New literatures and religions—New inventions and arts.
LG1860	269	These! These, my voice announcing—I will sleep no more, but arise;
LG1860	270	You oceans that have been calm within me! how I feel you, fathomless, stirring, preparing unprecedented waves and storms.
LG1860	271	See! steamers steaming through my poems!
LG1860	272	See, in my poems immigrants continually coming and landing;
LG1860	273	See, in arriere, the wigwam, the trail, the hunter's hut, the flat-boat, the maize-leaf, the claim, the rude fence, and the backwoods village;
LG1860	274	See, on the one side the Western Sea, and on the other side the Eastern Sea, how they advance and retreat upon my poems, as upon their own shores;
LG1860	275	See, pastures and forests in my poems—See, animals,wild and tame—See, beyond the Kanzas, count-lesscountless herds of buffalo, feeding on short curly grass;
LG1860	276	See, in my poems, old and new cities, solid, vast,inland, with paved streets, with iron and stone edifices, and ceaseless vehicles, and commerce;
LG1860	277	See the populace, millions upon millions, handsome,tall, muscular, both sexes, clothed in easy and dignified clothes—teaching, commanding, mar-ryingmarrying, generating, equally electing and elective;
LG1860	278	See, the many-cylinder'd steam printing-press—See,the electric telegraph—See, the strong and quick locomotive, as it departs, panting, blowing the steam-whistle;
LG1860	279	See, ploughmen, ploughing farms—See, miners,digging mines—See, the numberless factories;
LG1860	280	See, mechanics, busy at their benches, with tools—See from among them, superior judges, philo-sophsphilosophs, Presidents, emerge, dressed in working dresses;
LG1860	281	See, lounging through the shops and fields of The States, me, well-beloved, close-held by day and night,
LG1860	282	Hear the loud echo of my songs there! Read the hints come at last.
LG1860	283	O my comrade!
LG1860	284	O you and me at last—and us two only;
LG1860	285	O power, liberty, eternity at last!
LG1860	286	O to be relieved of distinctions! to make as much of vices as virtues!
LG1860	287	O to level occupations and the sexes! O to bring all to common ground! O adhesiveness!
LG1860	288	O the pensive aching to be together—you know not why, and I know not why.
LG1860	289	O a word to clear one's path ahead endlessly!
LG1860	290	O something extatic and undemonstrable! O music wild!
LG1860	291	O now I triumph—and you shall also;
LG1860	292	O hand in hand—O wholesome pleasure—O one more desirer and lover,
LG1860	293	O haste, firm holding—haste, haste on, with me.
LG1860	294	I CELEBRATE myself,
LG1860	295	And what I assume you shall assume,
LG1860	296	For every atom belonging to me, as good belongs to you.
LG1860	297	I loafe and invite my Soul,
LG1860	298	I lean and loafe at my ease, observing a spear of summer grass.
LG1860	299	Houses and rooms are full of perfumes—the shelves are crowded with perfumes,
LG1860	300	I breathe the fragrance myself, and know it and like it,
LG1860	301	The distillation would intoxicate me also, but I shall not let it.
LG1860	302	The atmosphere is not a perfume—it has no taste of the distillation, it is odorless,
LG1860	303	It is for my mouth forever—I am in love with it,
LG1860	304	I will go to the bank by the wood, and become undisguised and naked,
LG1860	305	I am mad for it to be in contact with me.
LG1860	306	The smoke of my own breath,
LG1860	307	Echoes, ripples, buzzed whispers, love-root, silk-thread, crotch and vine,
LG1860	308	My respiration and inspiration, the beating of my heart, the passing of blood and air through my lungs,
LG1860	309	The sniff of green leaves and dry leaves, and of the shore, and dark-colored sea-rocks, and of hay in the barn,
LG1860	310	The sound of the belched words of my voice, words loosed to the eddies of the wind,
LG1860	311	A few light kisses, a few embraces, a reaching around of arms,
LG1860	312	The play of shine and shade on the trees as the supple boughs wag,
LG1860	313	The delight alone, or in the rush of the streets, or along the fields and hill-sides,
LG1860	314	The feeling of health, the full-noon trill, the song of me rising from bed and meeting the sun.
LG1860	315	Have you reckoned a thousand acres much? Have you reckoned the earth much?
LG1860	316	Have you practised so long to learn to read?
LG1860	317	Have you felt so proud to get at the meaning of poems?
LG1860	318	Stop this day and night with me, and you shall pos-sesspossess the origin of all poems,
LG1860	319	You shall possess the good of the earth and sun—there are millions of suns left,
LG1860	320	You shall no longer take things at second or third hand, nor look through the eyes of the dead,nor feed on the spectres in books.
LG1860	321	You shall not look through my eyes either, nor take things from me,
LG1860	322	You shall listen to all sides, and filter them from yourself.
LG1860	323	I have heard what the talkers were talking, the talk of the beginning and the end,
LG1860	324	But I do not talk of the beginning or the end.
LG1860	325	There was never any more inception than there is now,
LG1860	326	Nor any more youth or age than there is now,
LG1860	327	And will never be any more perfection than there is now,
LG1860	328	Nor any more heaven or hell than there is now.
LG1860	329	Urge, and urge, and urge,
LG1860	330	Always the procreant urge of the world.
LG1860	331	Out of the dimness opposite equals advance—always substance and increase, always sex,
LG1860	332	Always a knit of identity—always distinction—always a breed of life.
LG1860	333	To elaborate is no avail—learned and unlearned feel that it is so.
LG1860	334	Sure as the most certain sure, plumb in the uprights,well entretied, braced in the beams,
LG1860	335	Stout as a horse, affectionate, haughty, electrical,
LG1860	336	I and this mystery here we stand.
LG1860	337	Clear and sweet is my Soul, and clear and sweet is all that is not my Soul.
LG1860	338	Lack one lacks both, and the unseen is proved by the seen,
LG1860	339	Till that becomes unseen, and receives proof in its turn.
LG1860	340	Showing the best, and dividing it from the worst, age vexes age,
LG1860	341	Knowing the perfect fitness and equanimity of things,while they discuss I am silent, and go bathe and admire myself.
LG1860	342	Welcome is every organ and attribute of me, and of any man hearty and clean,
LG1860	343	Not an inch, nor a particle of an inch, is vile, and none shall be less familiar than the rest.
LG1860	344	I am satisfied—I see, dance, laugh, sing;
LG1860	345	As the hugging and loving Bed-fellow sleeps at my side through the night, and withdraws at the peep of the day,
LG1860	346	And leaves for me baskets covered with white towels,swelling the house with their plenty,
LG1860	347	Shall I postpone my acceptation and realization, and scream at my eyes,
LG1860	348	That they turn from gazing after and down the road,
LG1860	349	And forthwith cipher and show me to a cent,
LG1860	350	Exactly the contents of one, and exactly the contents of two, and which is ahead?
LG1860	351	Trippers and askers surround me,
LG1860	352	People I meet—the effect upon me of my early life,or the ward and city I live in, or the nation,
LG1860	353	The latest news, discoveries, inventions, societies,authors old and new,
LG1860	354	My dinner, dress, associates, looks, work, compliments,dues,
LG1860	355	The real or fancied indifference of some man or woman I love,
LG1860	356	The sickness of one of my folks, or of myself, or ill-doing, or loss or lack of money, or depressions or exaltations,
LG1860	357	These come to me days and nights, and go from me again,
LG1860	358	But they are not the Me myself.
LG1860	359	Apart from the pulling and hauling stands what I am,
LG1860	360	Stands amused, complacent, compassionating, idle,unitary,
LG1860	361	Looks down, is erect, or bends an arm on an impalpable certain rest,
LG1860	362	Looking with side-curved head, curious what will come next,
LG1860	363	Both in and out of the game, and watching and wondering at it.
LG1860	364	Backward I see in my own days where I sweated through fog with linguists and contenders,
LG1860	365	I have no mockings or arguments—I witness and wait.
LG1860	366	I believe in you, my Soul—the other I am must not abase itself to you,
LG1860	367	And you must not be abased to the other.
LG1860	368	Loafe with me on the grass—loose the stop from your throat,
LG1860	369	Not words, not music or rhyme I want—not custom or lecture, not even the best,
LG1860	370	Only the lull I like, the hum of your valved voice.
LG1860	371	I mind how once we lay, such a transparent summer morning,
LG1860	372	How you settled your head athwart my hips, and gently turned over upon me,
LG1860	373	And parted the shirt from my bosom-bone, and plunged your tongue to my bare-stript heart,
LG1860	374	And reached till you felt my beard, and reached till you held my feet.
LG1860	375	Swiftly arose and spread around me the peace and joy and knowledge that pass all the art and argument of the earth,
LG1860	376	And I know that the hand of God is the promise of my own,
LG1860	377	And I know that the spirit of God is the brother of my own,
LG1860	378	And that all the men ever born are also my brothers,and the women my sisters and lovers,
LG1860	379	And that a kelson of the creation is love,
LG1860	380	And limitless are leaves, stiff or drooping in the fields,
LG1860	381	And brown ants in the little wells beneath them,
LG1860	382	And mossy scabs of the worm-fence, and heaped stones, elder, mullen, and pokeweed.
LG1860	383	A child said, What is the grass? fetching it to me with full hands;
LG1860	384	How could I answer the child? I do not know what it is, any more than he.
LG1860	385	I guess it must be the flag of my disposition, out of hopeful green stuff woven.
LG1860	386	Or I guess it is the handkerchief of the Lord,
LG1860	387	A scented gift and remembrancer, designedly dropped,
LG1860	388	Bearing the owner's name someway in the corners,that we may see and remark, and say Whose?
LG1860	389	Or I guess the grass is itself a child, the produced babe of the vegetation.
LG1860	390	Or I guess it is a uniform hieroglyphic,
LG1860	391	And it means, Sprouting alike in broad zones and narrow zones,
LG1860	392	Growing among black folks as among white,
LG1860	393	Kanuck, Tuckahoe, Congressman, Cuff, I give them the same, I receive them the same.
LG1860	394	And now it seems to me the beautiful uncut hair of graves.
LG1860	395	Tenderly will I use you, curling grass,
LG1860	396	It may be you transpire from the breasts of young men,
LG1860	397	It may be if I had known them I would have loved them,
LG1860	398	It may be you are from old people, and from women,and from offspring taken soon out of their mothers' laps,
LG1860	399	And here you are the mothers' laps.
LG1860	400	This grass is very dark to be from the white heads of old mothers,
LG1860	401	Darker than the colorless beards of old men,
LG1860	402	Dark to come from under the faint red roofs of mouths.
LG1860	403	O I perceive after all so many uttering tongues!
LG1860	404	And I perceive they do not come from the roofs of mouths for nothing.
LG1860	405	I wish I could translate the hints about the dead young men and women,
LG1860	406	And the hints about old men and mothers, and the offspring taken soon out of their laps.
LG1860	407	What do you think has become of the young and old men?
LG1860	408	And what do you think has become of the women and children?
LG1860	409	They are alive and well somewhere,
LG1860	410	The smallest sprout shows there is really no death,
LG1860	411	And if ever there was, it led forward life, and does not wait at the end to arrest it,
LG1860	412	And ceased the moment life appeared.
LG1860	413	All goes onward and outward—nothing collapses,
LG1860	414	And to die is different from what any one supposed,and luckier.
LG1860	415	Has any one supposed it lucky to be born?
LG1860	416	I hasten to inform him or her, it is just as lucky to die, and I know it.
LG1860	417	I pass death with the dying, and birth with the new-washed babe, and am not contained between my hat and boots,
LG1860	418	And peruse manifold objects, no two alike, and every one good,
LG1860	419	The earth good, and the stars good, and their adjuncts all good.
LG1860	420	I am not an earth, nor an adjunct of an earth,
LG1860	421	I am the mate and companion of people, all just as immortal and fathomless as myself;
LG1860	422	They do not know how immortal, but I know.
LG1860	423	Every kind for itself and its own—for me mine, male and female,
LG1860	424	For me those that have been boys, and that love women,
LG1860	425	For me the man that is proud, and feels how it stings to be slighted,
LG1860	426	For me the sweetheart and the old maid—for me mothers, and the mothers of mothers,
LG1860	427	For me lips that have smiled, eyes that have shed tears,
LG1860	428	For me children, and the begetters of children.
LG1860	429	Who need be afraid of the merge?
LG1860	430	Undrape! you are not guilty to me, nor stale, nor discarded,
LG1860	431	I see through the broadcloth and gingham, whether or no,
LG1860	432	And am around, tenacious, acquisitive, tireless, and can never be shaken away.
LG1860	433	The little one sleeps in its cradle,
LG1860	434	I lift the gauze and look a long time, and silently brush away flies with my hand.
LG1860	435	The youngster and the red-faced girl turn aside up the bushy hill,
LG1860	436	I peeringly view them from the top.
LG1860	437	The suicide sprawls on the bloody floor of the bedroom;
LG1860	438	It is so—I witnessed the corpse—there the pistol had fallen.
LG1860	439	The blab of the pave, the tires of carts, sluff of boot-soles, talk of the promenaders,
LG1860	440	The heavy omnibus, the driver with his interrogating thumb, the clank of the shod horses on the granite floor,
LG1860	441	The snow-sleighs, the clinking, shouted jokes, pelts of snow-balls,
LG1860	442	The hurrahs for popular favorites, the fury of roused mobs,
LG1860	443	The flap of the curtained litter, a sick man inside,borne to the hospital,
LG1860	444	The meeting of enemies, the sudden oath, the blows and fall,
LG1860	445	The excited crowd, the policeman with his star,quickly working his passage to the centre of the crowd,
LG1860	446	The impassive stones that receive and return so many echoes,
LG1860	447	The Souls moving along—(are they invisible, while the least of the stones is visible?)
LG1860	448	What groans of over-fed or half-starved who fall sun-strucksunstruck, or in fits,
LG1860	449	What exclamations of women taken suddenly, who hurry home and give birth to babes,
LG1860	450	What living and buried speech is always vibrating here—what howls restrained by decorum,
LG1860	451	Arrests of criminals, slights, adulterous offers made,acceptances, rejections with convex lips,
LG1860	452	I mind them or the show or resonance of them—I come and I depart.
LG1860	453	The big doors of the country-barn stand open and ready,
LG1860	454	The dried grass of the harvest-time loads the slow-drawn wagon,
LG1860	455	The clear light plays on the brown gray and green intertinged,
LG1860	456	The armfuls are packed to the sagging mow.
LG1860	457	I am there—I help—I came stretched atop of the load,
LG1860	458	I felt its soft jolts—one leg reclined on the other;
LG1860	459	I jump from the cross-beams and seize the clover and timothy,
LG1860	460	And roll head over heels, and tangle my hair full of wisps.
LG1860	461	Alone, far in the wilds and mountains, I hunt,
LG1860	462	Wandering, amazed at my own lightness and glee,
LG1860	463	In the late afternoon choosing a safe spot to pass the night,
LG1860	464	Kindling a fire and broiling the fresh-killed game,
LG1860	465	Soundly falling asleep on the gathered leaves, with my dog and gun by my side.
LG1860	466	The Yankee clipper is under her three sky-sails—she cuts the sparkle and scud,
LG1860	467	My eyes settle the land—I bend at her prow, or shout joyously from the deck.
LG1860	468	The boatmen and clam-diggers arose early and stopped for me,
LG1860	469	I tucked my trowser-ends in my boots, and went and had a good time;
LG1860	470	You should have been with us that day round the chowder-kettle.
LG1860	471	I saw the marriage of the trapper in the open air in the far-west—the bride was a red girl,
LG1860	472	Her father and his friends sat near, cross-legged and dumbly smoking—they had moccasons to their feet, and large thick blankets hanging from their shoulders;
LG1860	473	On a bank lounged the trapper—he was dressed mostly in skins—his luxuriant beard and curls protected his neck,
LG1860	474	One hand rested on his rifle—the other hand held firmly the wrist of the red girl,
LG1860	475	She had long eyelashes—her head was bare—her coarse straight locks descended upon her volup-tuousvoluptuous limbs and reached to her feet.
LG1860	476	The runaway slave came to my house and stopped outside,
LG1860	477	I heard his motions crackling the twigs of the wood-pilewoodpile,
LG1860	478	Through the swung half-door of the kitchen I saw him limpsy and weak,
LG1860	479	And went where he sat on a log, and led him in and assured him,
LG1860	480	And brought water, and filled a tub for his sweated body and bruised feet,
LG1860	481	And gave him a room that entered from my own, and gave him some coarse clean clothes,
LG1860	482	And remember perfectly well his revolving eyes and his awkwardness,
LG1860	483	And remember putting plasters on the galls of his neck and ankles;
LG1860	484	He staid with me a week before he was recuperated and passed north,
LG1860	485	I had him sit next me at table—my fire-lock leaned in the corner.
LG1860	486	Twenty-eight young men bathe by the shore,
LG1860	487	Twenty-eight young men, and all so friendly;
LG1860	488	Twenty-eight years of womanly life, and all so lonesome.
LG1860	489	She owns the fine house by the rise of the bank,
LG1860	490	She hides, handsome and richly drest, aft the blinds of the window.
LG1860	491	Which of the young men does she like the best?
LG1860	492	Ah, the homeliest of them is beautiful to her.
LG1860	493	Where are you off to, lady? for I see you,
LG1860	494	You splash in the water there, yet stay stock still in your room.
LG1860	495	Dancing and laughing along the beach came the twenty-ninth bather,
LG1860	496	The rest did not see her, but she saw them and loved them.
LG1860	497	The beards of the young men glistened with wet, it ran from their long hair,
LG1860	498	Little streams passed all over their bodies.
LG1860	499	An unseen hand also passed over their bodies,
LG1860	500	It descended tremblingly from their temples and ribs.
LG1860	501	The young men float on their backs—their white bellies bulge to the sun—they do not ask who seizes fast to them,
LG1860	502	They do not know who puffs and declines with pendant and bending arch,
LG1860	503	They do not think whom they souse with spray.
LG1860	504	The butcher-boy puts off his killing-clothes, or sharp-enssharpens his knife at the stall in the market,
LG1860	505	I loiter, enjoying his repartee and his shuffle and break-down.
LG1860	506	Blacksmiths with grimed and hairy chests environ the anvil,
LG1860	507	Each has his main-sledge—they are all out—there is a great heat in the fire.
LG1860	508	From the cinder-strewed threshold I follow their movements,
LG1860	509	The lithe sheer of their waists plays even with their massive arms,
LG1860	510	Overhand the hammers roll—overhand so slow—overhand so sure,
LG1860	511	They do not hasten—each man hits in his place.
LG1860	512	The negro holds firmly the reins of his four horses —the blocks swags underneath on its tied-over chain,
LG1860	513	The negro that drives the huge dray of the stone-yard —steady and tall he stands, poised on one leg on the string-piece,
LG1860	514	His blue shirt exposes his ample neck and breast, and loosens over his hip-band,
LG1860	515	His glance is calm and commanding—he tosses the slouch of his hat away from his forehead,
LG1860	516	The sun falls on his crispy hair and moustache—falls on the black of his polished and perfect limbs.
LG1860	517	I behold the picturesque giant and love him—and I do not stop there,
LG1860	518	I go with the team also.
LG1860	519	In me the caresser of life wherever moving—back-wardbackward as well as forward slueing,
LG1860	520	To niches aside and junior bending.
LG1860	521	Oxen that rattle the yoke or halt in the shade! what is that you express in your eyes?
LG1860	522	It seems to me more than all the print I have read in my life.
LG1860	523	My tread scares the wood-drake and wood-duck, on my distant and day-long ramble,
LG1860	524	They rise together—they slowly circle around.
LG1860	525	I believe in those winged purposes,
LG1860	526	And acknowledge red, yellow, white, playing within me,
LG1860	527	And consider green and violet, and the tufted crown,intentional,
LG1860	528	And do not call the tortoise unworthy because she is not something else,
LG1860	529	And the mocking-bird in the swamp never studied the gamut, yet trills pretty well to me,
LG1860	530	And the look of the bay mare shames silliness out of me.
LG1860	531	The wild gander leads his flock through the cool night,
LG1860	532	Ya-honk! he says, and sounds it down to me like an invitation;
LG1860	533	The pert may suppose it meaningless, but I listen close,
LG1860	534	I find its purpose and place up there toward the wintry sky.
LG1860	535	The sharp-hoofed moose of the north, the cat on the house-sill, the chickadee, the prairie-dog,
LG1860	536	The litter of the grunting sow as they tug at her teats,
LG1860	537	The brood of the turkey-hen, and she with her half-spread wings,
LG1860	538	I see in them and myself the same old law.
LG1860	539	The press of my foot to the earth springs a hundred affections,
LG1860	540	They scorn the best I can do to relate them.
LG1860	541	I am enamoured of growing outdoors.
LG1860	542	Of men that live among cattle, or taste of the ocean or woods,
LG1860	543	Of the builders and steerers of ships, and the wielders of axes and mauls, and the drivers of horses,
LG1860	544	I can eat and sleep with them week in and week out.
LG1860	545	What is commonest, cheapest, nearest, easiest, is Me,
LG1860	546	Me going in for my chances, spending for vast returns,
LG1860	547	Adorning myself to bestow myself on the first that will take me,
LG1860	548	Not asking the sky to come down to my good will,
LG1860	549	Scattering it freely forever.
LG1860	550	The pure contralto sings in the organ loft,
LG1860	551	The carpenter dresses his plank—the tongue of his foreplane whistles its wild ascending lisp,
LG1860	552	The married and unmarried children ride home to their Thanksgiving dinner,
LG1860	553	The pilot seizes the king-pin—he heaves down with a strong arm,
LG1860	554	The mate stands braced in the whale-boat—lance and harpoon are ready,
LG1860	555	The duck-shooter walks by silent and cautious stretches,
LG1860	556	The deacons are ordained with crossed hands at the altar,
LG1860	557	The spinning-girl retreats and advances to the hum of the big wheel,
LG1860	558	The farmer stops by the bars, as he walks on a First Day loafe, and looks at the oats and rye,
LG1860	559	The lunatic is carried at last to the asylum, a con-firmedconfirmed case,
LG1860	560	He will never sleep any more as he did in the cot in his mother's bedroom;
LG1860	561	The jour printer with gray head and gaunt jaws works at his case,
LG1860	562	He turns his quid of tobacco, while his eyes blurr with the manuscript;
LG1860	563	The malformed limbs are tied to the anatomist's table,
LG1860	564	What is removed drops horribly in a pail;
LG1860	565	The quadroon girl is sold at the stand—the drunkard nods by the bar-room stove,
LG1860	566	The machinist rolls up his sleeves—the policeman travels his beat—the gate-keeper marks who pass,
LG1860	567	The young fellow drives the express-wagon—I love him, though I do not know him,
LG1860	568	The half-breed straps on his light boots to compete in the race,
LG1860	569	The western turkey-shooting draws old and young—some lean on their rifles, some sit on logs,
LG1860	570	Out from the crowd steps the marksman, takes his position, levels his piece;
LG1860	571	The groups of newly-come emigrants cover the wharf or levee,
LG1860	572	As the woolly-pates hoe in the sugar-field, the over-seeroverseer views them from his saddle,
LG1860	573	The bugle calls in the ball-room, the gentlemen run for their partners, the dancers bow to each other,
LG1860	574	The youth lies awake in the cedar-roofed garret, and harks to the musical rain,
LG1860	575	The Wolverine sets traps on the creek that helps fill the Huron,
LG1860	576	The reformer ascends the platform, he spouts with his mouth and nose,
LG1860	577	The company returns from its excursion, the darkey brings up the rear and bears the well-riddled target,
LG1860	578	The squaw, wrapt in her yellow-hemmed cloth, is offering moccasons and bead-bags for sale,
LG1860	579	The connoisseur peers along the exhibition-gallery with half-shut eyes bent side-ways,
LG1860	580	As the deck-hands make fast the steamboat, the plank is thrown for the shore-going passengers,
LG1860	581	The young sister holds out the skein, while the elder sister winds it off in a ball, and stops now and then for the knots,
LG1860	582	The one-year wife is recovering and happy, having a week ago borne her first child,
LG1860	583	The clean-haired Yankee girl works with her sewing-machine, or in the factory or mill,
LG1860	584	The nine months' gone is in the parturition chamber,her faintness and pains are advancing,
LG1860	585	The paving-man leans on his two-handed rammer —the reporter's lead flies swiftly over the note-booknotebook—the sign-painter is lettering with red and gold,
LG1860	586	The canal-boy trots on the tow-path—the bookkeeper counts at his desk—the shoemaker waxes his thread,
LG1860	587	The conductor beats time for the band, and all the performers follow him,
LG1860	588	The child is baptized—the convert is making his first professions,
LG1860	589	The regatta is spread on the bay—how the white sails sparkle!
LG1860	590	The drover, watching his drove, sings out to them that would stray,
LG1860	591	The pedler sweats with his pack on his back, the purchaser higgling about the odd cent,
LG1860	592	The camera and plate are prepared, the lady must sit for her daguerreotype,
LG1860	593	The bride unrumples her white dress, the minute-hand of the clock moves slowly,
LG1860	594	The opium-eater reclines with rigid head and just-opened lips,
LG1860	595	The prostitute draggles her shawl, her bonnet bobs on her tipsy and pimpled neck,
LG1860	596	The crowd laugh at her blackguard oaths, the men jeer and wink to each other,
LG1860	597	(Miserable!-I do not laugh at your oaths, nor jeer you;)
LG1860	598	The President, holding a cabinet council, is sur-roundedsurrounded by the Great Secretaries,
LG1860	599	On the piazza walk five friendly matrons with twined arms,
LG1860	600	The crew of the fish-smack pack repeated layers of halibut in the hold,
LG1860	601	The Missourian crosses the plains, toting his wares and his cattle,
LG1860	602	As the fare-collector goes through the train, he gives notice by the jingling of loose change,
LG1860	603	The floor-men are laying the floor—the tinners are tinning the roof—the masons are calling for mortar,
LG1860	604	In single file, each shouldering his hod, pass onward the laborers,
LG1860	605	Seasons pursuing each other, the indescribable crowd is gathered—it is the Fourth of Seventh Month —What salutes of cannon and small arms!
LG1860	606	Seasons pursuing each other, the plougher ploughs,the mower mows, and the winter-grain falls in the ground,
LG1860	607	Off on the lakes the pike-fisher watches and waits by the hole in the frozen surface,
LG1860	608	The stumps stand thick round the clearing, the squatter strikes deep with his axe,
LG1860	609	Flatboatmen make fast, towards dusk, near the cotton-woodcottonwood or pekan-trees,
LG1860	610	Coon-seekers go through the regions of the Red river,or through those drained by the Tennessee, or through those of the Arkansaw,
LG1860	611	Torches shine in the dark that hangs on the Chatta-hoocheChattahooche or Altamahaw,
LG1860	612	Patriarchs sit at supper with sons and grandsons and great-grandsons around them,
LG1860	613	In walls of adobie, in canvas tents, rest hunters and trappers after their day's sport,
LG1860	614	The city sleeps and the country sleeps,
LG1860	615	The living sleep for their time, the dead sleep for their time,
LG1860	616	The old husband sleeps by his wife, and the young husband sleeps by his wife;
LG1860	617	And these one and all tend inward to me, and I tend outward to them,
LG1860	618	And such as it is to be of these, more or less, I am.
LG1860	619	I am of old and young, of the foolish as much as the wise,
LG1860	620	Regardless of others, ever regardful of others,
LG1860	621	Maternal as well as paternal, a child as well as a man,
LG1860	622	Stuffed with the stuff that is coarse, and stuffed with the stuff that is fine,
LG1860	623	One of the great nation, the nation of many nations,the smallest the same, and the largest the same,
LG1860	624	A southerner soon as a northerner, a planter non-chalantnonchalant and hospitable,
LG1860	625	A Yankee, bound my own way, ready for trade, my joints the limberest joints on earth and the sternest joints on earth,
LG1860	626	A Kentuckian, walking the vale of the Elkhorn in my deer-skin leggings,
LG1860	627	A boatman over lakes or bays, or along coasts—a Hoosier, Badger, Buckeye,
LG1860	628	A Louisianian or Georgian—a Poke-easy from sand-hills and pines,
LG1860	629	At home on Kanadian snow-shoes, or up in the bush,or with fishermen off Newfoundland,
LG1860	630	At home in the fleet of ice-boats, sailing with the rest,and tacking,
LG1860	631	At home on the hills of Vermont, or in the woods of Maine, or the Texan ranch,
LG1860	632	Comrade of Californians—comrade of free north-westerners, and loving their big proportions,
LG1860	633	Comrade of raftsmen and coalmen—comrade of all who shake hands and welcome to drink and meat,
LG1860	634	A learner with the simplest, a teacher of the thought-fullestthoughtfullest,
LG1860	635	A novice beginning, yet experient of myriads of seasons,
LG1860	636	Of every hue, trade, rank, caste and religion,
LG1860	637	Not merely of the New World, but of Africa, Europe,Asia—a wandering savage,
LG1860	638	A farmer, mechanic, artist, gentleman, sailor, lover,quaker,
LG1860	639	A prisoner, fancy-man, rowdy, lawyer, physician,priest.
LG1860	640	I resist anything better than my own diversity,
LG1860	641	And breathe the air, and leave plenty after me,
LG1860	642	And am not stuck up, and am in my place.
LG1860	643	The moth and the fish-eggs are in their place,
LG1860	644	The suns I see, and the suns I cannot see, are in their place,
LG1860	645	The palpable is in its place, and the impalpable is in its place.
LG1860	646	These are the thoughts of all men in all ages and lands—they are not original with me,
LG1860	647	If they are not yours as much as mine, they are nothing, or next to nothing,
LG1860	648	If they do not enclose everything, they are next to nothing,
LG1860	649	If they are not the riddle and the untying of the riddle, they are nothing,
LG1860	650	If they are not just as close as they are distant, they are nothing.
LG1860	651	This is the grass that grows wherever the land is and the water is,
LG1860	652	This is the common air that bathes the globe.
LG1860	653	This is the breath for America, because it is my breath,
LG1860	654	This is for laws, songs, behavior,
LG1860	655	This is the tasteless water of Souls—this is the true sustenance.
LG1860	656	This is for the illiterate, and for the judges of the Supreme Court, and for the Federal capitol and the State capitols,
LG1860	657	And for the admirable communes of literats, com-poserscomposers, singers, lecturers, engineers, and savans,
LG1860	658	And for the endless races of work-people, farmers,and seamen.
LG1860	659	This is the trilling of thousands of clear cornets,screaming of octave flutes, striking of triangles.
LG1860	660	I play not here marches for victors only—I play great marches for conquered and slain persons.
LG1860	661	Have you heard that it was good to gain the day?
LG1860	662	I also say it is good to fall—battles are lost in the same spirit in which they are won.
LG1860	663	I beat triumphal drums for the dead,
LG1860	664	I blow through my embouchures my loudest and gayest music to them.
LG1860	665	Vivas to those who have failed!
LG1860	666	And to those whose war-vessels sank in the sea!
LG1860	667	And those themselves who sank in the sea!
LG1860	668	And to all generals that lost engagements! and all overcome heroes!
LG1860	669	And the numberless unknown heroes, equal to the greatest heroes known.
LG1860	670	This is the meal pleasantly set—this is the meat and drink for natural hunger,
LG1860	671	It is for the wicked just the same as the righteous—I make appointments with all,
LG1860	672	I will not have a single person slighted or left away,
LG1860	673	The kept-woman, sponger, thief, are hereby invited,
LG1860	674	The heavy-lipped slave is invited—the venerealee is invited,
LG1860	675	There shall be no difference between them and the rest.
LG1860	676	This is the press of a bashful hand—this is the float and odor of hair,
LG1860	677	This is the touch of my lips to yours—this is the murmur of yearning,
LG1860	678	This is the far-off depth and height reflecting my own face,
LG1860	679	This is the thoughtful merge of myself, and the outlet again.
LG1860	680	Do you guess I have some intricate purpose?
LG1860	681	Well, I have—for the Fourth Month showers have,and the mica on the side of a rock has.
LG1860	682	Do you take it I would astonish?
LG1860	683	Does the daylight astonish? Does the early redstart,twittering through the woods?
LG1860	684	Do I astonish more than they?
LG1860	685	This hour I tell things in confidence,
LG1860	686	I might not tell everybody, but I will tell you.
LG1860	687	Who goes there! hankering, gross, mystical, nude?
LG1860	688	How is it I extract strength from the beef I eat?
LG1860	689	What is a man anyhow? What am I? What are you?
LG1860	690	All I mark as my own, you shall offset it with your own,
LG1860	691	Else it were time lost listening to me.
LG1860	692	I do not snivel that snivel the world over,
LG1860	693	That months are vacuums, and the ground but wallow and filth,
LG1860	694	That life is a suck and a sell, and nothing remains at the end but threadbare crape, and tears.
LG1860	695	Whimpering and truckling fold with powders for invalids—conformity goes to the fourth-removed,
LG1860	696	I cock my hat as I please, indoors or out.
LG1860	697	Why should I pray? Why should I venerate and be ceremonious?
LG1860	698	Having pried through the strata, analyzed to a hair,counsell'd with doctors, and calculated close,
LG1860	699	I find no sweeter fat than sticks to my own bones.
LG1860	700	In all people I see myself—none more, and not one a barleycorn less,
LG1860	701	And the good or bad I say of myself I say of them.
LG1860	702	And I know I am solid and sound,
LG1860	703	To me the converging objects of the universe per-petuallyperpetually flow,
LG1860	704	All are written to me, and I must get what the writing means.
LG1860	705	I know I am deathless,I know this orbit of mine cannot be swept by a carpenter's compass,
LG1860	706	I know I shall not pass like a child's carlacue cut with a burnt stick at night.
LG1860	707	I know I am august,
LG1860	708	I do not trouble my spirit to vindicate itself or be understood,
LG1860	709	I see that the elementary laws never apologize,
LG1860	710	I reckon I behave no prouder than the level I plant my house by, after all.
LG1860	711	I exist as I am—that is enough,
LG1860	712	If no other in the world be aware, I sit content,
LG1860	713	And if each and all be aware, I sit content.
LG1860	714	One world is aware, and by far the largest to me, and that is myself,
LG1860	715	And whether I come to my own to-day, or in ten thousand or ten million years,
LG1860	716	I can cheerfully take it now, or with equal cheerful-nesscheerfulness I can wait
LG1860	717	My foothold is tenoned and mortised in granite,
LG1860	718	I laugh at what you call dissolution,
LG1860	719	And I know the amplitude of time.
LG1860	720	I am the poet of the body,
LG1860	721	And I am the poet of the Soul.
LG1860	722	The pleasures of heaven are with me, and the pains of hell are with me,
LG1860	723	The first I graft and increase upon myself—the latter I translate into a new tongue.
LG1860	724	I am the poet of the woman the same as the man,
LG1860	725	And I say it is as great to be a woman as to be a man,
LG1860	726	And I say there is nothing greater than the mother of men.
LG1860	727	I chant the chant of dilation or pride,
LG1860	728	We have had ducking and deprecating about enough,
LG1860	729	I show that size is only development.
LG1860	730	Have you outstript the rest? Are you the President?
LG1860	731	It is a trifle—they will more than arrive there every one, and still pass on.
LG1860	732	I am He that walks with the tender and growing Night,
LG1860	733	I call to the earth and sea, half-held by the Night.
LG1860	734	Press close, bare-bosomed Night! Press close, mag-neticmagnetic, nourishing Night!
LG1860	735	Night of south winds! Night of the large few stars!
LG1860	736	Still, nodding night! Mad, naked, summer night.
LG1860	737	Smile, O voluptuous, cool-breathed Earth!
LG1860	738	Earth of the slumbering and liquid trees!
LG1860	739	Earth of departed sunset! Earth of the mountains,misty-topt!
LG1860	740	Earth of the vitreous pour of the full moon, just tinged with blue!
LG1860	741	Earth of shine and dark, mottling the tide of the river!
LG1860	742	Earth of the limpid gray of clouds, brighter and clearer for my sake!
LG1860	743	Far-swooping elbowed Earth! Rich, apple-blossomed Earth!
LG1860	744	Smile, for YOUR LOVER comes!
LG1860	745	Prodigal, you have given me love! Therefore I to you give love!
LG1860	746	O unspeakable passionate love!
LG1860	747	Thruster holding me tight, and that I hold tight!
LG1860	748	We hurt each other as the bridegroom and the bride hurt each other.
LG1860	749	You Sea! I resign myself to you also—I guess what you mean,
LG1860	750	I behold from the beach your crooked inviting fingers,
LG1860	751	I believe you refuse to go back without feeling of me;
LG1860	752	We must have a turn together—I undress—hurry me out of sight of the land,
LG1860	753	Cushion me soft, rock me in billowy drowse,
LG1860	754	Dash me with amorous wet—I can repay you.
LG1860	755	Sea of stretched ground-swells!
LG1860	756	Sea breathing broad and convulsive breaths!
LG1860	757	Sea of the brine of life! Sea of unshovelled and always-ready graves!
LG1860	758	Howler and scooper of storms! Capricious and dainty Sea!
LG1860	759	I am integral with you—I too am of one phase, and of all phases.
LG1860	760	Partaker of influx and efflux—extoller of hate and conciliation,
LG1860	761	Extoller of amies, and those that sleep in each others'arms.
LG1860	762	I am he attesting sympathy,
LG1860	763	Shall I make my list of things in the house, and skip the house that supports them?
LG1860	764	I am the poet of common sense, and of the demon-strabledemonstrable, and of immortality,
LG1860	765	And am not the poet of goodness only—I do not decline to be the poet of wickedness also.
LG1860	766	Washes and razors for foofoos—for me freckles and a bristling beard.
LG1860	767	What blurt is this about virtue and about vice?
LG1860	768	Evil propels me, and reform of evil propels me—I stand indifferent,
LG1860	769	My gait is no fault-finder's or rejecter's gait,
LG1860	770	I moisten the roots of all that has grown.
LG1860	771	Did you fear some scrofula out of the unflagging pregnancy?
LG1860	772	Did you guess the celestial laws are yet to be worked over and rectified?
LG1860	773	I step up to say that what we do is right, and what we affirm is right—and some is only the ore of right,
LG1860	774	Witnesses of us—one side a balance, and the antip-odalantipodal side a balance,
LG1860	775	Soft doctrine as steady help as stable doctrine,
LG1860	776	Thoughts and deeds of the present, our rouse and early start.
LG1860	777	This minute that comes to me over the past decillions,
LG1860	778	There is no better than it and now.
LG1860	779	What behaved well in the past, or behaves well to-day, is not such a wonder,
LG1860	780	The wonder is, always and always, how can there be a mean man or an infidel.
LG1860	781	Endless unfolding of words of ages!
LG1860	782	And mine a word of the modern—a word en-masse.
LG1860	783	A word of the faith that never balks,
LG1860	784	One time as good as another time—here or hence-forwardhenceforward, it is all the same to me.
LG1860	785	A word of reality—materialism first and last im-buingimbuing.
LG1860	786	Hurrah for positive Science! long live exact demon-strationdemonstration!
LG1860	787	Fetch stonecrop, mixt with cedar and branches of lilac,
LG1860	788	This is the lexicographer—this the chemist—this made a grammar of the old cartouches,
LG1860	789	These mariners put the ship through dangerous un-knownunknown seas,
LG1860	790	This is the geologist—this works with the scalpel—and this is a mathematician.
LG1860	791	Gentlemen! I receive you, and attach and clasp hands with you,
LG1860	792	The facts are useful and real—they are not my dwelling—I enter by them to an area of the dwelling.
LG1860	793	I am less the reminder of property or qualities, and more the reminder of life,
LG1860	794	And go on the square for my own sake and for others'sakes,
LG1860	795	And make short account of neuters and geldings, and favor men and women fully equipped,
LG1860	796	And beat the gong of revolt, and stop with fugitives,and them that plot and conspire.
LG1860	797	Walt Whitman, an American, one of the roughs, a kosmos,
LG1860	798	Disorderly, fleshy, sensual, eating, drinking, breeding,
LG1860	799	No sentimentalist—no stander above men and wo-menwomen, or apart from them,
LG1860	800	No more modest than immodest.
LG1860	801	Unscrew the locks from the doors!
LG1860	802	Unscrew the doors themselves from their jambs!
LG1860	803	Whoever degrades another degrades me,
LG1860	804	And whatever is done or said returns at last to me,
LG1860	805	And whatever I do or say, I also return.
LG1860	806	Through me the afflatus surging and surging—through me the current and index.
LG1860	807	I speak the pass-word primeval—I give the sign of democracy,
LG1860	808	By God! I will accept nothing which all cannot have their counterpart of on the same terms.
LG1860	809	Through me many long dumb voices,
LG1860	810	Voices of the interminable generations of slaves,
LG1860	811	Voices of prostitutes, and of deformed persons,
LG1860	812	Voices of the diseased and despairing, and of thieves and dwarfs,
LG1860	813	Voices of cycles of preparation and accretion,
LG1860	814	And of the threads that connect the stars—and of wombs, and of the fatherstuff,
LG1860	815	And of the rights of them the others are down upon,
LG1860	816	Of the trivial, flat, foolish, despised,
LG1860	817	Fog in the air, beetles rolling balls of dung.
LG1860	818	Through me forbidden voices,
LG1860	819	Voices of sexes and lusts—voices veiled, and I remove the veil,
LG1860	820	Voices indecent, by me clarified and transfigured.
LG1860	821	I do not press my finger across my mouth,
LG1860	822	I keep as delicate around the bowels as around the head and heart,
LG1860	823	Copulation is no more rank to me than death is.
LG1860	824	I believe in the flesh and the appetites,
LG1860	825	Seeing, hearing, feeling, are miracles, and each part and tag of me is a miracle.
LG1860	826	Divine am I inside and out, and I make holy what-everwhatever I touch or am touched from,
LG1860	827	The scent of these arm-pits, aroma finer than prayer,
LG1860	828	This head more than churches, bibles, and all the creeds.
LG1860	829	If I worship any particular thing, it shall be some of the spread of my own body.
LG1860	830	Translucent mould of me, it shall be you!
LG1860	831	Shaded ledges and rests, it shall be you!
LG1860	832	Firm masculine colter, it shall be you.
LG1860	833	Whatever goes to the tilth of me, it shall be you!
LG1860	834	You my rich blood! Your milky stream, pale strip-pingsstrippings of my life.
LG1860	835	Breast that presses against other breasts, it shall be you!
LG1860	836	My brain, it shall be your occult convolutions.
LG1860	837	Root of washed sweet-flag! Timorous pond-snipe!Nest of guarded duplicate eggs! it shall be you!
LG1860	838	Mixed tussled hay of head, beard, brawn, it shall be you!
LG1860	839	Trickling sap of maple! Fibre of manly wheat! it shall be you!
LG1860	840	Sun so generous, it shall be you!
LG1860	841	Vapors lighting and shading my face, it shall be you!
LG1860	842	You sweaty brooks and dews, it shall be you!
LG1860	843	Winds whose soft-tickling genitals rub against me, it shall be you!
LG1860	844	Broad, muscular fields! Branches of live oak! Lov-ingLoving lounger in my winding paths! it shall be you!
LG1860	845	Hands I have taken—face I have kissed—mortal I have ever touched! it shall be you.
LG1860	846	I dote on myself—there is that lot of me, and all so luscious,
LG1860	847	Each moment, and whatever happens, thrills me with joy.
LG1860	848	O I am so wonderful!
LG1860	849	I cannot tell how my ankles bend, nor whence the cause of my faintest wish,
LG1860	850	Nor the cause of the friendship I emit, nor the cause of the friendship I take again.
LG1860	851	That I walk up my stoop, I pause to consider if it really be,
LG1860	852	That I eat and drink is spectacle enough for the great authors and schools,
LG1860	853	A morning-glory at my window satisfies me more than the metaphysics of books.
LG1860	854	To behold the day-break!
LG1860	855	The little light fades the immense and diaphanous shadows,
LG1860	856	The air tastes good to my palate.
LG1860	857	Hefts of the moving world, at innocent gambols,silently rising, freshly exuding,
LG1860	858	Scooting obliquely high and low.
LG1860	859	Something I cannot see puts upward libidinous prongs,
LG1860	860	Seas of bright juice suffuse heaven.
LG1860	861	The earth by the sky staid with—the daily close of their junction,
LG1860	862	The heaved challenge from the east that moment over my head,
LG1860	863	The mocking taunt, See then whether you shall be master!
LG1860	864	Dazzling and tremendous, how quick the sun-rise would kill me,
LG1860	865	If I could not now and always send sun-rise out of me.
LG1860	866	We also ascend, dazzling and tremendous as the sun,
LG1860	867	We found our own, O my Soul, in the calm and cool of the day-break.
LG1860	868	My voice goes after what my eyes cannot reach,
LG1860	869	With the twirl of my tongue I encompass worlds, and volumes of worlds.
LG1860	870	Speech is the twin of my vision—it is unequal to measure itself;
LG1860	871	It provokes me forever,
LG1860	872	It says sarcastically, Walt, you understand enough— why don't you let it out then?
LG1860	873	Come now, I will not be tantalized—you conceive too much of articulation.
LG1860	874	Do you not know how the buds beneath are folded?
LG1860	875	Waiting in gloom, protected by frost,
LG1860	876	The dirt receding before my prophetical screams,
LG1860	877	I underlying causes, to balance them at last,
LG1860	878	My knowledge my live parts—it keeping tally with the meaning of things,
LG1860	879	Happiness—which, whoever hears me, let him or her set out in search of this day.
LG1860	880	My final merit I refuse you—I refuse putting from me the best I am.
LG1860	881	Encompass worlds, but never try to encompass me,
LG1860	882	I crowd your sleekest talk by simply looking toward you.
LG1860	883	Writing and talk do not prove me,
LG1860	884	I carry the plenum of proof, and everything else, in my face,
LG1860	885	With the hush of my lips I confound the topmost skeptic.
LG1860	886	I think I will do nothing for a long time but listen,
LG1860	887	To accrue what I hear into myself—to let sounds contribute toward me.
LG1860	888	I hear bravuras of birds, bustle of growing wheat,gossip of flames, clack of sticks cooking my meals.
LG1860	889	I hear the sound I love, the sound of the human voice,
LG1860	890	I hear all sounds running together, combined, fused or following,
LG1860	891	Sounds of the city and sounds out of the city—sounds of the day and night,
LG1860	892	Talkative young ones to those that like them—the recitative of fish-pedlers and fruit-pedlers—the loud laugh of work-people at their meals,
LG1860	893	The angry base of disjointed friendship—the faint tones of the sick,
LG1860	894	The judge with hands tight to the desk, his shaky lips pronouncing a death-sentence,
LG1860	895	The heave'e'yo of stevedores unlading ships by the wharves—the refrain of the anchor-lifters,
LG1860	896	The ring of alarm-bells—the cry of fire—the whirr of swift-streaking engines and hose-carts, with premonitory tinkles, and colored lights,
LG1860	897	The steam-whistle—the solid roll of the train of approaching cars,
LG1860	898	The slow-march played at night at the head of the association, marching two and two,
LG1860	899	(They go to guard some corpse—the flag-tops are draped with black muslin.)
LG1860	900	I hear the violoncello, or man's heart's complaint;
LG1860	901	I hear the keyed cornet—it glides quickly in through my ears,
LG1860	902	It shakes mad-sweet pangs through my belly and breast.
LG1860	903	I hear the chorus—it is a grand-opera,
LG1860	904	Ah, this indeed is music! This suits me.
LG1860	905	A tenor large and fresh as the creation fills me,
LG1860	906	The orbic flex of his mouth is pouring and filling me full.
LG1860	907	I hear the trained soprano—she convulses me like the climax of my love-grip,
LG1860	908	The orchestra wrenches such ardors from me, I did not know I possessed them,
LG1860	909	It throbs me to gulps of the farthest down horror,
LG1860	910	It sails me—I dab with bare feet—they are licked by the indolent waves,
LG1860	911	I am exposed, cut by bitter and poisoned hail,
LG1860	912	Steeped amid honeyed morphine, my windpipe throt-tledthrottled in fakes of death,
LG1860	913	At length let up again to feel the puzzle of puzzles,
LG1860	914	And that we call BEING.
LG1860	915	To be in any form—what is that?
LG1860	916	(Round and round we go, all of us, and ever come back thither,)
LG1860	917	If nothing lay more developed, the quahaug in its callous shell were enough.
LG1860	918	Mine is no callous shell,
LG1860	919	I have instant conductors all over me, whether I pass or stop,
LG1860	920	They seize every object, and lead it harmlessly through me.
LG1860	921	I merely stir, press, feel with my fingers, and am happy,
LG1860	922	To touch my person to some one else's is about as much as I can stand.
LG1860	923	Is this then a touch? quivering me to a new identity,
LG1860	924	Flames and ether making a rush for my veins,
LG1860	925	Treacherous tip of me reaching and crowding to help them,
LG1860	926	My flesh and blood playing out lightning to strike what is hardly different from myself,
LG1860	927	On all sides prurient provokers stiffening my limbs,
LG1860	928	Straining the udder of my heart for its withheld drip,
LG1860	929	Behaving licentious toward me, taking no denial,
LG1860	930	Depriving me of my best, as for a purpose,
LG1860	931	Unbuttoning my clothes, holding me by the bare waist,
LG1860	932	Deluding my confusion with the calm of the sun-light and pasture-fields,
LG1860	933	Immodestly sliding the fellow-senses away,
LG1860	934	They bribed to swap off with touch, and go and graze at the edges of me,
LG1860	935	No consideration, no regard for my draining strength or my anger,
LG1860	936	Fetching the rest of the herd around to enjoy them a while,
LG1860	937	Then all uniting to stand on a headland and worry me.
LG1860	938	The sentries desert every other part of me,
LG1860	939	They have left me helpless to a red marauder,
LG1860	940	They all come to the headland, to witness and assist against me.
LG1860	941	I am given up by traitors,
LG1860	942	I talk wildly—I have lost my wits—I and nobody else am the greatest traitor,
LG1860	943	I went myself first to the headland—my own hands carried me there.
LG1860	944	You villain touch! what are you doing? My breath is tight in its throat,
LG1860	945	Unclench your floodgates! you are too much for me.
LG1860	946	Blind, loving, wrestling touch! sheathed, hooded,sharp-toothed touch!
LG1860	947	Did it make you ache so, leaving me?
LG1860	948	Parting, tracked by arriving—perpetual payment of perpetual loan,
LG1860	949	Rich showering rain, and recompense richer after-wardafterward.
LG1860	950	Sprouts take and accumulate—stand by the curb prolific and vital,
LG1860	951	Landscapes, projected, masculine, full-sized, and golden.
LG1860	952	All truths wait in all things,
LG1860	953	They neither hasten their own delivery, nor resist it,
LG1860	954	They do not need the obstetric forceps of the surgeon,
LG1860	955	The insignificant is as big to me as any,
LG1860	956	What is less or more than a touch?
LG1860	957	Logic and sermons never convince,
LG1860	958	The damp of the night drives deeper into my Soul.
LG1860	959	Only what proves itself to every man and woman is so,
LG1860	960	Only what nobody denies is so.
LG1860	961	A minute and a drop of me settle my brain,
LG1860	962	I believe the soggy clods shall become lovers and lamps,
LG1860	963	And a compend of compends is the meat of a man or woman,
LG1860	964	And a summit and flower there is the feeling they have for each other,
LG1860	965	And they are to branch boundlessly out of that lesson until it becomes omnific,
LG1860	966	And until every one shall delight us, and we them.
LG1860	967	I believe a leaf of grass is no less than the journey-workjourneywork of the stars,
LG1860	968	And the pismire is equally perfect, and a grain of sand, and the egg of the wren,
LG1860	969	And the tree-toad is a chef-d'œuvre for the highest,
LG1860	970	And the running blackberry would adorn the parlors of heaven,
LG1860	971	And the narrowest hinge in my hand puts to scorn all machinery,
LG1860	972	And the cow crunching with depressed head surpasses any statue,
LG1860	973	And a mouse is miracle enough to stagger sextillions of infidels,
LG1860	974	And I could come every afternoon of my life to look at the farmer's girl boiling her iron tea-kettle and baking short-cake.
LG1860	975	I find I incorporate gneiss, coal, long-threaded moss,fruits, grains, esculent roots,
LG1860	976	And am stuccoed with quadrupeds and birds all over,
LG1860	977	And have distanced what is behind me for good reasons,
LG1860	978	And call anything close again, when I desire it.
LG1860	979	In vain the speeding or shyness,
LG1860	980	In vain the plutonic rocks send their old heat against my approach,
LG1860	981	In vain the mastodon retreats beneath its own pow-deredpowdered bones,
LG1860	982	In vain objects stand leagues off, and assume manifold shapes,
LG1860	983	In vain the ocean settling in hollows, and the great monsters lying low,
LG1860	984	In vain the buzzard houses herself with the sky,
LG1860	985	In vain the snake slides through the creepers and logs,
LG1860	986	In vain the elk takes to the inner passes of the woods,
LG1860	987	In vain the razor-billed auk sails far north to Labrador,
LG1860	988	I follow quickly, I ascend to the nest in the fissure of the cliff.
LG1860	989	I think I could turn and live with animals, they are so placid and self-contained,
LG1860	990	I stand and look at them sometimes an hour at a stretch.
LG1860	991	They do not sweat and whine about their condition,
LG1860	992	They do not lie awake in the dark and weep for their sins,
LG1860	993	They do not make me sick discussing their duty to God,
LG1860	994	No one is dissatisfied—not one is demented with the mania of owning things,
LG1860	995	Not one kneels to another, nor to his kind that lived thousands of years ago,
LG1860	996	Not one is respectable or industrious over the whole earth.
LG1860	997	So they show their relations to me, and I accept them,
LG1860	998	They bring me tokens of myself—they evince them plainly in their possession.
LG1860	999	I do not know where they get those tokens,
LG1860	1000	I may have passed that way untold times ago, and negligently dropt them,
LG1860	1001	Myself moving forward then and now forever,
LG1860	1002	Gathering and showing more always and with velocity,
LG1860	1003	Infinite and omnigenous, and the like of these among them,

LG1860	1191	The second First Day morning they were brought out in squads and massacred—it was beautiful early summer,

LG1860	1201	At eleven o'clock began the burning of the bodies:
LG1860	1202	That is the tale of the murder of the four hundred and twelve young men.
LG1860	1203	Did you read in the sea-books of the old-fashioned frigate-fight?
LG1860	1204	Did you learn who won by the light of the moon and stars?
LG1860	1205	Our foe was no skulk in his ship, I tell you,
LG1860	1206	His was the English pluck—and there is no tougher or truer, and never was, and never will be;
LG1860	1207	Along the lowered eve he came, horribly raking us.
LG1860	1208	We closed with him—the yards entangled—the cannon touched,
LG1860	1209	My captain lashed fast with his own hands.
LG1860	1210	We had received some eighteen-pound shots under the water,
LG1860	1211	On our lower-gun-deck two large pieces had burst at the first fire, killing all around, and blowing up overhead.
LG1860	1212	Ten o'clock at night, and the full moon shining, and the leaks on the gain, and five feet of water reported,

LG1860	1301	They are wafted with the odor of his body or breath —they fly out of the glance of his eyes.
LG1860	1302	Flaunt of the sunshine, I need not your bask,—lie over!
LG1860	1303	You light surfaces only—I force surfaces and depths also.
LG1860	1304	Earth! you seem to look for something at my hands,
LG1860	1305	Say, old Top-knot! what do you want?
LG1860	1306	Man or woman! I might tell how I like you, but cannot,
LG1860	1307	And might tell what it is in me, and what it is in you, but cannot,
LG1860	1308	And might tell that pining I have—that pulse of my nights and days.
LG1860	1309	Behold! I do not give lectures or a little charity,
LG1860	1310	What I give, I give out of myself.
LG1860	1311	You there, impotent, loose in the knees,
LG1860	1312	Open your scarfed chops till I blow grit within you,

LG1860	1314	I am not to be denied—I compel—I have stores plenty and to spare,
LG1860	1315	And anything I have I bestow.
LG1860	1316	I do not ask who you are—that is not important to me,
LG1860	1317	You can do nothing, and be nothing, but what I will infold you.

LG1860	1645	It flings my likeness, after the rest, and true as any,on the shadowed wilds,
LG1860	1646	It coaxes me to the vapor and the dusk.
LG1860	1647	I depart as air—I shake my white locks at the run-away sun,
LG1860	1648	I effuse my flesh in eddies, and drift it in lacy jags.
LG1860	1649	I bequeathe myself to the dirt, to grow from the grass I love,
LG1860	1650	If you want me again, look for me under your boot-soles.
LG1860	1651	You will hardly know who I am, or what I mean,
LG1860	1652	But I shall be good health to you nevertheless,
LG1860	1653	And filter and fibre your blood.
LG1860	1654	Failing to fetch me at first, keep encouraged,
LG1860	1655	Missing me one place, search another,
LG1860	1656	I stop somewhere waiting for you.
LG1860	1657	O mater! O fils!
LG1860	1658	O brood continental!
LG1860	1659	O flowers of the prairies!
LG1860	1660	O space boundless! O hum of mighty products!
LG1860	1661	O you teeming cities! O so invincible, turbulent,proud!
LG1860	1662	O race of the future! O women!
LG1860	1663	O fathers! O you men of passion and the storm!
LG1860	1664	O native power only! O beauty!
LG1860	1665	O yourself! O God! O divine average!
LG1860	1666	O you bearded roughs! O bards! O all those slum-berersslumberers!
LG1860	1667	O arouse! the dawn-bird's throat sounds shrill! Do you not hear the cock crowing?
