LG1860	1669	O I heard, and yet hear, angry thunder;—O you sailors! O ships! make quick preparation!
LG1860	1670	O from his masterful sweep, the warning cry of the eagle!
LG1860	1671	(Give way there, all! It is useless! Give up your spoils;)
LG1860	1672	O sarcasms! Propositions! (O if the whole world should prove indeed a sham, a sell!)
LG1860	1673	O I believe there is nothing real but America and freedom!
LG1860	1674	O to sternly reject all except Democracy!
LG1860	1675	O imperator! O who dare confront you and me?
LG1860	1676	O to promulgate our own! O to build for that which builds for mankind!

LG1860	1678	O all, all inseparable—ages, ages, ages!
LG1860	1679	O a curse on him that would dissever this Union for any reason whatever!
LG1860	1680	O climates, labors! O good and evil! O death!
LG1860	1681	O you strong with iron and wood! O Personality!
LG1860	1682	O the village or place which has the greatest man or woman! even if it be only a few ragged huts;
LG1860	1683	O the city where women walk in public processions in the streets, the same as the men;
LG1860	1684	O a wan and terrible emblem, by me adopted!
LG1860	1685	O shapes arising! shapes of the future centuries!
LG1860	1686	O muscle and pluck forever for me!
LG1860	1687	O workmen and workwomen forever for me!
LG1860	1688	O farmers and sailors! O drivers of horses forever for me!
LG1860	1689	O I will make the new bardic list of trades and tools!
LG1860	1690	O you coarse and wilful! I love you!
LG1860	1691	O South! O longings for my dear home! O soft and sunny airs!

LG1860	1693	O equality! O organic compacts! I am come to be your born poet!
LG1860	1694	O whirl, contest, sounding and resounding! I am your poet, because I am part of you;
LG1860	1695	O days by-gone! Enthusiasts! Antecedents!
LG1860	1696	O vast preparations for These States! O years!
LG1860	1697	O what is now being sent forward thousands of years to come!
LG1860	1698	O mediums! O to teach! to convey the invisible faith!
LG1860	1699	To promulge real things! to journey through all The States!
LG1860	1700	O creation! O to-day! O laws! O unmitigated adoration!
LG1860	1701	O for mightier broods of orators, artists, and singers!
LG1860	1702	O for native songs! carpenter's, boatman's, plough-manploughman's songs! shoemaker's songs!

LG1860	1704	O what I, here, preparing, warble for!
LG1860	1705	O you hastening light! O the sun of the world will ascend, dazzling, and take his height—and you too will ascend;

LG1860	1719	O purged lumine! you threaten me more than I can stand!
LG1860	1720	O present! I return while yet I may to you!
LG1860	1721	O poets to come, I depend upon you!
LG1860	1722	A NATION announcing itself, (many in one,)
LG1860	1723	I myself make the only growth by which I can be appreciated,
LG1860	1724	I reject none, accept all, reproduce all in my own forms.
LG1860	1725	A breed whose testimony is behavior,
LG1860	1726	What we are WE ARE—nativity is answer enough to objections;
LG1860	1727	We wield ourselves as a weapon is wielded,
LG1860	1728	We are powerful and tremendous in ourselves,
LG1860	1729	We are executive in ourselves—We are sufficient in the variety of ourselves,
LG1860	1730	We are the most beautiful to ourselves, and in our-selvesourselves,
LG1860	1731	Nothing is sinful to us outside of ourselves,
LG1860	1732	Whatever appears, whatever does not appear, we are beautiful or sinful in ourselves only.
LG1860	1733	Have you thought there could be but a single Supreme?
LG1860	1734	There can be any number of Supremes—One does not countervail another, any more than one eye-sighteyesight countervails another, or one life counter-vailscountervails another.
LG1860	1735	All is eligible to all,
LG1860	1736	All is for individuals—All is for you,
LG1860	1737	No condition is prohibited, not God's or any,
LG1860	1738	If one is lost, you are inevitably lost.
LG1860	1739	All comes by the body—only health puts you rapport with the universe.
LG1860	1740	Produce great persons, the rest follows.
LG1860	1741	How dare a sick man, or an obedient man, write poems for These States?
LG1860	1742	Which is the theory or book that, for our purposes, is not diseased?
LG1860	1743	Piety and conformity to them that like!
LG1860	1744	Peace, obesity, allegiance, to them that like!
LG1860	1745	I am he who tauntingly compels men, women, nations, to leap from their seats and contend for their lives.
LG1860	1746	I am he who goes through the streets with a barbed tongue, questioning every one I meet—ques-tioningquestioning you up there now:
LG1860	1747	Who are you, that wanted only to be told what you knew before?
LG1860	1748	Who are you, that wanted only a book to join you in your nonsense?
LG1860	1749	Are you, or would you be, better than all that has ever been before?
LG1860	1750	If you would be better than all that has ever been before, come listen to me, and not otherwise.
LG1860	1751	Fear grace—Fear delicatesse,
LG1860	1752	Fear the mellow sweet, the sucking of honey-juice,
LG1860	1753	Beware the advancing mortal ripening of nature,
LG1860	1754	Beware what precedes the decay of the ruggedness of states and men.
LG1860	1755	Ages, precedents, poems, have long been accumu-latingaccumulating undirected materials,
LG1860	1756	America brings builders, and brings its own styles.
LG1860	1757	Mighty bards have done their work, and passed to other spheres,
LG1860	1758	One work forever remains, the work of surpassing all they have done.
LG1860	1759	America, curious toward foreign characters, stands by its own at all hazards,
LG1860	1760	Stands removed, spacious, composite, sound,
LG1860	1761	Sees itself promulger of men and women, initiates the true use of precedents,
LG1860	1762	Does not repel them or the past, or what they have produced under their forms, or amid other pol-iticspolitics, or amid the idea of castes, or the old religions,
LG1860	1763	Takes the lesson with calmness, perceives the corpse slowly borne from the eating and sleeping rooms of the house,
LG1860	1764	Perceives that it waits a little while in the door— that it was fittest for its days,
LG1860	1765	That its life has descended to the stalwart and well- shaped heir who approaches,
LG1860	1766	And that he shall be fittest for his days.
LG1860	1767	Any period, one nation must lead,
LG1860	1768	One land must be the promise and reliance of the future.
LG1860	1769	These States are the amplest poem,
LG1860	1770	Here is not merely a nation, but a teeming nation of nations,
LG1860	1771	Here the doings of men correspond with the broad-castbroadcast doings of the day and night,
LG1860	1772	Here is what moves in magnificent masses, carelessly faithful of particulars,
LG1860	1773	Here are the roughs, beards, friendliness, combative-nesscombativeness, the Soul loves,
LG1860	1774	Here the flowing trains—here the crowds, equality, diversity, the Soul loves.
LG1860	1775	Race of races, and bards to corroborate!
LG1860	1776	Of them, standing among them, one lifts to the light his west-bred face,
LG1860	1777	To him the hereditary countenance bequeathed, both mother's and father's,
LG1860	1778	His first parts substances, earth, water, animals, trees,
LG1860	1779	Built of the common stock, having room for far and near,
LG1860	1780	Used to dispense with other lands, incarnating this land,
LG1860	1781	Attracting it body and Soul to himself, hanging on its neck with incomparable love,
LG1860	1782	Plunging his semitic muscle into its merits and demerits,
LG1860	1783	Making its geography, cities, beginnings, events, glories, defections, diversities, vocal in him,
LG1860	1784	Making its rivers, lakes, bays, embouchure in him,
LG1860	1785	Mississippi with yearly freshets and changing chutes —Missouri, Columbia, Ohio, Niagara, Hudson, spending themselves lovingly in him,
LG1860	1786	If the Atlantic coast stretch, or the Pacific coast stretch, he stretching with them north or south,
LG1860	1787	Spanning between them east and west, and touching whatever is between them,
LG1860	1788	Growths growing from him to offset the growth of pine, cedar, hemlock, live-oak, locust, chest-nutchestnut, cypress, hickory, lime-tree, cotton-wood, tulip-tree, cactus, tamarind, orange, magnolia, persimmon,

LG1860	1790	He likening sides and peaks of mountains, forests coated with transparent ice, and icicles hanging from the boughs,
LG1860	1791	Off him pasturage sweet and natural as savanna, upland, prairie,
LG1860	1792	Through him flights, songs, screams, answering those of the wild-pigeon, coot, fish-hawk, qua-bird, mocking-bird, condor, night-heron, eagle;
LG1860	1793	His spirit surrounding his country's spirit, unclosed to good and evil,
LG1860	1794	Surrounding the essences of real things, old times and present times,
LG1860	1795	Surrounding just found shores, islands, tribes of red aborigines,
LG1860	1796	Weather-beaten vessels, landings, settlements, the rapid stature and muscle,
LG1860	1797	The haughty defiance of the Year 1—war, peace, the formation of the Constitution,
LG1860	1798	The separate States, the simple, elastic scheme, the immigrants,
LG1860	1799	The Union, always swarming with blatherers, and always calm and impregnable,
LG1860	1800	The unsurveyed interior, log-houses, clearings, wild animals, hunters, trappers;
LG1860	1801	Surrounding the multiform agriculture, mines, tem-peraturetemperature, the gestation of new States,
LG1860	1802	Congress convening every Twelfth Month, the mem-bersmembers duly coming up from the uttermost parts;
LG1860	1803	Surrounding the noble character of mechanics and farmers, especially the young men,
LG1860	1804	Responding their manners, speech, dress, friendships —the gait they have of persons who never knew how it felt to stand in the presence of superiors,
LG1860	1805	The freshness and candor of their physiognomy, the copiousness and decision of their phrenology,

LG1860	1815	For these and the like, their own voices! For these, space ahead!
LG1860	1816	Others take finish, but the Republic is ever con-structiveconstructive, and ever keeps vista;
LG1860	1817	Others adorn the past—but you, O, days of the present, I adorn you!
LG1860	1818	O days of the future, I believe in you!
LG1860	1819	O America, because you build for mankind, I build for you!
LG1860	1820	O well-beloved stone-cutters! I lead them who plan with decision and science,

LG1860	1822	Bravas to States whose semitic impulses send whole-somewholesome children to the next age!
LG1860	1823	But damn that which spends itself on flaunters and dalliers, with no thought of the stain, pains, dismay, feebleness, it is bequeathing.
LG1860	1824	By great bards only can series of peoples and States be fused into the compact organism of one nation.
LG1860	1825	To hold men together by paper and seal, or by com-pulsioncompulsion, is no account,
LG1860	1826	That only holds men together which is living prin-ciplesprinciples, as the hold of the limbs of the body, or the fibres of plants.
LG1860	1827	Of all races and eras, These States, with veins full of poetical stuff, most need poets, and are to have the greatest, and use them the greatest,
LG1860	1828	Their Presidents shall not be their common referee so much as their poets shall.
LG1860	1829	Of mankind, the poet is the equable man,
LG1860	1830	Not in him, but off from him, things are grotesque, eccentric, fail of their full returns,
LG1860	1831	Nothing out of its place is good, nothing in its place is bad,
LG1860	1832	He bestows on every object or quality its fit propor-tionsproportions, neither more nor less,
LG1860	1833	He is the arbiter of the diverse, he is the key,
LG1860	1834	He is the equalizer of his age and land,
LG1860	1835	He supplies what wants supplying—he checks what wants checking,
LG1860	1836	In peace, out of him speaks the spirit of peace, large, rich, thrifty, building populous towns, encour-agingencouraging agriculture, arts, commerce, lighting the study of man, the Soul, health, immortality, government,
LG1860	1837	In war, he is the best backer of the war—he fetches artillery as good as the engineer's—he can make every word he speaks draw blood;
LG1860	1838	The years straying toward infidelity, he withholds by his steady faith,
LG1860	1839	He is no arguer, he is judgment,
LG1860	1840	He judges not as the judge judges, but as the sun falling round a helpless thing;
LG1860	1841	As he sees the farthest he has the most faith,
LG1860	1842	His thoughts are the hymns of the praise of things,
LG1860	1843	In the dispute on God and eternity he is silent,
LG1860	1844	He sees eternity less like a play with a prologue and denouement,
LG1860	1845	He sees eternity in men and women—he does not see men and women as dreams or dots.
LG1860	1846	Of the idea of perfect and free individuals, the idea of These States, the bard walks in advance, leader of leaders,
LG1860	1847	The attitude of him cheers up slaves, and horrifies foreign despots.
LG1860	1848	Without extinction is Liberty! Without retrograde is Equality!
LG1860	1849	They live in the feelings of young men, and the best women,
LG1860	1850	Not for nothing have the indomitable heads of the earth been always ready to fall for Liberty!
LG1860	1851	Are YOU indeed for Liberty?
LG1860	1852	Are you a man who would assume a place to teach here, or lead here, or be a poet here?
LG1860	1853	The place is august—the terms obdurate.
LG1860	1854	Who would assume to teach here, may well prepare himself, body and mind,
LG1860	1855	He may well survey, ponder, arm, fortify, harden, make lithe, himself,
LG1860	1856	He shall surely be questioned beforehand by me with many and stern questions.
LG1860	1857	Who are you, indeed, who would talk or sing in America?
LG1860	1858	Have you studied out MY LAND, its idioms and men?
LG1860	1859	Have you learned the physiology, phrenology, poli-ticspolitics, geography, pride, freedom, friendship, of my land? its substratums and objects?
LG1860	1860	Have you considered the organic compact of the first day of the first year of the independence of The States, signed by the Commissioners, ratified by The States, and read by Washington at the head of the army?
LG1860	1861	Have you possessed yourself of the Federal Constitu-tionConstitution?
LG1860	1862	Do you acknowledge Liberty with audible and abso-luteabsolute acknowledgment, and set slavery at nought for life and death?
LG1860	1863	Do you see who have left described processes and poems behind them, and assumed new ones?
LG1860	1864	Are you faithful to things? Do you teach whatever the land and sea, the bodies of men, womanhood, amativeness, angers, excesses, crimes, teach?
LG1860	1865	Have you sped through customs, laws, popularities?
LG1860	1866	Can you hold your hand against all seductions, follies, whirls, fierce contentions? Are you very strong? Are you of the whole people?
LG1860	1867	Are you not of some coterie? some school or religion?
LG1860	1868	Are you done with reviews and criticisms of life? ani-matinganimating to life itself?
LG1860	1869	Have you vivified yourself from the maternity of These States?
LG1860	1870	Have you sucked the nipples of the breasts of the mother of many children?
LG1860	1871	Have you too the old, ever-fresh, forbearance and impartiality?
LG1860	1872	Do you hold the like love for those hardening to maturity? for the last-born? little and big? and for the errant?
LG1860	1873	What is this you bring my America?
LG1860	1874	Is it uniform with my country?
LG1860	1875	Is it not something that has been better told or done before?
LG1860	1876	Have you not imported this, or the spirit of it, in some ship?
LG1860	1877	Is it a mere tale? a rhyme? a prettiness?
LG1860	1878	Has it never dangled at the heels of the poets, poli-ticianspoliticians, literats, of enemies' lands?

LG1860	1880	Does it answer universal needs? Will it improve manners?
LG1860	1881	Can your performance face the open fields and the sea-side?

LG1860	1884	Does it meet modern discoveries, calibers, facts, face to face?

LG1860	1886	What does it mean to me? to American persons, progresses, cities? Chicago, Kanada, Arkansas? the planter, Yankee, Georgian, native, immi-grantimmigrant, sailors, squatters, old States, new States?

LG1860	1889	Does it see what befalls and has always befallen each temporizer, patcher, outsider, partialist, alarmist, infidel, who has ever asked anything of America?
LG1860	1890	What mocking and scornful negligence?
LG1860	1891	The track strewed with the dust of skeletons?
LG1860	1892	By the roadside others disdainfully tossed?
LG1860	1893	Rhymes and rhymers pass away—poems distilled from other poems pass away,
LG1860	1894	The swarms of reflectors and the polite pass, and leave ashes;
LG1860	1895	Admirers, importers, obedient persons, make the soil of literature;
LG1860	1896	America justifies itself, give it time—no disguise can deceive it, or conceal from it—it is impassive enough,
LG1860	1897	Only toward the likes of itself will it advance to meet them,
LG1860	1898	If its poets appear, it will advance to meet them— there is no fear of mistake,
LG1860	1899	The proof of a poet shall be sternly deferred, till his country absorbs him as affectionately as he has absorbed it.
LG1860	1900	He masters whose spirit masters—he tastes sweetest who results sweetest in the long run,
LG1860	1901	The blood of the brawn beloved of time is uncon-straintunconstraint,
LG1860	1902	In the need of poems, philosophy, politics, manners, engineering, an appropriate native grand-opera, shipcraft, any craft, he or she is greatest who contributes the greatest original practical ex-ampleexample.
LG1860	1903	Already a nonchalant breed, silently emerging, fills the houses and streets,
LG1860	1904	People's lips salute only doers, lovers, satisfiers, positive knowers;
LG1860	1905	There will shortly be no more priests—I say their work is done,
LG1860	1906	Death is without emergencies here, but life is per-petualperpetual emergencies here,

LG1860	1908	Friendship, self-esteem, justice, health, clear the way with irresistible power;
LG1860	1909	How dare you place anything before a man?
LG1860	1910	Fall behind me, States!
LG1860	1911	A man, before all—myself, typical, before all.
LG1860	1912	Give me the pay I have served for!
LG1860	1913	Give me to speak beautiful words! take all the rest;
LG1860	1914	I have loved the earth, sun, animals—I have despised riches,
LG1860	1915	I have given alms to every one that asked, stood up for the stupid and crazy, devoted my income and labor to others,
LG1860	1916	I have hated tyrants, argued not concerning God, had patience and indulgence toward the people, taken off my hat to nothing known or unknown,
LG1860	1917	I have gone freely with powerful uneducated persons, and with the young, and with the mothers of families,
LG1860	1918	I have read these leaves to myself in the open air— I have tried them by trees, stars, rivers,
LG1860	1919	I have dismissed whatever insulted my own Soul or defiled my body,
LG1860	1920	I have claimed nothing to myself which I have not carefully claimed for others on the same terms.
LG1860	1921	I have studied my land, its idioms and men,
LG1860	1922	I am willing to wait to be understood by the growth of the taste of myself,
LG1860	1923	I reject none, I permit all,
LG1860	1924	Whom I have staid with once I have found longing for me ever afterward.
LG1860	1925	I swear I begin to see the meaning of these things!
LG1860	1926	It is not the earth, it is not America, who is so great,
LG1860	1927	It is I who am great, or to be great—it is you, or any one,
LG1860	1928	It is to walk rapidly through civilizations, govern-mentsgovernments, theories, nature, poems, shows, to indi-vidualsindividuals.
LG1860	1929	Underneath all are individuals,
LG1860	1930	I swear nothing is good to me now that ignores individuals!
LG1860	1931	The American compact is altogether with individuals,
LG1860	1932	The only government is that which makes minute of individuals,
LG1860	1933	The whole theory of the universe is directed to one single individual—namely, to You.
LG1860	1934	Underneath all is nativity,
LG1860	1935	I swear I will stand by my own nativity—pious or impious, so be it;
LG1860	1936	I swear I am charmed with nothing except nativity,
LG1860	1937	Men, women, cities, nations, are only beautiful from nativity.
LG1860	1938	Underneath all is the need of the expression of love for men and women,
LG1860	1939	I swear I have had enough of mean and impotent modes of expressing love for men and women,
LG1860	1940	After this day I take my own modes of expressing love for men and women.
LG1860	1941	I swear I will have each quality of my race in myself,
LG1860	1942	Talk as you like, he only suits These States whose manners favor the audacity and sublime turbu-lenceturbulence of The States.
LG1860	1943	Underneath the lessons of things, spirits, nature, governments, ownerships, I swear I perceive other lessons,
LG1860	1944	Underneath all to me is myself—to you, yourself, (the same monotonous old song,)
LG1860	1945	If all had not kernels for you and me, what were it to you and me?
LG1860	1946	O I see now, flashing, that this America is only you and me,
LG1860	1947	Its power, weapons, testimony, are you and me,
LG1860	1948	Its roughs, beards, haughtiness, ruggedness, are you and me,
LG1860	1949	Its ample geography, the sierras, the prairies, Mis-sissippiMississippi, Huron, Colorado, Boston, Toronto, Raleigh, Nashville, Havana, are you and me,

LG1860	1954	Its endless gestations of new States are you and me,
LG1860	1955	Its inventions, science, schools, are you and me,
LG1860	1956	Its deserts, forests, clearings, log-houses, hunters, are you and me,
LG1860	1957	Natural and artificial are you and me,
LG1860	1958	Freedom, language, poems, employments, are you and me,
LG1860	1959	Failures, successes, births, deaths, are you and me,
LG1860	1960	Past, present, future, are only you and me.
LG1860	1961	I swear I dare not shirk any part of myself,
LG1860	1962	Not any part of America, good or bad,
LG1860	1963	Not my body—not friendship, hospitality, pro-creationprocreation,
LG1860	1964	Not my Soul, nor the last explanation of prudence,
LG1860	1965	Not the similitude that interlocks me with all iden-titiesidentities that exist, or ever have existed,
LG1860	1966	Not faith, sin, defiance, nor any disposition or duty of myself,
LG1860	1967	Not the promulgation of Liberty—not to cheer up slaves and horrify despots,
LG1860	1968	Not to build for that which builds for mankind,
LG1860	1969	Not to balance ranks, complexions, creeds, and the sexes,
LG1860	1970	Not to justify science, nor the march of equality,
LG1860	1971	Nor to feed the arrogant blood of the brawn beloved of time.
LG1860	1972	I swear I am for those that have never been mastered!
LG1860	1973	For men and women whose tempers have never been mastered,
LG1860	1974	For those whom laws, theories, conventions, can never master.
LG1860	1975	I swear I am for those who walk abreast with the whole earth!
LG1860	1976	Who inaugurate one to inaugurate all.
LG1860	1977	I swear I will not be outfaced by irrational things!
LG1860	1978	I will penetrate what it is in them that is sarcastic upon me!
LG1860	1979	I will make cities and civilizations defer to me!
LG1860	1980	(This is what I have learnt from America—it is the amount—and it I teach again.)
LG1860	1981	I will confront these shows of the day and night!
LG1860	1982	I will know if I am to be less than they!
LG1860	1983	I will see if I am not as majestic as they!
LG1860	1984	I will see if I am not as subtle and real as they!
LG1860	1985	I will see if I am to be less generous than they!
LG1860	1986	I will see if I have no meaning, while the houses and ships have meaning!
LG1860	1987	I will see if the fishes and birds are to be enough for themselves, and I am not to be enough for myself.
LG1860	1988	I match my spirit against yours, you orbs, growths, mountains, brutes,
LG1860	1989	Copious as you are, I absorb you all in myself, and become the master myself.
LG1860	1990	The Many In One—what is it finally except myself?
LG1860	1991	These States—what are they except myself?
LG1860	1992	I have learned why the earth is gross, tantalizing, wicked—it is for my sake,
LG1860	1993	I take you to be mine, you beautiful, terrible, rude forms.
LG1860	1994	BROAD-AXE, shapely, naked, wan!
LG1860	1995	Head from the mother's bowels drawn!
LG1860	1996	Wooded flesh and metal bone! limb only one and lip only one!

LG1860	2087	Muscle and pluck forever!
LG1860	2088	What invigorates life, invigorates death,
LG1860	2089	And the dead advance as much as the living advance,
LG1860	2090	And the future is no more uncertain than the present,
LG1860	2091	And the roughness of the earth and of man encloses as much as the delicatesse of the earth and of man,
LG1860	2092	And nothing endures but personal qualities.
LG1860	2093	What do you think endures?
LG1860	2094	Do you think the greatest city endures?
LG1860	2095	Or a teeming manufacturing state? or a prepared constitution? or the best built steamships?
LG1860	2096	Or hotels of granite and iron? or any chef-d'œuvres of engineering, forts, armaments?
LG1860	2097	Away! These are not to be cherished for themselves,
LG1860	2098	They fill their hour, the dancers dance, the musicians play for them,
LG1860	2099	The show passes, all does well enough of course,
LG1860	2100	All does very well till one flash of defiance.
LG1860	2101	The greatest city is that which has the greatest man or woman,
LG1860	2102	If it be a few ragged huts, it is still the greatest city in the whole world.
LG1860	2103	The place where the greatest city stands is not the place of stretched wharves, docks, manufactures, deposits of produce,
LG1860	2104	Nor the place of ceaseless salutes of new comers, or the anchor-lifters of the departing,
LG1860	2105	Nor the place of the tallest and costliest buildings, or shops selling goods from the rest of the earth,
LG1860	2106	Nor the place of the best libraries and schools—nor the place where money is plentiest,
LG1860	2107	Nor the place of the most numerous population.
LG1860	2108	Where the city stands with the brawniest breed of orators and bards,
LG1860	2109	Where the city stands that is beloved by these, and loves them in return, and understands them,
LG1860	2110	Where these may be seen going every day in the streets, with their arms familiar to the shoulders of their friends,
LG1860	2111	Where no monuments exist to heroes, but in the common words and deeds,
LG1860	2112	Where thrift is in its place, and prudence is in its place,
LG1860	2113	Where behavior is the finest of the fine arts,
LG1860	2114	Where the men and women think lightly of the laws,
LG1860	2115	Where the slave ceases, and the master of slaves ceases,
LG1860	2116	Where the populace rise at once against the never-endingneverending audacity of elected persons,
LG1860	2117	Where fierce men and women pour forth, as the sea to the whistle of death pours its sweeping and unript waves,
LG1860	2118	Where outside authority enters always after the precedence of inside authority,
LG1860	2119	Where the citizen is always the head and ideal—and President, Mayor, Governor, and what not, are agents for pay,
LG1860	2120	Where children are taught from the jump that they are to be laws to themselves, and to depend on themselves,
LG1860	2121	Where equanimity is illustrated in affairs,
LG1860	2122	Where speculations on the Soul are encouraged,
LG1860	2123	Where women walk in public processions in the streets, the same as the men,
LG1860	2124	Where they enter the public assembly and take places the same as the men, and are appealed to by the orators, the same as the men,
LG1860	2125	Where the city of the faithfulest friends stands,
LG1860	2126	Where the city of the cleanliness of the sexes stands,
LG1860	2127	Where the city of the healthiest fathers stands,
LG1860	2128	Where the city of the best-bodied mothers stands,
LG1860	2129	There the greatest city stands.
LG1860	2130	How beggarly appear poems, arguments, orations, before an electric deed!

LG1860	2245	Her shape arises,
LG1860	2246	She, less guarded than ever, yet more guarded than ever,
LG1860	2247	The gross and soiled she moves among do not make her gross and soiled,
LG1860	2248	She knows the thoughts as she passes—nothing is concealed from her,
LG1860	2249	She is none the less considerate or friendly therefore,
LG1860	2250	She is the best-beloved—it is without exception— she has no reason to fear, and she does not fear,
LG1860	2251	Oaths, quarrels, hiccupped songs, proposals, smutty expressions, are idle to her as she passes,
LG1860	2252	She is silent—she is possessed of herself—they do not offend her,
LG1860	2253	She receives them as the laws of nature receive them —she is strong,
LG1860	2254	She too is a law of nature—there is no law stronger than she is.
LG1860	2255	His shape arises,
LG1860	2256	Arrogant, masculine, näive, rowdyish,
LG1860	2257	Laugher, weeper, worker, idler, citizen, countryman,

LG1860	2259	Of pure American breed, of reckless health, his body perfect, free from taint from top to toe, free forever from headache and dyspepsia, clean- breathed,
LG1860	2260	Ample-limbed, a good feeder, weight a hundred and eighty pounds, full-blooded, six feet high, forty inches round the breast and back,
LG1860	2261	Countenance sun-burnt, bearded, calm, unrefined,
LG1860	2262	Reminder of animals, meeter of savage and gentleman on equal terms,
LG1860	2263	Attitudes lithe and erect, costume free, neck gray and open, of slow movement on foot,
LG1860	2264	Passer of his right arm round the shoulders of his friends, companion of the street,
LG1860	2265	Persuader always of people to give him their sweetest touches, and never their meanest,
LG1860	2266	A Manhattanese bred, fond of Brooklyn, fond of Broadway, fond of the life of the wharves and the great ferries,
LG1860	2267	Enterer everywhere, welcomed everywhere, easily understood after all,
LG1860	2268	Never offering others, always offering himself, corrob-oratingcorroborating his phrenology,
LG1860	2269	Voluptuous, inhabitive, combative, conscientious, alimentive, intuitive, of copious friendship, sublimity, firmness, self-esteem, comparison, individuality, form, locality, eventuality,
LG1860	2270	Avowing by life, manners, works, to contribute illus-trationsillustrations of results of The States,
LG1860	2271	Teacher of the unquenchable creed, namely, egotism,
LG1860	2272	Inviter of others continually henceforth to try their strength against his.
LG1860	2273	The main shapes arise!
LG1860	2274	Shapes of Democracy, final—result of centuries,
LG1860	2275	Shapes of those that do not joke with life, but are in earnest with life,
LG1860	2276	Shapes, ever projecting other shapes,
LG1860	2277	Shapes of a hundred Free States, begetting another hundred north and south,
LG1860	2278	Shapes of turbulent manly cities,
LG1860	2279	Shapes of an untamed breed of young men, and natural persons,
LG1860	2280	Shapes of the women fit for These States,
LG1860	2281	Shapes of the composition of all the varieties of the earth,
LG1860	2282	Shapes of the friends and home-givers of the whole earth,
LG1860	2283	Shapes bracing the whole earth, and braced with the whole earth.
LG1860	2284	COME closer to me,
LG1860	2285	Push closer, my lovers, and take the best I possess,
LG1860	2286	Yield closer and closer, and give me the best you
LG1860	2287	possess.
LG1860	2288	This is unfinished business with me—How is it with you?
LG1860	2289	I was chilled with the cold types, cylinder, wet paper between us.
LG1860	2290	Male and Female!
LG1860	2291	I pass so poorly with paper and types, I must pass with the contact of bodies and souls.
LG1860	2292	American masses!
LG1860	2293	I do not thank you for liking me as I am, and liking the touch of me—I know that it is good for you to do so.
LG1860	2294	Workmen and Workwomen!
LG1860	2295	Were all educations, practical and ornamental, well displayed out of me, what would it amount to?
LG1860	2296	Were I as the head teacher, charitable proprietor, wise statesman, what would it amount to?
LG1860	2297	Were I to you as the boss employing and paying you, would that satisfy you?
LG1860	2298	The learned, virtuous, benevolent, and the usual terms,
LG1860	2299	A man like me, and never the usual terms.
LG1860	2300	Neither a servant nor a master am I,
LG1860	2301	I take no sooner a large price than a small price— I will have my own, whoever enjoys me,
LG1860	2302	I will be even with you, and you shall be even with me.
LG1860	2303	If you stand at work in a shop, I stand as nigh as the nighest in the same shop,
LG1860	2304	If you bestow gifts on your brother or dearest friend, I demand as good as your brother or dearest friend,
LG1860	2305	If your lover, husband, wife, is welcome by day or night, I must be personally as welcome,
LG1860	2306	If you become degraded, criminal, ill, then I become so for your sake,
LG1860	2307	If you remember your foolish and outlawed deeds, do you think I cannot remember my own foolish and outlawed deeds? plenty of them;
LG1860	2308	If you carouse at the table, I carouse at the opposite side of the table,
LG1860	2309	If you meet some stranger in the streets, and love him or her, do I not often meet strangers in the street, and love them?
LG1860	2310	If you see a good deal remarkable in me, I see just as much, perhaps more, in you.
LG1860	2311	Why, what have you thought of yourself?
LG1860	2312	Is it you then that thought yourself less?
LG1860	2313	Is it you that thought the President greater than you?
LG1860	2314	Or the rich better off than you? or the educated wiser than you?
LG1860	2315	Because you are greasy or pimpled, or that you was once drunk, or a thief, or diseased, or rheumatic, or a prostitute, or are so now, or from frivolity or impotence, or that you are no scholar, and never saw your name in print, do you give in that you are any less immortal?
LG1860	2316	Souls of men and women! it is not you I call unseen, unheard, untouchable and untouching,
LG1860	2317	It is not you I go argue pro and con about, and to settle whether you are alive or no,
LG1860	2318	I own publicly who you are, if nobody else owns— I see and hear you, and what you give and take,
LG1860	2319	What is there you cannot give and take?
LG1860	2320	I see not merely that you are polite or white-faced, married, single, citizens of old States, citizens of new States,
LG1860	2321	Eminent in some profession, a lady or gentleman in a parlor, or dressed in the jail uniform, or pulpit uniform;
LG1860	2322	Grown, half-grown, and babe, of this country and every country, indoors and outdoors, one just as much as the other, I see,
LG1860	2323	And all else is behind or through them.
LG1860	2324	The wife—and she is not one jot less than the husband,
LG1860	2325	The daughter—and she is just as good as the son,
LG1860	2326	The mother—and she is every bit as much as the father.
LG1860	2327	Offspring of those not rich, boys apprenticed to trades,
LG1860	2328	Young fellows working on farms, and old fellows working on farms,
LG1860	2329	The näive, the simple and hardy, he going to the polls to vote, he who has a good time, and he has who a bad time,
LG1860	2330	Mechanics, southerners, new arrivals, laborers, sailors, man-o'wars-men, merchantmen, coasters,
LG1860	2331	All these I see—but nigher and farther the same I see,
LG1860	2332	None shall escape me, and none shall wish to escape me.
LG1860	2333	I bring what you much need, yet always have,
LG1860	2334	Not money, amours, dress, eating, but as good;
LG1860	2335	I send no agent or medium, offer no representative of value, but offer the value itself.
LG1860	2336	There is something that comes home to one now and perpetually,
LG1860	2337	It is not what is printed, preached, discussed—it eludes discussion and print,
LG1860	2338	It is not to be put in a book—it is not in this book,
LG1860	2339	It is for you, whoever you are—it is no farther from you than your hearing and sight are from you,
LG1860	2340	It is hinted by nearest, commonest, readiest—it is not them, though it is endlessly provoked by them, (what is there ready and near you now?)
LG1860	2341	You may read in many languages, yet read nothing about it,
LG1860	2342	You may read the President's Message, and read nothing about it there,
LG1860	2343	Nothing in the reports from the State department or Treasury department, or in the daily papers or the weekly papers,
LG1860	2344	Or in the census returns, assessors' returns, prices current, or any accounts of stock.
LG1860	2345	The sun and stars that float in the open air—the apple-shaped earth, and we upon it—surely the drift of them is something grand!
LG1860	2346	I do not know what it is, except that it is grand, and that it is happiness,
LG1860	2347	And that the enclosing purport of us here is not a speculation, or bon-mot, or reconnoissance,
LG1860	2348	And that it is not something which by luck may turn out well for us, and without luck must be a failure for us,
LG1860	2349	And not something which may yet be retracted in a certain contingency.
LG1860	2350	The light and shade, the curious sense of body and identity, the greed that with perfect com-plaisancecomplaisance devours all things, the endless pride and out-stretching of man, unspeakable joys and sorrows,
LG1860	2351	The wonder every one sees in every one else he sees, and the wonders that fill each minute of time for-everforever, and each acre of surface and space forever,
LG1860	2352	Have you reckoned them for a trade, or farm-work? or for the profits of a store? or to achieve your-selfyourself a position? or to fill a gentleman's leisure, or a lady's leisure?
LG1860	2353	Have you reckoned the landscape took substance and form that it might be painted in a picture?
LG1860	2354	Or men and women that they might be written of, and songs sung?
LG1860	2355	Or the attraction of gravity, and the great laws and harmonious combinations, and the fluids of the air, as subjects for the savans?
LG1860	2356	Or the brown land and the blue sea for maps and charts?
LG1860	2357	Or the stars to be put in constellations and named fancy names?
LG1860	2358	Or that the growth of seeds is for agricultural tables, or agriculture itself?
LG1860	2359	Old institutions—these arts, libraries, legends, col-lectionscollections, and the practice handed along in manu-facturesmanufactures—will we rate them so high?
LG1860	2360	Will we rate our cash and business high? I have no objection,
LG1860	2361	I rate them high as the highest—then a child born of a woman and man I rate beyond all rate.
LG1860	2362	We thought our Union grand, and our Constitution grand,
LG1860	2363	I do not say they are not grand and good, for they are,
LG1860	2364	I am this day just as much in love with them as you,

LG1860	2371	The President is there in the White House for you— it is not you who are here for him,

LG1860	2373	The Congress convenes every Twelfth Month for you,

LG1860	2518	Below, the red cedar, festooned with tylandria—the pines and cypresses, growing out of the white sand that spreads far and flat;
LG1860	2519	Rude boats descending the big Pedee—climbing plants, parasites, with colored flowers and berries,enveloping huge trees,
LG1860	2520	The waving drapery on the live oak, trailing long and low, noiselessly waved by the wind;
LG1860	2521	The camp of Georgia wagoners, just after dark—the supper-fires, and the cooking and eating by whites and negroes,
LG1860	2522	Thirty or forty great wagons—the mules, cattle,horses, feeding from troughs,
LG1860	2523	The shadows, gleams, up under the leaves of the old sycamore-trees—the flames—also the black smoke from the pitch-pine, curling and rising;
LG1860	2524	Southern fishermen fishing—the sounds and inlets of North Carolina's coast—the shad-fishery and the herring-fishery—the large sweep-seines —the windlasses on shore worked by horses—the clearing, curing, and packing houses;
LG1860	2525	Deep in the forest, in the piney woods, turpentine and tar dropping from the incisions in the trees —There is the turpentine distillery,
LG1860	2526	There are the negroes at work, in good health—the ground in all directions is covered with pine straw;
LG1860	2527	In Tennessee and Kentucky, slaves busy in the coal-ingscoalings, at the forge, by the furnace-blaze, or at the corn-shucking;
LG1860	2528	In Virginia, the planter's son returning after a long absence, joyfully welcomed and kissed by the aged mulatto nurse;
LG1860	2529	On rivers, boatmen safely moored at night-fall, in their boats, under the shelter of high banks,
LG1860	2530	Some of the younger men dance to the sound of the banjo or fiddle—others sit on the gunwale,smoking and talking;
LG1860	2531	Late in the afternoon, the mocking-bird, the American mimic, singing in the Great Dismal Swamp—there are the greenish waters, the resinous odor,the plenteous moss, the cypress tree, and the juniper tree;
LG1860	2532	Northward, young men of Mannahatta—the target company from an excursion returning home at evening—the musket-muzzles all bear bunches of flowers presented by women;
LG1860	2533	Children at play—or on his father's lap a young boy fallen asleep, (how his lips move! how he smiles in his sleep!)
LG1860	2534	The scout riding on horseback over the plains west of the Mississippi—he ascends a knoll and sweeps his eye around;
LG1860	2535	California life—the miner, bearded, dressed in his rude costume—the stanch California friendship —the sweet air—the graves one, in passing,meets, solitary, just aside the horse-path;
LG1860	2536	Down in Texas, the cotton-field, the negro-cabins—drivers driving mules or oxen before rude carts —cotton-bales piled on banks and wharves;
LG1860	2537	Encircling all, vast-darting, up and wide, the Amer-icanAmerican Soul, with equal hemispheres—one Love,one Dilation or Pride;
LG1860	2538	In arriere, the peace-talk with the Iroquois, the aborigines—the calumet, the pipe of good-will arbitration, and indorsement,
LG1860	2539	The sachem blowing the smoke first toward the sun and then toward the earth,
LG1860	2540	The drama of the scalp-dance enacted with painted faces and guttural exclamations,
LG1860	2541	The setting out of the war-party—the long and stealthy march,
LG1860	2542	The single file—the swinging hatchets—the surprise and slaughter of enemies;
LG1860	2543	All the acts, scenes, ways, persons, attitudes of These States—reminiscences, all institutions,
LG1860	2544	All These States, compact—Every square mile of These States, without excepting a particle—you also—me also,
LG1860	2545	Me pleased, rambling in lanes and country fields,Paumanok's fields,
LG1860	2546	Me, observing the spiral flight of two little yellow butterflies, shuffling between each other, ascend-ingascending high in the air;
LG1860	2547	The darting swallow, the destroyer of insects—the fall traveller southward, but returning northward early in the spring;
LG1860	2548	The country boy at the close of the day, driving the herd of cows, and shouting to them as they loiter to browse by the road-side;
LG1860	2549	The city wharf—Boston, Philadelphia, Baltimore,Charleston, New Orleans, San Francisco,
LG1860	2550	The departing ships, when the sailors heave at the capstan;
LG1860	2551	Evening—me in my room—the setting sun,
LG1860	2552	The setting summer sun shining in my open window,showing me flies, suspended, balancing in the air in the centre of the room, darting athwart,up and down, casting swift shadows in specks on the opposite wall, where the shine is;
LG1860	2553	The athletic American matron speaking in public to crowds of listeners;
LG1860	2554	Males, females, immigrants, combinations—the co-piousnesscopiousness—the individuality and sovereignty of The States, each for itself—the money-makers;
LG1860	2555	Factories, machinery, the mechanical forces—the windlass, lever, pulley—All certainties,
LG1860	2556	The certainty of space, increase, freedom, futurity,
LG1860	2557	In space, the sporades, the scattered islands, the stars —on the firm earth, the lands, my lands,
LG1860	2558	O lands! all so dear to me—what you are, (what-everwhatever it is,) I become a part of that, whatever it is,
LG1860	2559	Southward there, I screaming, with wings slow flap-pingflapping, with the myriads of gulls wintering along the coasts of Florida—or in Louisiana, with pelicans breeding,
LG1860	2560	Otherways, there, atwixt the banks of the Arkansaw,the Rio Grande, the Nueces, the Brazos, the Tombigbee, the Red River, the Saskatchawan, or the Osage, I with the spring waters laughing and skipping and running;
LG1860	2561	Northward, on the sands, on some shallow bay of Paumanok, I, with parties of snowy herons wading in the wet to seek worms and aquatic plants;
LG1860	2562	Retreating, triumphantly twittering, the king-bird,from piercing the crow with its bill, for amuse-mentamusement—And I triumphantly twittering;
LG1860	2563	The migrating flock of wild geese alighting in autumn to refresh themselves—the body of the flock feed —the sentinels outside move around with erect heads watching, and are from time to time re-lievedrelieved by other sentinels—And I feeding and taking turns with the rest;
LG1860	2564	In Kanadian forests, the moose, large as an ox, cor-neredcornered by hunters, rising desperately on his hind-feethindfeet, and plunging with his fore-feet, the hoofs as sharp as knives—And I, plunging at the hunters, cornered and desperate;
LG1860	2565	In the Mannahatta, streets, piers, shipping, store-houses, and the countless workmen working in the shops,
LG1860	2566	And I too of the Mannahatta, singing thereof—and no less in myself than the whole of the Manna-hattaMannahatta in itself,
LG1860	2567	Singing the song of These, my ever united lands —my body no more inevitably united, part to part, and made one identity, any more than my lands are inevitably united, and made ONE IDENTITY,
LG1860	2568	Nativities, climates, the grass of the great Pastoral Plains,
LG1860	2569	Cities, labors, death, animals, products, good and evil —these me,
LG1860	2570	These affording, in all their particulars, endless feuillage to me and to America, how can I do less than pass the clew of the union of them, to afford the like to you?
LG1860	2571	Whoever you are! how can I but offer you divine leaves, that you also be eligible as I am?
LG1860	2572	How can I but, as here, chanting, invite you for yourself to collect bouquets of the incomparable feuillage of These States?
LG1860	2573	RESPONDEZ! Respondez!
LG1860	2574	Let every one answer! Let those who sleep be waked! Let none evade—not you, any more than others!
LG1860	2575	(If it really be as is pretended, how much longer must we go on with our affectations and sneaking?
LG1860	2576	Let me bring this to a close—I pronounce openly for a new distribution of roles,)
LG1860	2577	Let that which stood in front go behind! and let that which was behind advance to the front and speak!
LG1860	2578	Let murderers, thieves, bigots, fools, unclean persons,offer new propositions!
LG1860	2579	Let the old propositions be postponed!
LG1860	2580	Let faces and theories be turned inside out! Let meanings be freely criminal, as well as results!
LG1860	2581	Let there be no suggestion above the suggestion of drudgery!
LG1860	2582	Let none be pointed toward his destination! (Say!do you know your destination?)
LG1860	2583	Let trillions of men and women be mocked with bodies and mocked with Souls!
LG1860	2584	Let the love that waits in them, wait! Let it die,or pass still-born to other spheres!
LG1860	2585	Let the sympathy that waits in every man, wait!or let it also pass, a dwarf, to other spheres!
LG1860	2586	Let contradictions prevail! Let one thing contradict another! and let one line of my poems contradict another!
LG1860	2587	Let the people sprawl with yearning aimless hands!Let their tongues be broken! Let their eyes be discouraged! Let none descend into their hearts with the fresh lusciousness of love!
LG1860	2588	Let the theory of America be management, caste,comparison! (Say! what other theory would you?)
LG1860	2589	Let them that distrust birth and death lead the rest! (Say! why shall they not lead you?)
LG1860	2590	Let the crust of hell be neared and trod on! Let the days be darker than the nights! Let slumber bring less slumber than waking-time brings!
LG1860	2591	Let the world never appear to him or her for whom it was all made!
LG1860	2592	Let the heart of the young man exile itself from the heart of the old man! and let the heart of the old man be exiled from that of the young man!
LG1860	2593	Let the sun and moon go! Let scenery take the applause of the audience! Let there be apathy under the stars!
LG1860	2594	Let freedom prove no man's inalienable right! Every one who can tyrannize, let him tyrannize to his satisfaction!
LG1860	2595	Let none but infidels be countenanced!
LG1860	2596	Let the eminence of meanness, treachery, sarcasm,hate, greed, indecency, impotence, lust, be taken for granted above all! Let writers, judges, gov-ernmentsgovernments, households, religions, philosophies, take such for granted above all!
LG1860	2597	Let the worst men beget children out of the worst women!
LG1860	2598	Let priests still play at immortality!
LG1860	2599	Let Death be inaugurated!
LG1860	2600	Let nothing remain upon the earth except the ashes of teachers, artists, moralists, lawyers, and learned and polite persons!
LG1860	2601	Let him who is without my poems be assassinated!
LG1860	2602	Let the cow, the horse, the camel, the garden-bee—Let the mud-fish, the lobster, the mussel, eel, the sting-ray, and the grunting pig-fish—Let these,and the like of these, be put on a perfect equality with man and woman!
LG1860	2603	Let churches accommodate serpents, vermin, and the corpses of those who have died of the most filthy of diseases!
LG1860	2604	Let marriage slip down among fools, and be for none but fools!
LG1860	2605	Let men among themselves talk and think obscenely of women! and let women among themselves talk and think obscenely of men!
LG1860	2606	Let every man doubt every woman! and let every woman trick every man!
LG1860	2607	Let us all, without missing one, be exposed in public,naked, monthly, at the peril of our lives! Let our bodies be freely handled and examined by whoever chooses!
LG1860	2608	Let nothing but copies, pictures, statues, reminis-cencesreminiscences, elegant works, be permitted to exist upon the earth!
LG1860	2609	Let the earth desert God, nor let there ever hence-forthhenceforth be mentioned the name of God!
LG1860	2610	Let there be no God!
LG1860	2611	Let there be money, business, imports, exports, cus-tomcustom, authority, precedents, pallor, dyspepsia,smut, ignorance, unbelief!
LG1860	2612	Let judges and criminals be transposed! Let the prison-keepers be put in prison! Let those that were prisoners take the keys! (Say! why might they not just as well be transposed?)
LG1860	2613	Let the slaves be masters! Let the masters become slaves!
LG1860	2614	Let the reformers descend from the stands where they are forever bawling! Let an idiot or insane person appear on each of the stands!
LG1860	2615	Let the Asiatic, the African, the European, the American and the Australian, go armed against the murderous stealthiness of each other! Let them sleep armed! Let none believe in good-will!
LG1860	2616	Let there be no unfashionable wisdom! Let such be scorned and derided off from the earth!
LG1860	2617	Let a floating cloud in the sky—Let a wave of the sea—Let one glimpse of your eye-sight upon the landscape or grass—Let growing mint, spinach,onions, tomatoes—Let these be exhibited as shows at a great price for admission!
LG1860	2618	Let all the men of These States stand aside for a few smouchers! Let the few seize on what they choose! Let the rest gawk, giggle, starve, obey!
LG1860	2619	Let shadows be furnished with genitals! Let sub-stancessubstances be deprived of their genitals!
LG1860	2620	Let there be wealthy and immense cities—but through any of them, not a single poet, saviour,knower, lover!
LG1860	2621	Let the infidels of These States laugh all faith away!If one man be found who has faith, let the rest set upon him! Let them affright faith! Let them destroy the power of breeding faith!
LG1860	2622	Let the she-harlots and the he-harlots be prudent!Let them dance on, while seeming lasts! (O seeming! seeming! seeming!)
LG1860	2623	Let the preachers recite creeds! Let them teach only what they have been taught!
LG1860	2624	Let the preachers of creeds never dare to go meditate candidly upon the hills, alone, by day or by night! (If one ever once dare, he is lost!)
LG1860	2625	Let insanity have charge of sanity!
LG1860	2626	Let books take the place of trees, animals, rivers,clouds!
LG1860	2627	Let the daubed portraits of heroes supersede heroes!
LG1860	2628	Let the manhood of man never take steps after itself!Let it take steps after eunuchs, and after con-sumptiveconsumptive and genteel persons!
LG1860	2629	Let the white person tread the black person under his heel! (Say! which is trodden under heel, after all ?)
LG1860	2630	Let the reflections of the things of the world be studied in mirrors! Let the things themselves continue unstudied!
LG1860	2631	Let a man seek pleasure everywhere except in him-selfhimself! Let a woman seek happiness everywhere except in herself! (Say! what real happiness have you had one single time through your whole life ?)
LG1860	2632	Let the limited years of life do nothing for the limit-lesslimitless years of death! (Say! what do you suppose death will do, then ?)
LG1860	2633	YOU just maturing youth! You male or female!
LG1860	2634	Remember the organic compact of These States,
LG1860	2635	Remember the pledge of the Old Thirteen thence-forwardthenceforward to the rights, life, liberty, equality of man,
LG1860	2636	Remember what was promulged by the founders, rat-ifiedratified by The States, signed in black and white by the Commissioners, and read by Washington at the head of the army,
LG1860	2637	Remember the purpose of the founders,—Remember Washington;
LG1860	2638	Remember the copious humanity streaming from every direction toward America;
LG1860	2639	Remember the hospitality that belongs to nations and men; (Cursed be nation, woman, man, without hospitality!)
LG1860	2640	Remember, government is to subserve individuals,
LG1860	2641	Not any, not the President, is to have one jot more than you or me,
LG1860	2642	Not any habitan of America is to have one jot less than you or me.
LG1860	2643	Anticipate when the thirty or fifty millions, are to be-comebecome the hundred, or two hundred millions, of equal freemen and freewomen, amicably joined.
LG1860	2644	Recall ages—One age is but a part—ages are but a part;
LG1860	2645	Recall the angers, bickerings, delusions, superstitions, of the idea of caste,
LG1860	2646	Recall the bloody cruelties and crimes.
LG1860	2647	Anticipate the best women;
LG1860	2648	I say an unnumbered new race of hardy and well-definedwelldefined women are to spread through all These States,
LG1860	2649	I say a girl fit for These States must be free, capable, dauntless, just the same as a boy.
LG1860	2650	Anticipate your own life—retract with merciless power,
LG1860	2651	Shirk nothing—retract in time—Do you see those errors, diseases, weaknesses, lies, thefts?
LG1860	2652	Do you see that lost character?—Do you see de-caydecay, consumption, rum-drinking, dropsy, fever, mortal cancer or inflammation?
LG1860	2653	Do you see death, and the approach of death?
LG1860	2654	Think of the Soul;
LG1860	2655	I swear to you that body of yours gives proportions to your Soul somehow to live in other spheres,
LG1860	2656	I do not know how, but I know it is so.
LG1860	2657	Think of loving and being loved;
LG1860	2658	I swear to you, whoever you are, you can interfuse yourself with such things that everybody that sees you shall look longingly upon you.
LG1860	2659	Think of the past;
LG1860	2660	I warn you that in a little while, others will find their past in you and your times.
LG1860	2661	The race is never separated—nor man nor woman escapes,
LG1860	2662	All is inextricable—things, spirits, nature, nations, you too—from precedents you come.
LG1860	2663	Recall the ever-welcome defiers, (The mothers precede them;)
LG1860	2664	Recall the sages, poets, saviours, inventors, lawgivers, of the earth,
LG1860	2665	Recall Christ, brother of rejected persons—brother of slaves, felons, idiots, and of insane and diseased persons.
LG1860	2666	Think of the time when you was not yet born,
LG1860	2667	Think of times you stood at the side of the dying,
LG1860	2668	Think of the time when your own body will be dying.
LG1860	2669	Think of spiritual results,
LG1860	2670	Sure as the earth swims through the heavens, does every one of its objects pass into spiritual results.
LG1860	2671	Think of manhood, and you to be a man;
LG1860	2672	Do you count manhood, and the sweet of manhood, nothing?
LG1860	2673	Think of womanhood, and you to be a woman;
LG1860	2674	The creation is womanhood,
LG1860	2675	Have I not said that womanhood involves all?
LG1860	2676	Have I not told how the universe has nothing better than the best womanhood?
LG1860	2677	WITH antecedents,
LG1860	2678	With my fathers and mothers, and the accumulations of past ages,
LG1860	2679	With all which, had it not been, I would not now be here, as I am,
LG1860	2680	With Egypt, India, Phenicia, Greece, and Rome,
LG1860	2681	With the Celt, the Scandinavian, the Alb, and the Saxon,
LG1860	2682	With antique maritime ventures—with laws, arti-sanshipartisanship, wars, and journeys,
LG1860	2683	With the poet, the skald, the saga, the myth, and the oracle,
LG1860	2684	With the sale of slaves—with enthusiasts—with the troubadour, the crusader, and the monk,
LG1860	2685	With those old continents whence we have come to this new continent,
LG1860	2686	With the fading kingdoms and kings over there,
LG1860	2687	With the fading religions and priests,
LG1860	2688	With the small shores we look back to, from our own large and present shores,
LG1860	2689	With countless years drawing themselves onward, and arrived at these years,
LG1860	2690	You and Me arrived—America arrived, and making this year,
LG1860	2691	This year! sending itself ahead countless years to come.
LG1860	2692	O but it is not the years—it is I—it is You,
LG1860	2693	We touch all laws, and tally all antecedents,
LG1860	2694	We are the skald, the oracle, the monk, and the knight—we easily include them, and more,
LG1860	2695	We stand amid time, beginningless and endless—we stand amid evil and good,
LG1860	2696	All swings around us—there is as much darkness as light,
LG1860	2697	The very sun swings itself and its system of planets around us,
LG1860	2698	Its sun, and its again, all swing around us.
LG1860	2699	As for me,
LG1860	2700	I have the idea of all, and am all, and believe in all;
LG1860	2701	I believe materialism is true, and spiritualism is true— I reject no part.
LG1860	2702	Have I forgotten any part?
LG1860	2703	Come to me, whoever and whatever, till I give you recognition.
LG1860	2704	I respect Assyria, China, Teutonia, and the Hebrews,
LG1860	2705	I adopt each theory, myth, god, and demi-god,
LG1860	2706	I see that the old accounts, bibles, genealogies, are true, without exception,
LG1860	2707	I assert that all past days were what they should have been,
LG1860	2708	And that they could no-how have been better than they were,
LG1860	2709	And that to-day is what it should be—and that America is,
LG1860	2710	And that to-day and America could no-how be better than they are.
LG1860	2711	In the name of These States, and in your and my name, the Past,
LG1860	2712	And in the name of These States, and in your and my name, the Present time.
LG1860	2713	I know that the past was great, and the future will be great,
LG1860	2714	And I know that both curiously conjoint in the pres-entpresent time,
LG1860	2715	(For the sake of him I typify—for the common average man's sake—your sake, if you are he;)
LG1860	2716	And that where I am, or you are, this present day, there is the centre of all days, all races,
LG1860	2717	And there is the meaning, to us, of all that has ever come of races and days, or ever will come.
LG1860	2718	SPLENDOR of falling day, floating and filling me,
LG1860	2719	Hour prophetic—hour resuming the past,
LG1860	2720	Inflating my throat—you, divine average!
LG1860	2721	You, Earth and Life, till the last ray gleams, I sing.
LG1860	2722	Open mouth of my Soul, uttering gladness,
LG1860	2723	Eyes of my Soul, seeing perfection,
LG1860	2724	Natural life of me, faithfully praising things,
LG1860	2725	Corroborating forever the triumph of things.
LG1860	2726	Illustrious every one!
LG1860	2727	Illustrious what we name space—sphere of unnum-beredunnumbered spirits,
LG1860	2728	Illustrious the mystery of motion, in all beings, even the tiniest insect,
LG1860	2729	Illustrious the attribute of speech—the senses—the body,
LG1860	2730	Illustrious the passing light! Illustrious the pale reflection on the moon in the western sky!
LG1860	2731	Illustrious whatever I see, or hear, or touch, to the last.
LG1860	2732	Good in all,
LG1860	2733	In the satisfaction and aplomb of animals,
LG1860	2734	In the annual return of the seasons,
LG1860	2735	In the hilarity of youth,
LG1860	2736	In the strength and flush of manhood,
LG1860	2737	In the grandeur and exquisiteness of old age,
LG1860	2738	In the superb vistas of Death.
LG1860	2739	Wonderful to depart!
LG1860	2740	Wonderful to be here!
LG1860	2741	The heart, to jet the all-alike and innocent blood,
LG1860	2742	To breathe the air, how delicious!
LG1860	2743	To speak! to walk! to seize something by the hand!
LG1860	2744	To prepare for sleep, for bed—to look on my rose- colored flesh,
LG1860	2745	To be conscious of my body, so amorous, so large,
LG1860	2746	To be this incredible God I am,
LG1860	2747	To have gone forth among other Gods—those men and women I love.
LG1860	2748	Wonderful how I celebrate you and myself!
LG1860	2749	How my thoughts play subtly at the spectacles around!
LG1860	2750	How the clouds pass silently overhead!
LG1860	2751	How the earth darts on and on! and how the sun, moon, stars, dart on and on!
LG1860	2752	How the water sports and sings! (Surely it is alive!)
LG1860	2753	How the trees rise and stand up—with strong trunks —with branches and leaves!
LG1860	2754	(Surely there is something more in each of the trees— some living Soul.)
LG1860	2755	O amazement of things! even the least particle!
LG1860	2756	O spirituality of things!
LG1860	2757	O strain musical, flowing through ages and continents —now reaching me and America!
LG1860	2758	I take your strong chords—I intersperse them, and cheerfully pass them forward.
LG1860	2759	I too carol the sun, ushered, or at noon, or setting,
LG1860	2760	I too throb to the brain and beauty of the earth, and of all the growths of the earth,
LG1860	2761	I too have felt the resistless call of myself.
LG1860	2762	As I sailed down the Mississippi,
LG1860	2763	As I wandered over the prairies,
LG1860	2764	As I have lived—As I have looked through my windows, my eyes,
LG1860	2765	As I went forth in the morning—As I beheld the light breaking in the east,
LG1860	2766	As I bathed on the beach of the Eastern Sea, and again on the beach on the Western Sea,
LG1860	2767	As I roamed the streets of inland Chicago—whatever streets I have roamed,
LG1860	2768	Wherever I have been, I have charged myself with contentment and triumph.
LG1860	2769	I sing the Equalities,
LG1860	2770	I sing the endless finales of things,
LG1860	2771	I say Nature continues—Glory continues,
LG1860	2772	I praise with electric voice,
LG1860	2773	For I do not see one imperfection in the universe,
LG1860	2774	And I do not see one cause or result lamentable at last in the universe.
LG1860	2775	O setting sun! O when the time comes,
LG1860	2776	I still warble under you, if none else does, unmiti-gatedunmitigated adoration!
LG1860	2777	A THOUGHT of what I am here for,
LG1860	2778	Of these years I sing—how they pass through con-vulsedconvulsed pains, as through parturitions;
LG1860	2779	How America illustrates birth, gigantic youth, the promise, the sure fulfilment, despite of people —Illustrates evil as well as good;
LG1860	2780	Of how many hold despairingly yet to the models departed, caste, myths, obedience, compulsion,and to infidelity;
LG1860	2781	How few see the arrived models, the Athletes, The States—or see freedom or spirituality—or hold any faith in results,
LG1860	2782	(But I see the Athletes—and I see the results glorious and inevitable—and they again leading to other results;)
LG1860	2783	How the great cities appear—How the Democratic masses, turbulent, wilful, as I love them,
LG1860	2784	How the whirl, the contest, the wrestle of evil with good, the sounding and resounding, keep on and on;
LG1860	2785	How society waits unformed, and is between things ended and things begun;
LG1860	2786	How America is the continent of glories, and of the triumph of freedom, and of the Democracies, and of the fruits of society, and of all that is begun;
LG1860	2787	And how The States are complete in themselves—And how all triumphs and glories are complete in themselves, to lead onward,
LG1860	2788	And how these of mine, and of The States, will in their turn be convulsed, and serve other par-turitionsparturitions and transitions,
LG1860	2789	And how all people, sights, combinations, the Demo-craticDemocratic masses too, serve—and how every fact serves,
LG1860	2790	And how now, or at any time, each serves the exquisite transition of Death.
LG1860	2791	HISTORIAN! you who celebrate bygones!
LG1860	2792	You have explored the outward, the surface of the races—the life that has exhibited itself,
LG1860	2793	You have treated man as the creature of politics,aggregates, rulers, and priests;
LG1860	2794	But now I also, arriving, contribute something:
LG1860	2795	I, an habitué of the Alleghanies, treat man as he is in the influences of Nature, in himself, in his own inalienable rights,
LG1860	2796	Advancing, to give the spirit and the traits of new Democratic ages, myself, personally,
LG1860	2797	(Let the future behold them all in me—Me, so puzzling and contradictory—Me, a Manhattan-eseManhattanese, the most loving and arrogant of men;)
LG1860	2798	I do not tell the usual facts, proved by records and documents,
LG1860	2799	What I tell, (talking to every born American,)requires no further proof than he or she who will hear me, will furnish, by silently meditating alone;
LG1860	2800	I press the pulse of the life that has hitherto seldom exhibited itself, but has generally sought con-cealmentConcealment, (the great pride of man, in himself,)
LG1860	2801	I illuminate feelings, faults, yearnings, hopes—I have come at last, no more ashamed nor afraid;
LG1860	2802	Chanter of Personality, outlining a history yet to be,
LG1860	2803	I project the ideal man, the American of the future.
LG1860	2804	THE thought of fruitage,
LG1860	2805	Of Death, (the life greater)—of seeds dropping into the ground—of birth,
LG1860	2806	Of the steady concentration of America, inland,upward, to impregnable and swarming places,
LG1860	2807	Of what Indiana, Kentucky, Ohio and the rest, are to be,
LG1860	2808	Of what a few years will show there in Missouri,Kansas, Iowa, Wisconsin, Minnesota and the rest,
LG1860	2809	Of what the feuillage of America is the preparation for—and of what all the sights, North, South,East and West, are;
LG1860	2810	Of the temporary use of materials for identity's sake,
LG1860	2811	Of departing—of the growth of a mightier race than any yet,
LG1860	2812	Of myself, soon, perhaps, closing up my songs by these shores,
LG1860	2813	Of California—of Oregon—and of me journeying hence to live and sing there;
LG1860	2814	Of the Western Sea—of the spread inland between it and the spinal river,
LG1860	2815	Of the great pastoral area, athletic and feminine,
LG1860	2816	Of all sloping down there where the fresh free-giverfreegiver, the mother, the Mississippi flows—and Westward still;
LG1860	2817	Of future men and women there—of happiness in those high plateaus, ranging three thousand miles, warm and cold,
LG1860	2818	Of cities yet unsurveyed and unsuspected, (as I am also, and as it must be,)
LG1860	2819	Of the new and good names—of the strong develop-mentsdevelopments—of the inalienable homesteads,
LG1860	2820	Of a free original life there—of simple diet, and clean and sweet blood,
LG1860	2821	Of litheness, majestic faces, clear eyes, and perfect physique there,
LG1860	2822	Of immense spiritual results, future years, inland,spread there each side of the Anahuacs,
LG1860	2823	Of these Leaves well-understood there, (being made for that area,)
LG1860	2824	Of the native scorn of grossness and gain there,
LG1860	2825	(O it lurks in me night and day—What is gain,after all, to savageness and freedom?)
LG1860	2826	TO oratists—to male or female,
LG1860	2827	Vocalism, breath, measure, concentration, determina-tiondetermination, and the divine power to use words.
LG1860	2828	Are you eligible?
LG1860	2829	Are you full-lung'd and limber-lipp'd from long trial? from vigorous practice? from physique?
LG1860	2830	Do you move in these broad lands as broad as they?
LG1860	2831	Remembering inland America, the high plateaus, stretching long?
LG1860	2832	Remembering Kanada—Remembering what edges the vast round edge of the Mexican Sea?
LG1860	2833	Come duly to the divine power to use words?
LG1860	2834	For only at last, after many years—after chastity, friendship, procreation, prudence, and nakedness,
LG1860	2835	After treading ground and breasting river and lake,
LG1860	2836	After a loosened throat—after absorbing eras, tem-peramentstemperaments, races—after knowledge, freedom, crimes,
LG1860	2837	After complete faith—after clarifyings, elevations, and removing obstructions,
LG1860	2838	After these, and more, it is just possible there comes to a man, a woman, the divine power to use words.
LG1860	2839	Then toward that man or that woman swiftly hasten all—None refuse, all attend,
LG1860	2840	Armies, ships, antiquities, the dead, libraries, paint-ingspaintings, machines, cities, hate, despair, amity, pain, theft, murder, aspiration, form in close ranks,
LG1860	2841	They debouch as they are wanted to march obediently through the mouth of that man, or that woman.
LG1860	2842	O now I see arise orators fit for inland America,
LG1860	2843	And I see it is as slow to become an orator as to become a man,
LG1860	2844	And I see that power is folded in a great vocalism.
LG1860	2845	Of a great vocalism, when you hear it, the merciless light shall pour, and the storm rage around,
LG1860	2846	Every flash shall be a revelation, an insult,
LG1860	2847	The glaring flame turned on depths, on heights, on suns, on stars,
LG1860	2848	On the interior and exterior of man or woman,
LG1860	2849	On the laws of Nature—on passive materials,
LG1860	2850	On what you called death—and what to you there-foretherefore was death,
LG1860	2851	As far as there can be death.
LG1860	2852	LAWS for Creations,
LG1860	2853	For strong artists and leaders—for fresh broods of teachers, and perfect literats for America,
LG1860	2854	For diverse savans, and coming musicians.
LG1860	2855	There shall be no subject but it shall be treated with reference to the ensemble of the world, and the compact truth of the world—And no coward or copyist shall be allowed;
LG1860	2856	There shall be no subject too pronounced—All works shall illustrate the divine law of indirections;
LG1860	2857	There they stand—I see them already, each poised and in its place,
LG1860	2858	Statements, models, censuses, poems, dictionaries, biographies, essays, theories—How complete! How relative and interfused! No one super-sedessupersedes another;
LG1860	2859	They do not seem to me like the old specimens,
LG1860	2860	They seem to me like Nature at last, (America has given birth to them, and I have also;)
LG1860	2861	They seem to me at last as perfect as the animals, and as the rocks and weeds—fitted to them,
LG1860	2862	Fitted to the sky, to float with floating clouds—to rustle among the trees with rustling leaves,
LG1860	2863	To stretch with stretched and level waters, where ships silently sail in the distance.
LG1860	2864	What do you suppose Creation is?
LG1860	2865	What do you suppose will satisfy the Soul, except to walk free and own no superior?
LG1860	2866	What do you suppose I have intimated to you in a hundred ways, but that man or woman is as good as God?
LG1860	2867	And that there is no God any more divine than Yourself?
LG1860	2868	And that that is what the oldest and newest myths finally mean?
LG1860	2869	And that you or any one must approach Creations through such laws?
LG1860	2870	POETS to come!
LG1860	2871	Not to-day is to justify me, and Democracy, and what we are for,
LG1860	2872	But you, a new brood, native, athletic, continental, greater than before known,
LG1860	2873	You must justify me.
LG1860	2874	Indeed, if it were not for you, what would I be?
LG1860	2875	What is the little I have done, except to arouse you?
LG1860	2876	I depend on being realized, long hence, where the broad fat prairies spread, and thence to Oregon and California inclusive,
LG1860	2877	I expect that the Texan and the Arizonian, ages hence, will understand me,
LG1860	2878	I expect that the future Carolinian and Georgian will understand me and love me,
LG1860	2879	I expect that Kanadians, a hundred, and perhaps many hundred years from now, in winter, in the splendor of the snow and woods, or on the icy lakes, will take me with them, and permanently enjoy themselves with me.
LG1860	2880	Of to-day I know I am momentary, untouched—I am the bard of the future,
LG1860	2881	I but write one or two indicative words for the future,
LG1860	2882	I but advance a moment, only to wheel and hurry back in the darkness.
LG1860	2883	I am a man who, sauntering along, without fully stopping, turns a casual look upon you, and then averts his face,
LG1860	2884	Leaving it to you to prove and define it,
LG1860	2885	Expecting the main things from you.
LG1860	2886	WHO has gone farthest? For I swear I will go farther;
LG1860	2887	And who has been just? For I would be the most just person of the earth;
LG1860	2888	And who most cautious? For I would be more cautious;
LG1860	2889	And who has been happiest? O I think it is I! I think no one was ever happier than I;
LG1860	2890	And who has lavished all? For I lavish constantly the best I have;
LG1860	2891	And who has been firmest? For I would be firmer;
LG1860	2892	And who proudest? For I think I have reason to be the proudest son alive—for I am the son of the brawny and tall-topt city;
LG1860	2893	And who has been bold and true? For I would be the boldest and truest being of the universe;
LG1860	2894	And who benevolent? For I would show more be-nevolencebenevolence than all the rest;
LG1860	2895	And who has projected beautiful words through the longest time? By God! I will outvie him! I will say such words, they shall stretch through longer time!
LG1860	2896	And who has received the love of the most friends?For I know what it is to receive the passionate love of many friends;
LG1860	2897	And to whom has been given the sweetest from women, and paid them in kind? For I will take the like sweets and pay them in kind;
LG1860	2898	And who possesses a perfect and enamoured body?For I do not believe any one possesses a more perfect or enamoured body than mine;
LG1860	2899	And who thinks the amplest thoughts? For I will surround those thoughts;
LG1860	2900	And who has made hymns fit for the earth? For I am mad with devouring extacy to make joyous hymns for the whole earth!
LG1860	2901	THEY shall arise in the States—mediums shall,
LG1860	2902	They shall report Nature, laws, physiology, and happiness,
LG1860	2903	They shall illustrate Democracy and the kosmos,
LG1860	2904	They shall be alimentive, amative, perceptive,
LG1860	2905	They shall be complete women and men—their pose brawny and supple, their drink water, their blood clean and clear,
LG1860	2906	They shall enjoy materialism and the sight of prod-uctsproducts—they shall enjoy the sight of the beef,lumber, bread-stuffs, of Chicago, the great city,
LG1860	2907	They shall train themselves to go in public to become oratists, (orators and oratresses,)
