LG1860	2909	Of them, and of their works, shall emerge divine conveyers, to convey gospels,
LG1860	2910	Characters, events, retrospections, shall be conveyed in gospels—Trees, animals, waters, shall be conveyed,
LG1860	2911	Death, the future, the invisible faith, shall all be conveyed.
LG1860	2912	NOW we start hence, I with the rest, on our jour-neysjourneys through The States,
LG1860	2913	We willing learners of all, teachers of all, and lovers of all.
LG1860	2914	I have watched the seasons dispensing themselves, and passing on,
LG1860	2915	And I have said, Why should not a man or woman do as much as the seasons, and effuse as much?
LG1860	2916	We dwell a while in every city and town,
LG1860	2917	We pass through Kanada, the north-east, the vast valley of the Mississippi, and the Southern States,
LG1860	2918	We confer on equal terms with each of The States,
LG1860	2919	We make trial of ourselves, and invite men and women to hear,
LG1860	2920	We say to ourselves, Remember, fear not, be candid, promulge the body and the Soul,
LG1860	2921	Promulge real things—Never forget the equality of humankind, and never forget immortality;
LG1860	2922	Dwell a while, and pass on—Be copious, temperate, chaste, magnetic,
LG1860	2923	And what you effuse may then return as the seasons return,
LG1860	2924	And may be just as much as the seasons.
LG1860	2925	ME imperturbe,
LG1860	2926	Me standing at ease in Nature,
LG1860	2927	Master of all, or mistress of all—aplomb in the midst of irrational things,
LG1860	2928	Imbued as they—passive, receptive, silent as they,
LG1860	2929	Finding my occupation, poverty, notoriety, foibles,crimes, less important than I thought;
LG1860	2930	Me private, or public, or menial, or solitary—all these subordinate, (I am eternally equal with the best—I am not subordinate;)
LG1860	2931	Me toward the Mexican Sea, or in the Mannahatta,or the Tennessee, or far north, or inland,
LG1860	2932	A river-man, or a man of the woods, or of any farm-life of These States, or of the coast, or the lakes,or Kanada,
LG1860	2933	Me, wherever my life is to be lived, O to be self-bal-ancedself-balanced for contingencies!
LG1860	2934	O to confront night, storms, hunger, ridicule, acci-dentsaccidents, rebuffs, as the trees and animals do.
LG1860	2935	I WAS looking a long while for the history of the past for myself, and for these Chants—and now I have found it,
LG1860	2936	It is not in those paged fables in the libraries, (them I neither accept nor reject,)
LG1860	2937	It is no more in the legends than in all else,
LG1860	2938	It is in the present—it is this earth to-day,
LG1860	2939	It is in Democracy—in this America—the old world also,
LG1860	2940	It is the life of one man or one woman to-day, the average man of to-day;
LG1860	2941	It is languages, social customs, literatures, arts,
LG1860	2942	It is the broad show of artificial things, ships, ma-chinerymachinery, politics, creeds, modern improvements,and the interchanges of nations,
LG1860	2943	All for the average man of to-day.
LG1860	2944	AMERICAN mouth-songs!
LG1860	2945	Those of mechanics—each one singing, his, as it should be, blithe and strong,
LG1860	2946	The carpenter singing his, as he measures his plank or beam,
LG1860	2947	The mason singing his, as he makes ready for work, or leaves off work,
LG1860	2948	The boatman singing what belongs to him in his boat —the deck-hand singing on the steamboat deck,
LG1860	2949	The shoemaker singing as he sits on his bench—the hatter singing as he stands,
LG1860	2950	The wood-cutter's song—the ploughboy's, on his way in the morning, or at the noon intermission, or at sundown;
LG1860	2951	The delicious singing of the mother—or of the young wife at work—or of the girl sewing or washing—Each singing what belongs to her, and to none else,
LG1860	2952	The day what belongs to the day—At night, the party, of young fellows, robust, friendly, clean- blooded, singing with melodious voices, melo-diousmelodious thoughts.
LG1860	2953	Come! some of you! still be flooding The States with hundreds and thousands of mouth-songs, fit for The States only.
LG1860	2954	As I walk, solitary, unattended,
LG1860	2955	Around me I hear that eclat of the world—politics, produce,
LG1860	2956	The announcements of recognized things—science,
LG1860	2957	The approved growth of cities, and the spread of inventions.
LG1860	2958	I see the ships, (they will last a few years,)
LG1860	2959	The vast factories with their foremen and workmen,
LG1860	2960	And hear the indorsement of all, and do not object to it.
LG1860	2961	But we too announce solid things,
LG1860	2962	Science, ships, politics, cities, factories, are not noth-ingnothing—they serve,
LG1860	2963	They stand for realities—all is as it should be.
LG1860	2964	Then my realities,
LG1860	2965	What else is so real as mine?
LG1860	2966	Libertad, and the divine average—Freedom to every slave on the face of the earth,
LG1860	2967	The rapt promises and lumine of seers—the spir-itualspiritual world—these centuries-lasting songs,
LG1860	2968	And our visions, the visions of poets, the most solid announcements of any.
LG1860	2969	For we support all,
LG1860	2970	After the rest is done and gone, we remain,
LG1860	2971	There is no final reliance but upon us,
LG1860	2972	Democracy rests finally upon us, (I, my brethren, begin it,)
LG1860	2973	And our visions sweep through eternity.
LG1860	2974	ELEMENTAL drifts!
LG1860	2975	O I wish I could impress others as you and the waves have just been impressing me.
LG1860	2976	As I ebbed with an ebb of the ocean of life,
LG1860	2977	As I wended the shores I know,
LG1860	2978	As I walked where the sea-ripples wash you, Pau-manokPaumanok,
LG1860	2979	Where they rustle up, hoarse and sibilant,
LG1860	2980	Where the fierce old mother endlessly cries for her castaways,
LG1860	2981	I, musing, late in the autumn day, gazing off south-wardsouthward,
LG1860	2982	Alone, held by the eternal self of me that threatens to get the better of me, and stifle me,
LG1860	2983	Was seized by the spirit that trails in the lines underfoot,
LG1860	2984	In the rim, the sediment, that stands for all the water and all the land of the globe.
LG1860	2985	Fascinated, my eyes, reverting from the south, dropped, to follow those slender winrows,

LG1860	2988	Miles walking, the sound of breaking waves the other side of me,
LG1860	2989	Paumanok, there and then, as I thought the old thought of likenesses,
LG1860	2990	These you presented to me, you fish-shaped island,
LG1860	2991	As I wended the shores I know,
LG1860	2992	As I walked with that eternal self of me, seeking types.
LG1860	2993	As I wend the shores I know not,
LG1860	2994	As I listen to the dirge, the voices of men and women wrecked,
LG1860	2995	As I inhale the impalpable breezes that set in upon me,
LG1860	2996	As the ocean so mysterious rolls toward me closer and closer,
LG1860	2997	At once I find, the least thing that belongs to me, or that I see or touch, I know not;
LG1860	2998	I, too, but signify, at the utmost, a little washed-up drift,
LG1860	2999	A few sands and dead leaves to gather,
LG1860	3000	Gather, and merge myself as part of the sands and drift.
LG1860	3001	O baffled, balked,
LG1860	3002	Bent to the very earth, here preceding what follows,
LG1860	3003	Oppressed with myself that I have dared to open my mouth,
LG1860	3004	Aware now, that, amid all the blab whose echoes recoil upon me, I have not once had the least idea who or what I am,

LG1860	3009	O I perceive I have not understood anything—not a single object—and that no man ever can.
LG1860	3010	I perceive Nature here, in sight of the sea, is taking advantage of me, to dart upon me, and sting me,

LG1860	3013	You oceans both! You tangible land! Nature!
LG1860	3014	Be not too rough with me—I submit—I close with you,
LG1860	3015	These little shreds shall, indeed, stand for all.
LG1860	3016	You friable shore, with trails of debris!
LG1860	3017	You fish-shaped island! I take what is underfoot;
LG1860	3018	What is yours is mine, my father.
LG1860	3019	I too Paumanok,
LG1860	3020	I too have bubbled up, floated the measureless float, and been washed on your shores;

LG1860	3039	We, loose winrows, little corpses,
LG1860	3040	Froth, snowy white, and bubbles,

LG1860	3053	GREAT are the myths—I too delight in them,
LG1860	3054	Great are Adam and Eve—I too look back and accept them,

LG1860	3069	Great is Youth—equally great is Old Age—great are the Day and Night,

LG1860	3081	Great is the greatest Nation—the nation of clusters of equal nations.

LG1860	3085	Great is the quality of Truth in man,

LG1860	3091	O truth of the earth! O truth of things! I am de-termineddetermined to press my way toward you,

LG1860	3094	It is the fulness, color, form, diversity of the earth, and of men and women, and of all qualities and processes,
LG1860	3095	It is greater than wealth—it is greater than build-ingsbuildings, ships, religions, paintings, music.
LG1860	3096	Great is the English speech—what speech is so great as the English?
LG1860	3097	Great is the English brood—what brood has so vast a destiny as the English?
LG1860	3098	It is the mother of the brood that must rule the earth with the new rule,
LG1860	3099	The new rule shall rule as the Soul rules, and as the love, justice, equality in the Soul, rule.
LG1860	3100	Great is Law—great are the old few landmarks of the law,
LG1860	3101	They are the same in all times, and shall not be disturbed.
LG1860	3102	Great are commerce, newspapers, books, free-trade, railroads, steamers, international mails, tele-graphstelegraphs, exchanges.
LG1860	3103	Great is Justice!
LG1860	3104	Justice is not settled by legislators and laws—it is in the Soul,
LG1860	3105	It cannot be varied by statues, any more than love, pride, the attraction of gravity, can,
LG1860	3106	It is immutable—it does not depend on majorities— majorities or what not come at last before the same passionless and exact tribunal.
LG1860	3107	For justice are the grand natural lawyers and perfect judges—it is in their Souls,
LG1860	3108	It is well assorted—they have not studied for noth-ingnothing—the great includes the less,
LG1860	3109	They rule on the highest grounds—they oversee all eras, states, administrations.
LG1860	3110	The perfect judge fears nothing—he could go front to front before God,
LG1860	3111	Before the perfect judge all shall stand back—life and death shall stand back—heaven and hell shall stand back.
LG1860	3112	Great is Goodness!
LG1860	3113	I do not know what it is, any more than I know what health is—but I know it is great.
LG1860	3114	Great is Wickedness—I find I often admire it, just as much as I admire goodness,
LG1860	3115	Do you call that a paradox? It certainly is a paradox.
LG1860	3116	The eternal equilibrium of things is great, and the eternal overthrow of things is great,
LG1860	3117	And there is another paradox.
LG1860	3118	Great is Life, real and mystical, wherever and whoever,
LG1860	3119	Great is Death—sure as Life holds all parts together, Death holds all parts together,
LG1860	3120	Death has just as much purport as Life has,
LG1860	3121	Do you enjoy what Life confers? you shall enjoy what Death confers,
LG1860	3122	I do not understand the realities of Death, but I know they are great,
LG1860	3123	I do not understand the least reality of Life—how then can I understand the realities of Death?
LG1860	3124	A YOUNG man came to me with a message from his brother,
LG1860	3125	How should the young man know the whether and when of his brother?
LG1860	3126	Tell him to send me the signs.
LG1860	3127	And I stood before the young man face to face, and took his right hand in my left hand, and his left hand in my right hand,
LG1860	3128	And I answered for his brother, and for men, and I answered for THE POET, and sent these signs.
LG1860	3129	Him all wait for—him all yield up to—his word is decisive and final,
LG1860	3130	Him they accept, in him lave, in him perceive them-selvesthemselves, as amid light,
LG1860	3131	Him they immerse, and he immerses them.
LG1860	3132	Beautiful women, the haughtiest nations, laws, the landscape, people, animals,
LG1860	3133	The profound earth and its attributes, and the unquiet ocean,
LG1860	3134	All enjoyments and properties, and money, and what-everwhatever money will buy,
LG1860	3135	The best farms—others toiling and planting, and he unavoidably reaps,
LG1860	3136	The noblest and costliest cities—others grading and building, and he domiciles there,
LG1860	3137	Nothing for any one, but what is for him—near and far are for him,
LG1860	3138	The ships in the offing—the perpetual shows and marches on land, are for him, if they are for any body.
LG1860	3139	He puts things in their attitudes,
LG1860	3140	He puts to-day out of himself, with plasticity and love,
LG1860	3141	He places his own city, times, reminiscences, parents, brothers and sisters, associations, employment, politics, so that the rest never shame them after-wardafterward, nor assume to command them.
LG1860	3142	He is the answerer,
LG1860	3143	What can be answered he answers—and what cannot be answered, he shows how it cannot be answered.
LG1860	3144	A man is a summons and challenge;
LG1860	3145	(It is vain to skulk—Do you hear that mocking and laughter? Do you hear the ironical echoes?)
LG1860	3146	Books, friendships, philosophers, priests, action, pleas-urepleasure, pride, beat up and down, seeking to give satisfaction,
LG1860	3147	He indicates the satisfaction, and indicates them that beat up and down also.
LG1860	3148	Whichever the sex, whatever the season or place, he may go freshly and gently and safely, by day or by night,
LG1860	3149	He has the pass-key of hearts—to him the response of the prying of hands on the knobs.
LG1860	3150	His welcome is universal—the flow of beauty is not more welcome or universal than he is,
LG1860	3151	The person he favors by day or sleeps with at night is blessed.
LG1860	3152	Every existence has its idiom—everything has an idiom and tongue,
LG1860	3153	He resolves all tongues into his own, and bestows it upon men, and any man translates, and any man translates himself also,
LG1860	3154	One part does not counteract another part—he is the joiner—he sees how they join.
LG1860	3155	He says indifferently and alike, How are you, friend? to the President at his levee,
LG1860	3156	And he says, Good-day, my brother! to Cudge that hoes in the sugar-field,
LG1860	3157	And both understand him, and know that his speech is right.
LG1860	3158	He walks with perfect ease in the capitol,
LG1860	3159	He walks among the Congress, and one representative says to another, Here is our equal, appearing and new.
LG1860	3160	Then the mechanics take him for a mechanic,
LG1860	3161	And the soldiers suppose him to be a captain, and the sailors that he has followed the sea,
LG1860	3162	And the authors take him for an author, and the artists for an artist,
LG1860	3163	And the laborers perceive he could labor with them and love them,
LG1860	3164	No matter what the work is, that he is the one to fol-lowfollow it, or has followed it,
LG1860	3165	No matter what the nation, that he might find his brothers and sisters there.
LG1860	3166	The English believe he comes of their English stock,
LG1860	3167	A Jew to the Jew he seems—a Russ to the Russ— usual and near, removed from none.
LG1860	3168	Whoever he looks at in the traveller's coffee-house claims him,
LG1860	3169	The Italian or Frenchman is sure, and the German is sure, and the Spaniard is sure, and the island Cuban is sure;
LG1860	3170	The engineer, the deck-hand on the great lakes, or on the Mississippi, or St. Lawrence, or Sacramento, or Hudson, or Paumanok Sound, claims him.
LG1860	3171	The gentleman of perfect blood acknowledges his per-fectperfect blood,

LG1860	3173	They are not vile any more—they hardly know them-selvesthemselves, they are so grown.
LG1860	3174	Do you think it would be good to be the writer of melodious verses?

LG1860	3178	SOMETHING startles me where I thought I was safest,

LG1860	3183	O Earth!
LG1860	3184	O how can the ground of you not sicken?

LG1860	3209	What chemistry!
LG1860	3210	That the winds are really not infectious,

LG1860	3226	ALL day I have walked the city, and talked with my friends, and thought of prudence,

LG1860	3228	After all, the last explanation remains to be made about prudence,
LG1860	3229	Little and large alike drop quietly aside from the prudence that suits immortality.
LG1860	3230	The Soul is of itself,
LG1860	3231	All verges to it—all has reference to what ensues,
LG1860	3232	All that a person does, says, thinks, is of conse-quenceconsequence,
LG1860	3233	Not a move can a man or woman make, that affects him or her in a day, month, any part of the direct life-time, or the hour of death, but the same affects him or her onward afterward through the indirect life-time.
LG1860	3234	The indirect is more than the direct,
LG1860	3235	The spirit receives from the body just as much as it gives to the body, if not more.
LG1860	3236	Not one word or deed—not venereal sore, discolor-ationdiscoloration, privacy of the onanist, putridity of gluttons or rum-drinkers, peculation, cunning, betrayal, murder, seduction, prostitution, but has results beyond death, as really as before death.
LG1860	3237	Charity and personal force are the only investments worth anything.

LG1860	3240	Savage, felon, President, judge, farmer, sailor, me-chanicmechanic, young, old, it is the same,

LG1860	3242	Singly, wholly, to affect now, affected their time, will forever affect, all of the past, and all of the present, and all of the future,

LG1860	3258	Did you guess anything lived only its moment?
LG1860	3259	The world does not so exist—no parts palpable or impalpable so exist,
LG1860	3260	No consummation exists without being from some long previous consummation—and that from some other,
LG1860	3261	Without the farthest conceivable one coming a bit nearer the beginning than any.
LG1860	3262	Whatever satisfies Souls is true,
LG1860	3263	Prudence entirely satisfies the craving and glut of Souls,
LG1860	3264	Itself finally satisfies the Soul,
LG1860	3265	The Soul has that measureless pride which revolts from every lesson but its own.
LG1860	3266	Now I give you an inkling,
LG1860	3267	Now I breathe the word of the prudence that walks abreast with time, space, reality,
LG1860	3268	That answers the pride which refuses every lesson but its own.
LG1860	3269	What is prudence, is indivisible,
LG1860	3270	Declines to separate one part of life from every part,
LG1860	3271	Divides not the righteous from the unrighteous, or the living from the dead,
LG1860	3272	Matches every thought or act by its correlative,

LG1860	3280	PERFECT sanity shows the master among philosophs,

LG1860	3307	I NEED no assurances—I am a man who is pre-occupiedpreoccupied, of his own Soul;

LG1860	3323	WHAT shall I give? and which are my miracles?

LG1860	3358	THERE was a child went forth every day,

LG1860	3360	And that object became part of him for the day, or a certain part of the day, or for many years, or stretching cycles of years.

LG1860	3384	Affection that will not be gainsayed—the sense of what is real—the thought if, after all, it should prove unreal,
LG1860	3385	The doubts of day-time and the doubts of night-time— the curious whether and how,
LG1860	3386	Whether that which appears so is so, or is it all flashes and specks?
LG1860	3387	Men and women crowding fast in the streets—if they are not flashes and specks, what are they?
LG1860	3388	The streets themselves, and the façades of houses, and goods in the windows,
LG1860	3389	Vehicles, teams, the heavy-planked wharves—the huge crossing at the ferries,
LG1860	3390	The village on the highland, seen from afar at sunset— the river between,
LG1860	3391	Shadows, aureola and mist, light falling on roofs and gables of white or brown, three miles off,
LG1860	3392	The schooner near by, sleepily dropping down the tide—the little boat slack-towed astern,
LG1860	3393	The hurrying tumbling waves, quick-broken crests, slapping,
LG1860	3394	The strata of colored clouds, the long bar of maroon- tint, away solitary by itself—the spread of purity it lies motionless in,
LG1860	3395	The horizon's edge, the flying sea-crow, the fragrance of salt-marsh and shore-mud;
LG1860	3396	These became part of that child who went forth every day, and who now goes, and will always go forth every day,
LG1860	3397	And these become part of him or her that peruses them here.
LG1860	3398	IT is ended—I dally no more,
LG1860	3399	After to-day I inure myself to run, leap, swim, wrestle, fight,
LG1860	3400	To stand the cold or heat—to take good aim with a gun—to sail a boat—to manage horses—to beget superb children,
LG1860	3401	To speak readily and clearly—to feel at home among common people,
LG1860	3402	And to hold my own in terrible positions, on land and sea.
LG1860	3403	Not for an embroiderer,
LG1860	3404	(There will always be plenty of embroiderers—I welcome them also;)
LG1860	3405	But for the fibre of things, and for inherent men and women.
LG1860	3406	Not to chisel ornaments,
LG1860	3407	But to chisel with free stroke the heads and limbs of plenteous Supreme Gods, that The States may realize them, walking and talking.
LG1860	3408	Let me have my own way,
LG1860	3409	Let others promulge the laws—I will make no ac-countaccount of the laws,
LG1860	3410	Let others praise eminent men and hold up peace— I hold up agitation and conflict,
LG1860	3411	I praise no eminent man—I rebuke to his face the one that was thought most worthy.
LG1860	3412	(Who are you? you mean devil! And what are you secretly guilty of, all your life?
LG1860	3413	Will you turn aside all your life? Will you grub and chatter all your life?)
LG1860	3414	(And who are you—blabbing by rote, years, pages, languages, reminiscences,
LG1860	3415	Unwitting to-day that you do not know how to speak a single word?)
LG1860	3416	Let others finish specimens—I never finish specimens,
LG1860	3417	I shower them by exhaustless laws, as nature does, fresh and modern continually.
LG1860	3418	I give nothing as duties,
LG1860	3419	What others give as duties, I give as living impulses;
LG1860	3420	(Shall I give the heart's action as a duty?)
LG1860	3421	Let others dispose of questions—I dispose of noth-ingnothing—I arouse unanswerable questions;
LG1860	3422	Who are they I see and touch, and what about them?
LG1860	3423	What about these likes of myself, that draw me so close by tender directions and indirections?
LG1860	3424	Let others deny the evil their enemies charge against them—but how can I the like?
LG1860	3425	Nothing ever has been, or ever can be, charged against me, half as bad as the evil I really am;
LG1860	3426	I call to the world to distrust the accounts of my friends, but listen to my enemies—as I my-selfmyself do;
LG1860	3427	I charge you, too, forever, reject those who would expound me—for I cannot expound myself,
LG1860	3428	I charge that there be no theory or school founded out of me,
LG1860	3429	I charge you to leave all free, as I have left all free.
LG1860	3430	After me, vista!
LG1860	3431	O, I see life is not short, but immeasurably long,
LG1860	3432	I henceforth tread the world, chaste, temperate, an early riser, a gymnast, a steady grower,
LG1860	3433	Every hour the semen of centuries—and still of cen-turiescenturies.
LG1860	3434	I will follow up these continual lessons of the air, water, earth,
LG1860	3435	I perceive I have no time to lose.
LG1860	3436	WHO learns my lesson complete?
LG1860	3437	Boss, journeyman, apprentice—churchman and athe-istatheist,
LG1860	3438	The stupid and the wise thinker—parents and off-springoffspring—merchant, clerk, porter, and customer,
LG1860	3439	Editor, author, artist, and schoolboy—Draw nigh and commence;
LG1860	3440	It is no lesson — it lets down the bars to a good lesson,
LG1860	3441	And that to another, and every one to another still.
LG1860	3442	The great laws take and effuse without argument,
LG1860	3443	I am of the same style, for I am their friend,
LG1860	3444	I love them quits and quits—I do not halt and make salaams.
LG1860	3445	I lie abstracted, and hear beautiful tales of things, and the reasons of things,
LG1860	3446	They are so beautiful, I nudge myself to listen.
LG1860	3447	I cannot say to any person what I hear—I cannot say it to myself—it is very wonderful.
LG1860	3448	It is no small matter, this round and delicious globe, moving so exactly in its orbit forever and ever, without one jolt, or the untruth of a single second,
LG1860	3449	I do not think it was made in six days, nor in ten thousand years, nor ten billions of years,
LG1860	3450	Nor planned and built one thing after another, as an architect plans and builds a house.
LG1860	3451	I do not think seventy years is the time of a man or woman,
LG1860	3452	Nor that seventy millions of years is the time of a man or woman,
LG1860	3453	Nor that years will ever stop the existence of me, or any one else.
LG1860	3454	Is it wonderful that I should be immortal? as every one is immortal,
LG1860	3455	I know it is wonderful—but my eye-sight is equally wonderful, and how I was conceived in my moth-er'smother's womb is equally wonderful;
LG1860	3456	And how I was not palpable once, but am now—and was born on the last day of Fifth Month, in the Year 43 of America,
LG1860	3457	And passed from a babe, in the creeping trance of three summers and three winters, to articulate and walk—All this is equally wonderful.
LG1860	3458	And that I grew six feet high, and that I have become a man thirty-six years old in the Year 79 of America—and that I am here anyhow—are all equally wonderful.
LG1860	3459	And that my Soul embraces you this hour, and we af-fectaffect each other without ever seeing each other, and never perhaps to see each other, is every bit as wonderful.
LG1860	3460	And that I can think such thoughts as these, is just as wonderful,
LG1860	3461	And that I can remind you, and you think them and know them to be true, is just as wonderful.
LG1860	3462	And that the moon spins round the earth, and on with the earth, is equally wonderful,
LG1860	3463	And that they balance themselves with the sun and stars, is equally wonderful.
LG1860	3464	Come! I should like to hear you tell me what there is in yourself that is not just as wonderful,
LG1860	3465	And I should like to hear the name of anything be-tweenbetween First Day morning and Seventh Day night that is not just as wonderful.
LG1860	3466	THIS night I am happy;
LG1860	3467	As I walk the beach where the old mother sways to and fro, singing her savage and husky song,
LG1860	3468	As I watch the stars shining—I think a thought of the clef of the universes, and of the future.
LG1860	3469	What can the future bring me more than I have?
LG1860	3470	Do you suppose I wish to enjoy life in other spheres?
LG1860	3471	I say distinctly I comprehend no better sphere than this earth,
LG1860	3472	I comprehend no better life than the life of my body.
LG1860	3473	I do not know what follows the death of my body,
LG1860	3474	But I know well that whatever it is, it is best for me,
LG1860	3475	And I know well that whatever is really Me shall live just as much as before.
LG1860	3476	I am not uneasy but I shall have good housing to myself,
LG1860	3477	But this is my first—how can I like the rest any better?
LG1860	3478	Here I grew up—the studs and rafters are grown parts of me.
LG1860	3479	I am not uneasy but I am to be beloved by young and old men, and to love them the same,
LG1860	3480	I suppose the pink nipples of the breasts of women with whom I shall sleep will touch the side of my face the same,
LG1860	3481	But this is the nipple of a breast of my mother, always near and always divine to me, her true child and son, whatever comes.
LG1860	3482	I suppose I am to be eligible to visit the stars, in my time,
LG1860	3483	I suppose I shall have myriads of new experiences— and that the experience of this earth will prove only one out of myriads;
LG1860	3484	But I believe my body and my Soul already indicate those experiences,
LG1860	3485	And I believe I shall find nothing in the stars more majestic and beautiful than I have already found on the earth,
LG1860	3486	And I believe I have this night a clew through the universes,
LG1860	3487	And I believe I have this night thought a thought of the clef of eternity.
LG1860	3488	A VAST SIMILITUDE interlocks all,
LG1860	3489	All spheres, grown, ungrown, small, large, suns, moons, planets, comets, asteroids,
LG1860	3490	All the substances of the same, and all that is spiritual, upon the same,
LG1860	3491	All distances of place, however wide,
LG1860	3492	All distances of time—all inanimate forms,
LG1860	3493	All Souls—all living bodies, though they be ever so different, or in different worlds,
LG1860	3494	All gaseous, watery, vegetable, mineral processes— the fishes, the brutes,
LG1860	3495	All men and women—me also,
LG1860	3496	All nations, colors, barbarisms, civilizations, languages,

LG1860	3498	All lives and deaths—all of past, present, future,
LG1860	3499	This vast similitude spans them, and always has spanned, and shall forever span them, and compactly hold them.
LG1860	3500	O BITTER sprig! Confession sprig!
LG1860	3501	In the bouquet I give you place also—I bind you in,
LG1860	3502	Proceeding no further till, humbled publicly,
LG1860	3503	I give fair warning, once for all.
LG1860	3504	I own that I have been sly, thievish, mean, a prevari-catorprevaricator, greedy, derelict,

LG1860	3506	What foul thought but I think it—or have in me the stuff out of which it is thought?

LG1860	3514	O culpable! O traitor!

LG1860	3522	I feel I am of them—I belong to those convicts and prostitutes myself,
LG1860	3523	And henceforth I will not deny them—for how can I deny myself?
LG1860	3524	UNFOLDED out of the folds of the woman, man comes unfolded, as is always to come unfolded,
LG1860	3525	Unfolded only out of the superbest woman of the earth, is to come the superbest man of the earth,
LG1860	3526	Unfolded out of the friendliest woman, is to come the friendliest man,
LG1860	3527	Unfolded only out of the perfect body of a woman,can a man be formed of perfect body,

LG1860	3530	Unfolded by brawny embraces from the well-muscled woman I love, only thence come the brawny embraces of the man,

LG1860	3535	First the man is shaped in the woman, he can then be shaped in himself.
LG1860	3536	NIGHT on the Prairies;
LG1860	3537	I walk by myself—I stand and look at the stars, which I think now I never realized before.
LG1860	3538	Now I absorb immortality and peace,
LG1860	3539	I admire death and test propositions.
LG1860	3540	How plenteous! How spiritual! How resumé!
LG1860	3541	The same Old Man and Soul—the same old aspi-rationsaspirations, and the same content.
LG1860	3542	I was thinking the day most splendid, till I saw what the not-day exhibited,
LG1860	3543	I was thinking this globe enough, till there tumbled upon me myriads of other globes.
LG1860	3544	Now while the great thoughts of space and eternity fill me, I will measure myself by them,
LG1860	3545	And now, touched with the lives of other globes, arrived as far along as those of the earth,
LG1860	3546	Or waiting to arrive, or passed on farther than those of the earth,
LG1860	3547	I henceforth no more ignore them than I ignore my own life,
LG1860	3548	Or the lives on the earth arrived as far as mine, or waiting to arrive.
LG1860	3549	O how plainly I see now that life cannot exhibit all to me—as the day cannot,
LG1860	3550	O I see that I am to wait for what will be exhibited by death.
LG1860	3551	SEA-WATER, and all living below it,
LG1860	3552	Forests at the bottom of the sea—the branches and leaves,
LG1860	3553	Sea-lettuce, vast lichens, strange flowers and seeds—the thick tangle, the openings, and the pink turf,

LG1860	3561	The change onward from ours to that of beings who walk other spheres.
LG1860	3562	I SIT and look out upon all the sorrows of the world,and upon all oppression and shame,
LG1860	3563	I hear secret convulsive sobs from young men, at anguish with themselves, remorseful after deeds done;

LG1860	3572	O ME, man of slack faith so long!
LG1860	3573	Standing aloof—denying portions so long;
LG1860	3574	Me with mole's eyes, unrisen to buoyancy and vision —unfree,
LG1860	3575	Only aware to-day of compact, all-diffused truth,
LG1860	3576	Discovering to-day there is no lie, or form of lie, and can be none, but grows just as inevitably upon itself as the truth does upon itself,
LG1860	3577	Or as any law of the earth, or any natural production of the earth does.
LG1860	3578	(This is curious, and may not be realized immedi-atelyimmediately—But it must be realized;
LG1860	3579	I feel in myself that I represent falsehoods equally with the rest,
LG1860	3580	And that the universe does.)
LG1860	3581	Where has failed a perfect return, indifferent of lies or the truth?
LG1860	3582	Is it upon the ground, or in water or fire? or in the spirit of man? or in the meat and blood?
LG1860	3583	Meditating among liars, and retreating sternly into myself, I see that there are really no liars or lies after all,

LG1860	3590	FORMS, qualities, lives, humanity, language, thoughts,

LG1860	3656	What do you hear, Walt Whitman?

LG1860	3684	Who are they who salute, and that one after another salute you?
LG1860	3685	I see a great round wonder rolling through the air,
LG1860	3686	I see diminute farms, hamlets, ruins, grave-yards, jails,factories, palaces, hovels, huts of barbarians, tents of nomads, upon the surface,
LG1860	3687	I see the shaded part on one side, where the sleepers are sleeping—and the sun-lit part on the other side,
LG1860	3688	I see the curious silent change of the light and shade,
LG1860	3689	I see distant lands, as real and near to the inhabitants of them, as my land is to me.

LG1860	3877	(You will come forward in due time to my side.)
LG1860	3878	My spirit has passed in compassion and determination around the whole earth,
LG1860	3879	I have looked for equals and lovers, and found them ready for me in all lands;
LG1860	3880	I think some divine rapport has equalized me with them.
LG1860	3881	O vapors! I think I have risen with you, and moved away to distant continents, and fallen down there, for reasons,
LG1860	3882	I think I have blown with you, O winds,
LG1860	3883	O waters, I have fingered every shore with you.
LG1860	3884	I have run through what any river or strait of the globe has run through,
LG1860	3885	I have taken my stand on the bases of peninsulas, and on the highest embedded rocks, to cry thence.
LG1860	3886	Salut au Monde!
LG1860	3887	What cities the light or warmth penetrates, I pen-etratepenetrate those cities myself,
LG1860	3888	All islands to which birds wing their way, I wing my way myself.
LG1860	3889	Toward all,

LG1860	3891	To remain after me in sight forever,
LG1860	3892	For all the haunts and homes of men.
LG1860	3893	O TO make a most jubilant poem!
LG1860	3894	O full of music! Full of manhood, womanhood,infancy!
LG1860	3895	O full of common employments! Full of grain and trees.
LG1860	3896	O for the voices of animals! O for the swiftness and balance of fishes!
LG1860	3897	O for the dropping of rain-drops in a poem!
LG1860	3898	O for the sunshine and motion of waves in a poem.
LG1860	3899	O to be on the sea! the wind, the wide waters around;
LG1860	3900	O to sail in a ship under full sail at sea.
LG1860	3901	O the joy of my spirit! It is uncaged! It darts like lightning!
LG1860	3902	It is not enough to have this globe, or a certain time —I will have thousands of globes, and all time.
LG1860	3903	O the engineer's joys!
LG1860	3904	To go with a locomotive!
LG1860	3905	To hear the hiss of steam—the merry shriek—the steam-whistle—the laughing locomotive!
LG1860	3906	To push with resistless way, and speed off in the distance.
LG1860	3907	O the horseman's and horsewoman's joys!
LG1860	3908	The saddle—the gallop—the pressure upon the seat —the cool gurgling by the ears and hair.
LG1860	3909	O the fireman's joys!
LG1860	3910	I hear the alarm at dead of night,
LG1860	3911	I hear bells—shouts!—I pass the crowd—I run!
LG1860	3912	The sight of the flames maddens me with pleasure.
LG1860	3913	O the joy of the strong-brawned fighter, towering in the arena, in perfect condition, conscious of power, thirsting to meet his opponent.
LG1860	3914	O the joy of that vast elemental sympathy which only the human Soul is capable of generating and emitting in steady and limitless floods.
LG1860	3915	O the mother's joys!
LG1860	3916	The watching—the endurance—the precious love—the anguish—the patiently yielded life.
LG1860	3917	O the joy of increase, growth, recuperation,
LG1860	3918	The joy of soothing and pacifying—the joy of concord and harmony.
LG1860	3919	O to go back to the place where I was born!
LG1860	3920	O to hear the birds sing once more!
LG1860	3921	To ramble about the house and barn, and over the fields, once more,
LG1860	3922	And through the orchard and along the old lanes once more.
LG1860	3923	O male and female!
LG1860	3924	O the presence of women! (I swear, nothing is more exquisite to me than the presence of women;)
LG1860	3925	O for the girl, my mate! O for happiness with my mate!
LG1860	3926	O the young man as I pass! O I am sick after the friendship of him who, I fear, is indifferent to me.
LG1860	3927	O the streets of cities!
LG1860	3928	The flitting faces—the expressions, eyes, feet, cos-tumescostumes! O I cannot tell how welcome they are to me;
LG1860	3929	O of men—of women toward me as I pass—The memory of only one look—the boy lingering and waiting.
LG1860	3930	O to have been brought up on bays, lagoons, creeks,or along the coast!
LG1860	3931	O to continue and be employed there all my life!
LG1860	3932	O the briny and damp smell—the shore—the salt weeds exposed at low water,
LG1860	3933	The work of fishermen—the work of the eel-fisher and clam-fisher.
LG1860	3934	O it is I!
LG1860	3935	I come with my clam-rake and spade! I come with my eel-spear;
LG1860	3936	Is the tide out? I join the group of clam-diggers on the flats,
LG1860	3937	I laugh and work with them—I joke at my work,like a mettlesome young man.
LG1860	3938	In winter I take my eel-basket and eel-spear and travel out on foot on the ice—I have a small axe to cut holes in the ice;
LG1860	3939	Behold me, well-clothed, going gayly, or returning in the afternoon—my brood of tough boys accom-panyingaccompanying me,
LG1860	3940	My brood of grown and part-grown boys, who love to be with none else so well as they love to be with me,
LG1860	3941	By day to work with me, and by night to sleep with me.
LG1860	3942	Or, another time, in warm weather, out in a boat, to lift the lobster-pots, where they are sunk with heavy stones, (I know the buoys;)
LG1860	3943	O the sweetness of the Fifth Month morning upon the water, as I row, just before sunrise, toward the buoys;
LG1860	3944	I pull the wicker pots up slantingly—the dark green lobsters are desperate with their claws, as I take them out—I insert wooden pegs in the joints of their pincers,
LG1860	3945	I go to all the places, one after another, and then row back to the shore,
LG1860	3946	There, in a huge kettle of boiling water, the lobsters shall be boiled till their color becomes scarlet.
LG1860	3947	Or, another time, mackerel-taking,
LG1860	3948	Voracious, mad for the hook, near the surface, they seem to fill the water for miles;
LG1860	3949	Or, another time, fishing for rock-fish in Chesapeake Bay—I one of the brown-faced crew;
LG1860	3950	Or, another time, trailing for blue-fish off Paumanok,I stand with braced body,
LG1860	3951	My left foot is on the gunwale—my right arm throws the coils of slender rope,
LG1860	3952	In sight around me the quick veering and darting of fifty skiffs, my companions.
LG1860	3953	O boating on the rivers!
LG1860	3954	The voyage down the Niagara, (the St. Lawrence,)—the superb scenery—the steamers,
LG1860	3955	The ships sailing—the Thousand Islands—the occa-sionaloccasional timber-raft, and the raftsmen with long-reaching sweep-oars,
LG1860	3956	The little huts on the rafts, and the stream of smoke when they cook supper at evening.
LG1860	3957	O something pernicious and dread!
LG1860	3958	Something far away from a puny and pious life!
LG1860	3959	Something unproved! Something in a trance!
LG1860	3960	Something escaped from the anchorage, and driving free.
LG1860	3961	O to work in mines, or forging iron!
LG1860	3962	Foundry casting—the foundry itself—the rude high roof—the ample and shadowed space,
LG1860	3963	The furnace—the hot liquid poured out and running.
LG1860	3964	O the joys of the soldier!
LG1860	3965	To feel the presence of a brave general! to feel his sympathy!
LG1860	3966	To behold his calmness! to be warmed in the rays of his smile!
LG1860	3967	To go to battle! to hear the bugles play, and the drums beat!
LG1860	3968	To hear the artillery! to see the glittering of the bay-onetsbayonets and musket-barrels in the sun!
LG1860	3969	To see men fall and die and not complain!
LG1860	3970	To taste the savage taste of blood! to be so devilish!
LG1860	3971	To gloat so over the wounds and deaths of the enemy.
LG1860	3972	O the whaleman's joys! O I cruise my old cruise again!
LG1860	3973	I feel the ship's motion under me—I feel the Atlantic breezes fanning me,
LG1860	3974	I hear the cry again sent down from the mast-head, There she blows,
LG1860	3975	Again I spring up the rigging, to look with the rest—We see—we descend, wild with excitement,
LG1860	3976	I leap in the lowered boat—We row toward our prey,where he lies,
LG1860	3977	We approach, stealthy and silent—I see the moun-tainousmountainous mass, lethargic, basking,
LG1860	3978	I see the harpooner standing up—I see the weapon dart from his vigorous arm;
LG1860	3979	O swift, again, now, far out in the ocean, the wounded whale, settling, running to windward, tows me,
LG1860	3980	Again I see him rise to breathe—We row close again,
LG1860	3981	I see a lance driven through his side, pressed deep,turned in the wound,
LG1860	3982	Again we back off—I see him settle again—the life is leaving him fast,

LG1860	4075	ONCE, Paumanok,
LG1860	4076	When the snows had melted, and the Fifth Month grass was growing,
LG1860	4077	Up this sea-shore, in some briers,

LG1860	4087	Two together!

LG1860	4490	I knew a man,
LG1860	4491	He was a common farmer—he was the father of five sons,

LG1860	4649	I am for you, and you are for me, not only for our own sake, but for others' sakes;
LG1860	4650	Enveloped in you sleep greater heroes and bards,
LG1860	4651	They refuse to awake at the touch of any man but me.
LG1860	4652	It is I, you women—I make my way,
LG1860	4653	I am stern, acrid, large, undissuadable—but I love you,
LG1860	4654	I do not hurt you any more than is necessary for you,
LG1860	4655	I pour the stuff to start sons and daughters fit for These States—I press with slow rude muscle,
LG1860	4656	I brace myself effectually—I listen to no entreaties,
LG1860	4657	I dare not withdraw till I deposit what has so long accumulated within me.
LG1860	4658	Through you I drain the pent-up rivers of myself,
LG1860	4659	In you I wrap a thousand onward years,
LG1860	4660	On you I graft the grafts of the best-beloved of me and of America,
LG1860	4661	The drops I distil upon you shall grow fierce and athletic girls, new artists, musicians, and singers,
LG1860	4662	The babes I beget upon you are to beget babes in their turn,
LG1860	4663	I shall demand perfect men and women out of my love-spendings,
LG1860	4664	I shall expect them to interpenetrate with others, as I and you interpenetrate now,
LG1860	4665	I shall count on the fruits of the gushing showers of them, as I count on the fruits of the gushing showers I give now,
LG1860	4666	I shall look for loving crops from the birth, life, death, immortality, I plant so lovingly now.
LG1860	4667	SPONTANEOUS me, Nature,
LG1860	4668	The loving day, the friend I am happy with,
LG1860	4669	The arm of my friend hanging idly over my shoulder,
LG1860	4670	The hill-side whitened with blossoms of the mountain ash,
LG1860	4671	The same, late in autumn—the gorgeous hues of red,yellow, drab, purple, and light and dark green,

LG1860	4674	The real poems, (what we call poems being merely pictures,)
LG1860	4675	The poems of the privacy of the night, and of men like me,
LG1860	4676	This poem, drooping shy and unseen, that I always carry, and that all men carry,

LG1860	4682	Soft forenoon airs that blow from the south-west,

LG1860	4693	The limpid liquid within the young man,
LG1860	4694	The vexed corrosion, so pensive and so painful,
LG1860	4695	The torment—the irritable tide that will not be at rest,
LG1860	4696	The like of the same I feel—the like of the same in others,
LG1860	4697	The young woman that flushes and flushes, and the young man that flushes and flushes,

LG1860	4700	The souse upon me of my lover the sea, as I lie willing and naked,

LG1860	4704	The consequent meanness of me should I skulk or find myself indecent, while birds and animals never once skulk or find themselves indecent,
LG1860	4705	The great chastity of paternity, to match the great chastity of maternity,

LG1860	4707	The greed that eats me day and night with hungry gnaw, till I saturate what shall produce boys to fill my place when I am through,
LG1860	4708	The wholesome relief, repose, content,
LG1860	4709	And this bunch plucked at random from myself,
LG1860	4710	It has done its work—I toss it carelessly to fall where it may.
LG1860	4711	O FURIOUS! O confine me not!
LG1860	4712	(What is this that frees me so in storms?
LG1860	4713	What do my shouts amid lightnings and raging winds mean?)
LG1860	4714	O to drink the mystic deliria deeper than any other man!
LG1860	4715	O savage and tender achings!
LG1860	4716	(I bequeath them to you, my children,
LG1860	4717	I tell them to you, for reasons, O bridegroom and bride.)
LG1860	4718	O to be yielded to you, whoever you are, and you to be yielded me, in defiance of the world!
LG1860	4719	(Know, I am a man, attracting, at any time, her I but look upon, or touch with the tips of my fingers,
LG1860	4720	Or that touches my face, or leans against me.)
LG1860	4721	O to return to Paradise!
LG1860	4722	O to draw you to me—to plant on you, for the first time, the lips of a determined man!
LG1860	4723	O rich and feminine! O to show you to realize the blood of life for yourself, whoever you are—and no matter when and where you live.
LG1860	4724	O the puzzle—the thrice-tied knot—the deep and dark pool! O all untied and illumined!
LG1860	4725	O to speed where there is space enough and air enough at last!
LG1860	4726	O to be absolved from previous follies and degrada-tionsdegradations—I from mine, and you from yours!

LG1860	4731	O madness amorous! O trembling!
LG1860	4732	O to escape utterly from others' anchors and holds!

LG1860	4735	To ascend—to leap to the heavens of the love indicated to me!
LG1860	4736	To rise thither with my inebriate Soul!
LG1860	4737	To be lost, if it must be so!
LG1860	4738	To feed the remainder of life with one hour of ful-nessfulness and freedom!

LG1860	4740	YOU and I—what the earth is, we are,
LG1860	4741	We two—how long we were fooled!
LG1860	4742	Now delicious, transmuted, swiftly we escape, as Nature escapes,
LG1860	4743	We are Nature—long have we been absent, but now we return,
LG1860	4744	We become plants, leaves, foliage, roots, bark,
LG1860	4745	We are bedded in the ground—we are rocks,

LG1860	4748	We are two fishes swimming in the sea together,

LG1860	4753	We are two predatory hawks—we soar above and look down,

LG1860	4760	We have circled and circled till we have arrived home again—we two have,
LG1860	4761	We have voided all but freedom, and all but our own joy.
LG1860	4762	NATIVE moments! when you come upon me—Ah you are here now!
LG1860	4763	Give me now libidinous joys only!
LG1860	4764	Give me the drench of my passions! Give me life coarse and rank!
LG1860	4765	To-day, I go consort with nature's darlings—to-night too,

LG1860	4782	INQUIRING, tireless, seeking that yet unfound,

LG1860	4791	IN the new garden, in all the parts,

LG1860	4800	AGES and ages, returning at intervals,
LG1860	4801	Undestroyed, wandering immortal,

LG1860	4816	EARLY in the morning,

LG1860	4835	You road I travel and look around! I believe you are not all that is here,

LG1860	4862	The picture alive, every part in its best light,

LG1860	4870	You shall be more to me than my poem.
LG1860	4871	I think heroic deeds were all conceived in the open air,
LG1860	4872	I think I could stop here myself, and do miracles,

LG1860	4875	From this hour, freedom!
LG1860	4876	From this hour I ordain myself loosed of limits and imaginary lines,

LG1860	4881	I inhale great draughts of air,
LG1860	4882	The east and the west are mine, and the north and the south are mine.
LG1860	4883	I am larger than I thought,
LG1860	4884	I did not know I held so much goodness.
LG1860	4885	All seems beautiful to me,
LG1860	4886	I can repeat over to men and women, You have done such good to me, I would do the same to you.
LG1860	4887	I will recruit for myself and you as I go,
LG1860	4888	I will scatter myself among men and women as I go,
LG1860	4889	I will toss the new gladness and roughness among them;
LG1860	4890	Whoever denies me, it shall not trouble me,
LG1860	4891	Whoever accepts me, he or she shall be blessed, and shall bless me.
LG1860	4892	Now if a thousand perfect men were to appear, it would not amaze me,

LG1860	4894	Now I see the secret of the making of the best persons,
LG1860	4895	It is to grow in the open air, and to eat and sleep with the earth.
LG1860	4896	Here is space—here a great personal deed has room,
LG1860	4897	A great deed seizes upon the hearts of the whole race of men,
LG1860	4898	Its effusion of strength and will overwhelms law, and mocks all authority and all argument against it.
LG1860	4899	Here is the test of wisdom,
LG1860	4900	Wisdom is not finally tested in schools,
LG1860	4901	Wisdom cannot be passed from one having it, to an-otheranother not having it,
LG1860	4902	Wisdom is of the Soul, is not susceptible of proof, is its own proof,
LG1860	4903	Applies to all stages and objects and qualities, and is content,
LG1860	4904	Is the certainty of the reality and immortality of things, and the excellence of things;

LG1860	4906	Now I reëxamine philosophies and religions,
LG1860	4907	They may prove well in lecture-rooms, yet not prove at all under the spacious clouds, and along the landscape and flowing currents.
LG1860	4908	Here is realization,
LG1860	4909	Here is a man tallied—he realizes here what he has in him,
LG1860	4910	The animals, the past, the future, light, space,majesty, love, if they are vacant of you, you are vacant of them.
LG1860	4911	Only the kernel of every object nourishes;
LG1860	4912	Where is he who tears off the husks for you and me?
LG1860	4913	Where is he that undoes stratagems and envelopes for you and me?
LG1860	4914	Here is adhesiveness—it is not previously fashioned —it is apropos;
LG1860	4915	Do you know what it is, as you pass, to be loved by strangers?
LG1860	4916	Do you know the talk of those turning eye-balls?
LG1860	4917	Here is the efflux of the Soul,
LG1860	4918	The efflux of the Soul comes through beautiful gates of laws, provoking questions;
LG1860	4919	These yearnings, why are they? These thoughts in the darkness, why are they?
LG1860	4920	Why are there men and women that while they are nigh me, the sun-light expands my blood?
LG1860	4921	Why, when they leave me, do my pennants of joy sink flat and lank?

LG1860	4925	What with some driver, as I ride on the seat by his side?

LG1860	4929	I think it pervades the air, waiting at all times,
LG1860	4930	Now it flows into us—we are rightly charged.
LG1860	4931	Here rises the fluid and attaching character;
LG1860	4932	The fluid and attaching character is the freshness and sweetness of man and woman,
LG1860	4933	The herbs of the morning sprout no fresher and sweeter every day out of the roots of them-selvesthemselves, than it sprouts fresh and sweet contin-uallycontinually out of itself.

LG1860	4936	Toward it heaves the shuddering longing ache of contact.
LG1860	4937	Allons! Whoever you are, come travel with me!
LG1860	4938	Travelling with me, you find what never tires.
LG1860	4939	The earth never tires,
LG1860	4940	The earth is rude, silent, incomprehensible at first—Nature is rude and incomprehensible at first;
LG1860	4941	Be not discouraged—keep on—there are divine things, well enveloped,
LG1860	4942	I swear to you there are divine things more beautiful than words can tell.
LG1860	4943	Allons! We must not stop here!
LG1860	4944	However sweet these laid-up stores—however con-venientconvenient this dwelling, we cannot remain here,

LG1860	4947	Allons! The inducements shall be great to you;
LG1860	4948	We will sail pathless and wild seas;
LG1860	4949	We will go where winds blow, waves dash, and the Yankee clipper speeds by under full sail.
LG1860	4950	Allons! With power, liberty, the earth, the elements!
LG1860	4951	Health, defiance, gayety, self-esteem, curiosity;
LG1860	4952	Allons! from all formules!
LG1860	4953	From your formules, O bat-eyed and materialistic priests!
LG1860	4954	The stale cadaver blocks up the passage—the burial waits no longer.
LG1860	4955	Allons! Yet take warning!
LG1860	4956	He travelling with me needs the best blood, thews,endurance,
LG1860	4957	None may come to the trial, till he or she bring courage and health.
LG1860	4958	Come not here if you have already spent the best of yourself;
LG1860	4959	Only those may come, who come in sweet and deter-mineddetermined bodies,

LG1860	4961	I and mine do not convince by arguments, similes,rhymes,

LG1860	4963	Listen! I will be honest with you,
LG1860	4964	I do not offer the old smooth prizes, but offer rough new prizes,
LG1860	4965	These are the days that must happen to you:
LG1860	4966	You shall not heap up what is called riches,

LG1860	5248	SCENTED herbage of my breast,
LG1860	5249	Leaves from you I yield, I write, to be perused best afterwards,

LG1860	5255	O burning and throbbing—surely all will one day be accomplished;

LG1860	5288	WHOEVER you are holding me now in hand,
LG1860	5289	Without one thing all will be useless,
LG1860	5290	I give you fair warning, before you attempt me further,

LG1860	5303	And in libraries I lie as one dumb, a gawk, or unborn, or dead,)

LG1860	5313	And thus, touching you, would I silently sleep and be carried eternally.

LG1860	5317	Even while you should think you had unquestionably caught me, behold!
LG1860	5318	Already you see I have escaped from you.
LG1860	5319	For it is not for what I have put into it that I have written this book,

LG1860	5321	Nor do those know me best who admire me, and vauntingly praise me,

LG1860	5326	THESE I, singing in spring, collect for lovers,
LG1860	5327	(For who but I should understand lovers, and all their sorrow and joy?
LG1860	5328	And who but I should be the poet of comrades?)

LG1860	5354	STATES!
LG1860	5355	Were you looking to be held together by the lawyers?

LG1860	5362	O mother! have you done much for me?

LG1860	5396	NOT heaving from my ribbed breast only,
LG1860	5397	Not in sighs at night, in rage, dissatisfied with myself,

LG1860	5413	OF the terrible question of appearances,

LG1860	5441	HOURS continuing long, sore and heavy-hearted,

LG1860	5464	WHEN I heard at the close of the day how my name had been received with plaudits in the capitol,still it was not a happy night for me that fol-lowedfollowed;

LG1860	5522	WHO is now reading this?
LG1860	5523	May-be one is now reading this who knows some wrong-doing of my past life,

LG1860	5563	I SAW in Louisiana a live-oak growing,
LG1860	5564	All alone stood it, and the moss hung down from the branches,
LG1860	5565	Without any companion it grew there, uttering joyous leaves of dark green,
LG1860	5566	And its look, rude, unbending, lusty, made me think of myself,
LG1860	5567	But I wondered how it could utter joyous leaves,standing alone there, without its friend, its lover near—for I knew I could not,

LG1860	5575	I know very well I could not.
LG1860	5576	MUSIC always round me, unceasing, unbeginning—yet long untaught I did not hear,
LG1860	5577	But now the chorus I hear, and am elated,
LG1860	5578	A tenor, strong, ascending, with power and health,with glad notes of day-break I hear,
LG1860	5579	A soprano, at intervals, sailing buoyantly over the tops of immense waves,
LG1860	5580	A transparent base, shuddering lusciously under and through the universe,
LG1860	5581	The triumphant tutti—the funeral wailings, with sweet flutes and violins—All these I fill myself with;
LG1860	5582	I hear not the volumes of sound merely—I am moved by the exquisite meanings,
LG1860	5583	I listen to the different voices winding in and out,striving, contending with fiery vehemence to excel each other in emotion,
LG1860	5584	I do not think the performers know themselves—But now I think I begin to know them.
LG1860	5585	PASSING stranger! you do not know how longingly I look upon you,
LG1860	5586	You must be he I was seeking, or she I was seeking,(It comes to me, as of a dream,)
LG1860	5587	I have somewhere surely lived a life of joy with you,
LG1860	5588	All is recalled as we flit by each other, fluid, affec-tionateaffectionate, chaste, matured,
LG1860	5589	You grew up with me, were a boy with me, or a girl with me,
LG1860	5590	I ate with you, and slept with you—your body has become not yours only, nor left my body mine only,
LG1860	5591	You give me the pleasure of your eyes, face, flesh, as we pass—you take of my beard, breast, hands,in return,
LG1860	5592	I am not to speak to you—I am to think of you when I sit alone, or wake at night alone,
LG1860	5593	I am to wait—I do not doubt I am to meet you again,
LG1860	5594	I am to see to it that I do not lose you.
LG1860	5595	THIS moment as I sit alone, yearning and thoughtful,it seems to me there are other men in other lands, yearning and thoughtful;
LG1860	5596	It seems to me I can look over and behold them,in Germany, Italy, France, Spain—Or far, far away, in China, or in Russia or India—talking other dialects;
LG1860	5597	And it seems to me if I could know those men better,I should become attached to them, as I do to men in my own lands,
LG1860	5598	It seems to me they are as wise, beautiful, benevolent,as any in my own lands;
LG1860	5599	O I know we should be brethren and lovers,
LG1860	5600	I know I should be happy with them.
LG1860	5601	I HEAR it is charged against me that I seek to destroy institutions;
LG1860	5602	But really I am neither for nor against institutions,
LG1860	5603	(What indeed have I in common with them?—Or what with the destruction of them?)
LG1860	5604	Only I will establish in the Mannahatta, and in every city of These States, inland and seaboard,
LG1860	5605	And in the fields and woods, and above every keel little or large, that dents the water,
LG1860	5606	Without edifices, or rules, or trustees, or any ar-gumentargument,
LG1860	5607	The institution of the dear love of comrades.
LG1860	5608	THE prairie-grass dividing—its own odor breathing,
LG1860	5609	I demand of it the spiritual corresponding,
LG1860	5610	Demand the most copious and close companionship of men,
LG1860	5611	Demand the blades to rise of words, acts, beings,
LG1860	5612	Those of the open atmosphere, coarse, sunlit, fresh,nutritious,
LG1860	5613	Those that go their own gait, erect, stepping with freedom and command—leading, not following,
LG1860	5614	Those with a never-quell'd audacity—those with sweet and lusty flesh, clear of taint, choice and chary of its love-power,
LG1860	5615	Those that look carelessly in the faces of Presidents and Governors, as to say, Who are you?
LG1860	5616	Those of earth-born passion, simple, never constrained,never obedient,
LG1860	5617	Those of inland America.
LG1860	5618	WE two boys together clinging,
LG1860	5619	One the other never leaving,
LG1860	5620	Up and down the roads going—North and South excursions making,
LG1860	5621	Power enjoying—elbows stretching—fingers clutch-ingclutching,
LG1860	5622	Armed and fearless—eating, drinking, sleeping, lov-ingloving,
LG1860	5623	No law less than ourselves owning—sailing, soldier-ingsoldiering, thieving, threatening,
LG1860	5624	Misers, menials, priests alarming—air breathing,water drinking, on the turf or the sea-beach dancing,
LG1860	5625	With birds singing—With fishes swimming—With trees branching and leafing,
LG1860	5626	Cities wrenching, ease scorning, statutes mocking,feebleness chasing,
LG1860	5627	Fulfilling our foray.
LG1860	5628	O LOVE!
LG1860	5629	O dying—always dying!
LG1860	5630	O the burials of me, past and present!
LG1860	5631	O me, while I stride ahead, material, visible, imperi-ousimperious as ever!
LG1860	5632	O me, what I was for years, now dead, (I lament not —I am content;),
LG1860	5633	O to disengage myself from those corpses of me,which I turn and look at, where I cast them!
LG1860	5634	To pass on, (O living! always living!) and leave the corpses behind!
LG1860	5635	WHEN I peruse the conquered fame of heroes, and the victories of mighty generals, I do not envy the generals,
LG1860	5636	Nor the President in his Presidency, nor the rich in his great house;
LG1860	5637	But when I read of the brotherhood of lovers, how it was with them,
LG1860	5638	How through life, through dangers, odium, un-changingunchanging, long and long,
LG1860	5639	Through youth, and through middle and old age, how unfaltering, how affectionate and faithful they were,
LG1860	5640	Then I am pensive—I hastily put down the book,and walk away, filled with the bitterest envy.
LG1860	5641	ONE flitting glimpse, caught through an interstice,
LG1860	5642	Of a crowd of workmen and drivers in a bar-room,around the stove, late of a winter night—And I unremarked, seated in a corner;
LG1860	5643	Of a youth who loves me, and whom I love, silently approaching, and seating himself near, that he may hold me by the hand;
LG1860	5644	A long while, amid the noises of coming and going —of drinking and oath and smutty jest,
LG1860	5645	There we two, content, happy in being together,speaking little, perhaps not a word.
LG1860	5646	A PROMISE and gift to California,
LG1860	5647	Also to the great Pastoral Plains, and for Oregon:
LG1860	5648	Sojourning east a while longer, soon I travel to you,to remain, to teach robust American love;
LG1860	5649	For I know very well that I and robust love belong among you, inland, and along the Western Sea,
LG1860	5650	For These States tend inland, and toward the Western Sea—and I will also.
LG1860	5651	WHAT ship, puzzled at sea, cons for the true reck-oningreckoning?
LG1860	5652	Or, coming in, to avoid the bars, and follow the chan-nelchannel, a perfect pilot needs?
LG1860	5653	Here, sailor! Here, ship! take aboard the most per-fectperfect pilot,
LG1860	5654	Whom, in a little boat, putting off, and rowing, I, hailing you, offer.
LG1860	5655	What place is besieged, and vainly tries to raise the siege?
LG1860	5656	Lo! I send to that place a commander, swift, brave, immortal,
LG1860	5657	And with him horse and foot—and parks of artillery,
LG1860	5658	And artillerymen, the deadliest that ever fired gun.
LG1860	5659	WHAT think you I take my pen in hand to record?
LG1860	5660	The battle-ship, perfect-model'd, majestic, that I saw pass the offing to-day under full sail?
LG1860	5661	The splendors of the past day? Or the splendor of the night that envelops me?
LG1860	5662	Or the vaunted glory and growth of the great city spread around me?—No;
LG1860	5663	But I record of two simple men I saw to-day, on the pier, in the midst of the crowd, parting the part-ingparting of dear friends,
LG1860	5664	The one to remain hung on the other's neck, and pas-sionatelypassionately kissed him,
LG1860	5665	While the one to depart, tightly prest the one to remain in his arms.
LG1860	5666	NO labor-saving machine,
LG1860	5667	Nor discovery have I made,
LG1860	5668	Nor will I be able to leave behind me any wealthy bequest to found a hospital or library,
LG1860	5669	Nor reminiscence of any deed of courage, for America,
LG1860	5670	Nor literary success, nor intellect—nor book for the book-shelf;
LG1860	5671	Only these carols, vibrating through the air, I leave,
LG1860	5672	For comrades and lovers.
LG1860	5673	I DREAMED in a dream, I saw a city invincible to the attacks of the whole of the rest of the earth,
LG1860	5674	I dreamed that was the new City of Friends,
LG1860	5675	Nothing was greater there than the quality of robust love—it led the rest,
LG1860	5676	It was seen every hour in the actions of the men of that city,
LG1860	5677	And in all their looks and words.
LG1860	5678	TO you of New England,
LG1860	5679	To the man of the Seaside State, and of Pennsylvania,
LG1860	5680	To the Kanadian of the north—to the Southerner I love,
LG1860	5681	These, with perfect trust, to depict you as myself—the germs are in all men;
LG1860	5682	I believe the main purport of These States is to found a superb friendship, exalté, previously unknown,
LG1860	5683	Because I perceive it waits, and has been always wait-ingwaiting, latent in all men.
LG1860	5684	EARTH! my likeness!
LG1860	5685	Though you look so impassive, ample and spheric there,
LG1860	5686	I now suspect that is not all;
LG1860	5687	I now suspect there is something fierce in you, eligible to burst forth;
LG1860	5688	For an athlete is enamoured of me—and I of him,
LG1860	5689	But toward him there is something fierce and terrible in me, eligible to burst forth,
LG1860	5690	I dare not tell it in words—not even in these songs.
LG1860	5691	A LEAF for hand in hand!
LG1860	5692	You natural persons old and young! You on the Eastern Sea, and you on the Western!
LG1860	5693	You on the Mississippi, and on all the branches and bayous of the Mississippi!
LG1860	5694	You friendly boatmen and mechanics! You roughs!
LG1860	5695	You twain! And all processions moving along the streets!
LG1860	5696	I wish to infuse myself among you till I see it com-moncommon for you to walk hand in hand.
LG1860	5697	PRIMEVAL my love for the woman I love,
LG1860	5698	O bride ! O wife ! more resistless, more enduring than I can tell, the thought of you !
LG1860	5699	Then separate, as disembodied, the purest born,
LG1860	5700	The ethereal, the last athletic reality, my consolation,
LG1860	5701	I ascend—I float in the regions of your love, O man,
LG1860	5702	O sharer of my roving life.
LG1860	5703	SOMETIMES with one I love, I fill myself with rage, for fear I effuse unreturned love;
LG1860	5704	But now I think there is no unreturned love—the pay is certain, one way or another,
LG1860	5705	Doubtless I could not have perceived the universe,or written one of my poems, if I had not freely given myself to comrades, to love.
LG1860	5706	THAT shadow, my likeness, that goes to and fro, seek-ingseeking a livelihood, chattering, chaffering,
LG1860	5707	How often I find myself standing and looking at it where it flits,
LG1860	5708	How often I question and doubt whether that is really me;
LG1860	5709	But in these, and among my lovers, and carolling my songs,
LG1860	5710	O I never doubt whether that is really me.
LG1860	5711	AMONG the men and women, the multitude, I per-ceiveperceive one picking me out by secret and divine signs,
LG1860	5712	Acknowledging none else—not parent, wife, hus-bandhusband, brother, child, any nearer than I am;
LG1860	5713	Some are baffled—But that one is not—that one knows me.
LG1860	5714	Lover and perfect equal!
LG1860	5715	I meant that you should discover me so, by my faint indirections,
LG1860	5716	And I, when I meet you, mean to discover you by the like in you.
LG1860	5717	TO the young man, many things to absorb, to engraft,to develop, I teach, to help him become élève of mine,
LG1860	5718	But if blood like mine circle not in his veins,
LG1860	5719	If he be not silently selected by lovers, and do not silently select lovers,
LG1860	5720	Of what use is it that he seek to become élève of mine?
LG1860	5721	O YOU whom I often and silently come where you are, that I may be with you,
LG1860	5722	As I walk by your side, or sit near, or remain in the same room with you,
LG1860	5723	Little you know the subtle electric fire that for your sake is playing within me.
LG1860	5724	HERE my last words, and the most baffling,
LG1860	5725	Here the frailest leaves of me, and yet my strongest-lasting,
LG1860	5726	Here I shade down and hide my thoughts—I do not expose them,
LG1860	5727	And yet they expose me more than all my other poems.
LG1860	5728	FULL of life, sweet-blooded, compact, visible,
LG1860	5729	I, forty years old the Eighty-third Year of The States,
LG1860	5730	To one a century hence, or any number of centuries hence,
LG1860	5731	To you, yet unborn, these, seeking you.
LG1860	5732	When you read these, I, that was visible, am become invisible;
LG1860	5733	Now it is you, compact, visible, realizing my poems, seeking me,
LG1860	5734	Fancying how happy you were, if I could be with you, and become your lover;
LG1860	5735	Be it as if I were with you. Be not too certain but I am now with you.
LG1860	5736	FLOOD-TIDE below me! I watch you, face to face;
LG1860	5737	Clouds of the west! sun there half an hour high! I see you also face to face.
LG1860	5738	Crowds of men and women attired in the usual cos-tumescostumes! how curious you are to me!
LG1860	5739	On the ferry-boats, the hundreds and hundreds that cross, returning home, are more curious to me than you suppose,
LG1860	5740	And you that shall cross from shore to shore years hence, are more to me, and more in my med-itationsmeditations, than you might suppose.
LG1860	5741	The impalpable sustenance of me from all things, at all hours of the day,
LG1860	5742	The simple, compact, well-joined scheme—myself disintegrated, every one disintegrated, yet part of the scheme,
LG1860	5743	The similitudes of the past, and those of the future,
LG1860	5744	The glories strung like beads on my smallest sights and hearings—on the walk in the street, and the passage over the river,
LG1860	5745	The current rushing so swiftly, and swimming with me far away,
LG1860	5746	The others that are to follow me, the ties between me and them,
LG1860	5747	The certainty of others—the life, love, sight, hear-inghearing of others.
LG1860	5748	Others will enter the gates of the ferry, and cross from shore to shore,
LG1860	5749	Others will watch the run of the flood-tide,
LG1860	5750	Others will see the shipping of Manhattan north and west, and the heights of Brooklyn to the south and east,
LG1860	5751	Others will see the islands large and small,
LG1860	5752	Fifty years hence, others will see them as they cross,the sun half an hour high,
LG1860	5753	A hundred years hence, or ever so many hundred years hence, others will see them,
LG1860	5754	Will enjoy the sunset, the pouring in of the flood-tide, the falling back to the sea of the ebb-tide.
LG1860	5755	It avails not, neither time or place—distance avails not,
LG1860	5756	I am with you, you men and women of a generation,or ever so many generations hence,
LG1860	5757	I project myself—also I return—I am with you, and know how it is.
LG1860	5758	Just as you feel when you look on the river and sky,so I felt,
LG1860	5759	Just as any of you is one of a living crowd, I was one of a crowd,
LG1860	5760	Just as you are refreshed by the gladness of the river,and the bright flow, I was refreshed,
LG1860	5761	Just as you stand and lean on the rail, yet hurry with the swift current, I stood, yet was hurried,
LG1860	5762	Just as you look on the numberless masts of ships,and the thick-stemmed pipes of steamboats, I looked.
LG1860	5763	I too many and many a time crossed the river, the sun half an hour high,
LG1860	5764	I watched the Twelfth Month sea-gulls—I saw them high in the air, floating with motionless wings,oscillating their bodies,
LG1860	5765	I saw how the glistening yellow lit up parts of their bodies, and left the rest in strong shadow,
LG1860	5766	I saw the slow-wheeling circles, and the gradual edging toward the south.
LG1860	5767	I too saw the reflection of the summer sky in the water,
LG1860	5768	Had my eyes dazzled by the shimmering track of beams,
LG1860	5769	Looked at the fine centrifugal spokes of light round the shape of my head in the sun-lit water,
LG1860	5770	Looked on the haze on the hills southward and south-westward,
LG1860	5771	Looked on the vapor as it flew in fleeces tinged with violet,
LG1860	5772	Looked toward the lower bay to notice the arriving ships,
LG1860	5773	Saw their approach, saw aboard those that were near me,
LG1860	5774	Saw the white sails of schooners and sloops, saw the ships at anchor,
LG1860	5775	The sailors at work in the rigging, or out astride the spars,
LG1860	5776	The round masts, the swinging motion of the hulls,the slender serpentine pennants,
LG1860	5777	The large and small steamers in motion, the pilots in their pilot-houses,
LG1860	5778	The white wake left by the passage, the quick trem-uloustremulous whirl of the wheels,
LG1860	5779	The flags of all nations, the falling of them at sun-set,
LG1860	5780	The scallop-edged waves in the twilight, the ladled cups, the frolicsome crests and glistening,
LG1860	5781	The stretch afar growing dimmer and dimmer, the gray walls of the granite store-houses by the docks,
LG1860	5782	On the river the shadowy group, the big steam-tug closely flanked on each side by the barges—the hay-boat, the belated lighter,
LG1860	5783	On the neighboring shore, the fires from the foundry chimneys burning high and glaringly into the night,
LG1860	5784	Casting, their flicker of black, contrasted with wild red and yellow light, over the tops of houses,and down into the clefts of streets.
LG1860	5785	These, and all else, were to me the same as they are to you,
LG1860	5786	I project myself a moment to tell you—also I return.
LG1860	5787	I loved well those cities,
LG1860	5788	I loved well the stately and rapid river,
LG1860	5789	The men and women I saw were all near to me,
LG1860	5790	Others the same—others who look back on me,because I looked forward to them,
LG1860	5791	(The time will come, though I stop here to-day and to-night.)
LG1860	5792	What is it, then, between us?
LG1860	5793	What is the count of the scores or hundreds of years between us?
LG1860	5794	Whatever it is, it avails not—distance avails not, and place avails not.
LG1860	5795	I too lived, (I was of old Brooklyn,)
LG1860	5796	I too walked the streets of Manhattan Island, and bathed in the waters around it,
LG1860	5797	I too felt the curious abrupt questionings stir within me,
LG1860	5798	In the day, among crowds of people, sometimes they came upon me,
LG1860	5799	In my walks home late at night, or as I lay in my bed, they came upon me.
LG1860	5800	I too had been struck from the float forever held in solution,
LG1860	5801	I too had received identity by my body,
LG1860	5802	That I was, I knew was of my body—and what I should be, I knew I should be of my body.
LG1860	5803	It is not upon you alone the dark patches fall,
LG1860	5804	The dark threw patches down upon me also,
LG1860	5805	The best I had done seemed to me blank and sus-picioussuspicious,
LG1860	5806	My great thoughts, as I supposed them, were they not in reality meagre? would not people laugh at me?
LG1860	5807	It is not you alone who know what it is to be evil,
LG1860	5808	I am he who knew what it was to be evil,
LG1860	5809	I too knitted the old knot of contrariety,
LG1860	5810	Blabbed, blushed, resented, lied, stole, grudged,
LG1860	5811	Had guile, anger, lust, hot wishes I dared not speak,
LG1860	5812	Was wayward, vain, greedy, shallow, sly, cowardly,malignant,
LG1860	5813	The wolf, the snake, the hog, not wanting in me,
LG1860	5814	The cheating look, the frivolous word, the adulterous wish, not wanting,
LG1860	5815	Refusals, hates, postponements, meanness, laziness,none of these wanting.
LG1860	5816	But I was a Manhattanese, free, friendly, and proud
LG1860	5817	I was called by my nighest name by clear loud voices of young men as they saw me approaching or passing,
LG1860	5818	Felt their arms on my neck as I stood, or the neg-ligentnegligent leaning of their flesh against me as I sat,

LG1860	5820	Lived the same life with the rest, the same old laugh-inglaughing, gnawing, sleeping,
LG1860	5821	Played the part that still looks back on the actor or actress,
LG1860	5822	The same old rôle, the rôle that is what we make it,as great as we like,
LG1860	5823	Or as small as we like, or both great and small.
LG1860	5824	Closer yet I approach you,
LG1860	5825	What thought you have of me, I had as much of you —I laid in my stores in advance,
LG1860	5826	I considered long and seriously of you before you were born.
LG1860	5827	Who was to know what should come home to me?
LG1860	5828	Who knows but I am enjoying this?
LG1860	5829	Who knows but I am as good as looking at you now,for all you cannot see me?
LG1860	5830	It is not you alone, nor I alone,
LG1860	5831	Not a few races, nor a few generations, nor a few centuries,
LG1860	5832	It is that each came, or comes, or shall come, from its due emission, without fail, either now, or then, or henceforth.
LG1860	5833	Every thing indicates—the smallest does, and the largest does,
LG1860	5834	A necessary film envelops all, and envelops the Soul for a proper time.
LG1860	5835	Now I am curious what sight can ever be more stately and admirable to me than my mast-hemm'd Man-hattaManhatta,

LG1860	5837	The sea-gulls oscillating their bodies, the hay-boat in the twilight, and the belated lighter;
LG1860	5838	Curious what Gods can exceed these that clasp me by the hand, and with voices I love call me promptly and loudly by my nighest name as I approach,
LG1860	5839	Curious what is more subtle than this which ties me to the woman or man that looks in my face,
LG1860	5840	Which fuses me into you now, and pours my meaning into you.
LG1860	5841	We understand, then, do we not?
LG1860	5842	What I promised without mentioning it, have you not accepted?
LG1860	5843	What the study could not teach—what the preaching could not accomplish is accomplished, is it not?
LG1860	5844	What the push of reading could not start is started by me personally, is it not?
LG1860	5845	Flow on, river! flow with the flood-tide, and ebb with the ebb-tide!
LG1860	5846	Frolic on, crested and scallop-edged waves!
LG1860	5847	Gorgeous clouds of the sunset! drench with your splendor me, or the men and women generations after me;

LG1860	5850	Bully for you! you proud, friendly, free Manhat-taneseManhattanese!
LG1860	5851	Throb, baffled and curious brain! throw out questions and answers!
LG1860	5852	Suspend here and everywhere, eternal float of solu-tionsolution!

LG1860	5857	Play the old rôle, the rôle that is great or small,according as one makes it!
LG1860	5858	Consider, you who peruse me, whether I may not in unknown ways be looking upon you;

LG1860	5861	Receive the summer-sky, you water! and faithfully hold it, till all downcast eyes have time to take it from you;

LG1860	5866	Appearances, now or henceforth, indicate what you are;
LG1860	5867	You necessary film, continue to envelop the Soul;
LG1860	5868	About my body for me, and your body for you, be hung our divinest aromas;
LG1860	5869	Thrive, cities! bring your freight, bring your shows,ample and sufficient rivers;
LG1860	5870	Expand, being than which none else is perhaps more spiritual;
LG1860	5871	Keep your places, objects than which none else is more lasting.
LG1860	5872	We descend upon you and all things—we arrest you all,
LG1860	5873	We realize the Soul only by you, you faithful solids and fluids,
