LG1860	5875	Through you every proof, comparison, and all the suggestions and determinations of ourselves.
LG1860	5876	You have waited, you always wait, you dumb, beauti-fulbeautiful ministers! you novices!
LG1860	5877	We receive you with free sense at last, and are insatiate henceforward,
LG1860	5878	Not you any more shall be able to foil us, or with-holdwithhold yourselves from us,
LG1860	5879	We use you, and do not cast you aside—we plant you permanently within us,
LG1860	5880	We fathom you not—we love you—there is per-fectionperfection in you also,
LG1860	5881	You furnish your parts toward eternity,
LG1860	5882	Great or small, you furnish your parts toward the Soul.
LG1860	5883	O MAGNET-SOUTH! O glistening, perfumed South! My South!
LG1860	5884	O quick mettle, rich blood, impulse, and love! Good and evil! O all dear to me!
LG1860	5885	O dear to me my birth-things—All moving things, and the trees where I was born—the grains, plants, rivers;
LG1860	5886	Dear to me my own slow sluggish rivers where they flow, distant, over flats of silvery sands, or through swamps,
LG1860	5887	Dear to me the Roanoke, the Savannah, the Altama-hawAltamahaw, the Pedee, the Tombigbee, the Santee, the Coosa, and the Sabine;
LG1860	5888	O pensive, far away wandering, I return with my Soul to haunt their banks again,
LG1860	5889	Again in Florida I float on transparent lakes—I float on the Okeechobee—I cross the hummock land, or through pleasant openings, or dense forests,
LG1860	5890	I see the parrots in the woods—I see the papaw tree and the blossoming titi;
LG1860	5891	Again, sailing in my coaster, on deck, I coast off Georgia—I coast up the Carolinas,
LG1860	5892	I see where the live-oak is growing—I see where the yellow-pine, the scented bay-tree, the lemon and orange, the cypress, the graceful palmetto;
LG1860	5893	I pass rude sea-headlands and enter Pamlico Sound through an inlet, and dart my vision inland,
LG1860	5894	O the cotton plant! the growing fields of rice, sugar, hemp!
LG1860	5895	The cactus, guarded with thorns—the laurel-tree, with large white flowers,
LG1860	5896	The range afar—the richness and barrenness—the old woods charged with mistletoe and trailing moss,
LG1860	5897	The piney odor and the gloom—the awful natural stillness, (Here in these dense swamps the free- booter carries his gun, and the fugitive slave has his concealed hut;)
LG1860	5898	O the strange fascination of these half-known, half- impassable swamps, infested by reptiles, resound-ingresounding with the bellow of the alligator, the sad noises of the night-owl and the wild-cat, and the whirr of the rattlesnake;
LG1860	5899	The mocking-bird, the American mimic, singing all the forenoon—singing through the moon-lit night,
LG1860	5900	The humming-bird, the wild-turkey, the raccoon, the opossum;
LG1860	5901	A Tennessee corn-field—the tall, graceful, long-leaved corn—slender, flapping, bright green, with tas-selstassels—with beautiful ears, each well-sheathed in its husk,
LG1860	5902	An Arkansas prairie—a sleeping lake, or still bayou;
LG1860	5903	O my heart! O tender and fierce pangs—I can stand them not—I will depart;
LG1860	5904	O to be a Virginian, where I grew up! O to be a Carolinian!
LG1860	5905	O longings irrepressible! O I will go back to old Ten-nesseeTennessee, and never wander more!
LG1860	5906	WHOEVER you are, I fear you are walking the walks of dreams,
LG1860	5907	I fear those realities are to melt from under your feet and hands;
LG1860	5908	Even now, your features, joys, speech, house, trade, manners, troubles, follies, costume, crimes, dis-sipatedissipate away from you,
LG1860	5909	Your true Soul and body appear before me,
LG1860	5910	They stand forth out of affairs—out of commerce, shops, law, science, work, farms, clothes, the house, medicine, print, buying, selling, eating, drinking, suffering, dying.
LG1860	5911	Whoever you are, now I place my hand upon you, that you be my poem,
LG1860	5912	I whisper with my lips close to your ear,
LG1860	5913	I have loved many women and men, but I love none better than you.
LG1860	5914	O I have been dilatory and dumb,
LG1860	5915	I should have made my way straight to you long ago,
LG1860	5916	I should have blabbed nothing but you, I should have chanted nothing but you.
LG1860	5917	I will leave all, and come and make the hymns of you;
LG1860	5918	None have understood you, but I understand you,
LG1860	5919	None have done justice to you—you have not done justice to yourself,
LG1860	5920	None but have found you imperfect—I only find no imperfection in you,
LG1860	5921	None but would subordinate you—I only am he who will never consent to subordinate you,
LG1860	5922	I only am he who places over you no master, owner, better, God, beyond what waits intrinsically in yourself.
LG1860	5923	Painters have painted their swarming groups, and the centre figure of all,
LG1860	5924	From the head of the centre figure spreading a nim-busnimbus of gold-colored light,
LG1860	5925	But I paint myriads of heads, but paint no head with-outwithout its nimbus of gold-colored light,
LG1860	5926	From my hand, from the brain of every man and woman it streams, effulgently flowing forever.
LG1860	5927	O I could sing such grandeurs and glories about you!
LG1860	5928	You have not known what you are—you have slum-beredslumbered upon yourself all your life,
LG1860	5929	Your eyelids have been the same as closed most of the time,
LG1860	5930	What you have done returns already in mockeries,
LG1860	5931	Your thrift, knowledge, prayers, if they do not return in mockeries, what is their return?
LG1860	5932	The mockeries are not you,
LG1860	5933	Underneath them, and within them, I see you lurk,
LG1860	5934	I pursue you where none else has pursued you,
LG1860	5935	Silence, the desk, the flippant expression, the night, the accustomed routine, if these conceal you from others, or from yourself, they do not conceal you from me,
LG1860	5936	The shaved face, the unsteady eye, the impure com-plexioncomplexion, if these balk others, they do not balk me,
LG1860	5937	The pert apparel, the deformed attitude, drunken-nessdrunkenness, greed, premature death, all these I part aside,
LG1860	5938	I track through your windings and turnings—I come upon you where you thought eye should never come upon you.
LG1860	5939	There is no endowment in man or woman that is not tallied in you,
LG1860	5940	There is no virtue, no beauty, in man or woman, but as good is in you,
LG1860	5941	No pluck, no endurance in others, but as good is in you,
LG1860	5942	No pleasure waiting for others, but an equal pleasure waits for you.
LG1860	5943	As for me, I give nothing to any one, except I give the like carefully to you,
LG1860	5944	I sing the songs of the glory of none, not God, sooner than I sing the songs of the glory of you.
LG1860	5945	Whoever you are! claim your own at any hazard!
LG1860	5946	These shows of the east and west are tame compared to you,
LG1860	5947	These immense meadows—these interminable rivers —you are immense and interminable as they,
LG1860	5948	These furies, elements, storms, motions of Nature, throes of apparent dissolution—you are he or she who is master or mistress over them,
LG1860	5949	Master or mistress in your own right over Nature, elements, pain, passion, dissolution.
LG1860	5950	The hopples fall from your ankles—you find an un-failingunfailing sufficiency,
LG1860	5951	Old or young, male or female, rude, low, rejected by the rest, whatever you are promulges itself,
LG1860	5952	Through birth, life, death, burial, the means are pro-videdprovided, nothing is scanted,
LG1860	5953	Through angers, losses, ambition, ignorance, ennui, what you are picks its way.
LG1860	5954	COURAGE! my brother or my sister!
LG1860	5955	Keep on! Liberty is to be subserved, whatever occurs;
LG1860	5956	That is nothing, that is quelled by one or two failures, or any number of failures,
LG1860	5957	Or by the indifference or ingratitude of the people, or by any unfaithfulness,
LG1860	5958	Or the show of the tushes of power—soldiers, cannon, penal statutes.
LG1860	5959	What we believe in waits latent forever through Asia, Africa, Europe, North and South America, Australia, Cuba, and all the islands and archi-pelagoesarchipelagoes of the sea.
LG1860	5960	What we believe in invites no one, promises nothing, sits in calmness and light, is positive and com-posedcomposed, knows no discouragement,
LG1860	5961	Waits patiently its time—a year—a century—a hundred centuries.
LG1860	5962	The battle rages with many a loud alarm and fre-quentfrequent advance and retreat,
LG1860	5963	The infidel triumphs—or supposes he triumphs,
LG1860	5964	The prison, scaffold, garrote, hand-cuffs, iron necklace and anklet, lead-balls, do their work,
LG1860	5965	The named and unnamed heroes pass to other spheres,
LG1860	5966	The great speakers and writers are exiled—they lie sick in distant lands,
LG1860	5967	The cause is asleep—the strongest throats are still, choked with their own blood,
LG1860	5968	The young men drop their eyelashes toward the ground when they meet,
LG1860	5969	But for all this, liberty has not gone out of the place, nor the infidel entered into possession.
LG1860	5970	When liberty goes out of a place, it is not the first to go, nor the second or third to go,
LG1860	5971	It waits for all the rest to go—it is the last.
LG1860	5972	When there are no more memories of the superb lovers of the nations of the world,
LG1860	5973	The superb lovers' names scouted in the public gatherings by the lips of the orators,
LG1860	5974	Boys not christened after them, but christened after traitors and murderers instead,
LG1860	5975	Tyrants' and priests' successes really acknowledged anywhere, for all the ostensible appearance,
LG1860	5976	You or I walking abroad upon the earth, elated at the sight of slaves, no matter who they are,
LG1860	5977	And when all life, and all the Souls of men and women are discharged from any part of the earth,
LG1860	5978	Then shall the instinct of liberty be discharged from that part of the earth,
LG1860	5979	Then shall the infidel and the tyrant come into possession.
LG1860	5980	Then courage!
LG1860	5981	For till all ceases, neither must you cease.
LG1860	5982	I do not know what you are for, (I do not what I am for myself, nor what any thing is for,)
LG1860	5983	But I will search carefully for it in being foiled,
LG1860	5984	In defeat, poverty, imprisonment—for they too are great.
LG1860	5985	Did we think victory great?
LG1860	5986	So it is—But now it seems to me, when it cannot be helped, that defeat is great,
LG1860	5987	And that death and dismay are great.
LG1860	5988	MY spirit to yours, dear brother,
LG1860	5989	Do not mind because many, sounding your name, do not understand you,
LG1860	5990	I do not sound your name, but I understand you,(there are others also;)
LG1860	5991	I specify you with joy, O my comrade, to salute you,and to salute those who are with you, before and since—and those to come also,
LG1860	5992	That we all labor together, transmitting the same charge and succession;
LG1860	5993	We few, equals, indifferent of lands, indifferent of times,
LG1860	5994	We, enclosers of all continents, all castes—allowers of all theologies,
LG1860	5995	Compassionaters, perceivers, rapport of men,
LG1860	5996	We walk silent among disputes and assertions, but reject not the disputers, nor any thing that is asserted,

LG1860	5998	They close peremptorily upon us, to surround us,my comrade,

LG1860	6000	Till we saturate time and eras, that the men and women of races, ages to come, may prove breth-renbrethren and lovers, as we are.
LG1860	6001	FROM all the rest I single out you, having a message for you:
LG1860	6002	You are to die—Let others tell you what they please, I cannot prevaricate,
LG1860	6003	I am exact and merciless, but I love you—There is no escape for you.
LG1860	6004	Softly I lay my right hand upon you—you just feel it,
LG1860	6005	I do not argue—I bend my head close, and half- envelop it,
LG1860	6006	I sit quietly by—I remain faithful,
LG1860	6007	I am more than nurse, more than parent or neighbor,
LG1860	6008	I absolve you from all except yourself, spiritual, bodily—that is eternal,
LG1860	6009	(The corpse you will leave will be but excremen-titiousecrementitious.)
LG1860	6010	The sun bursts through in unlooked-for directions!
LG1860	6011	Strong thoughts fill you, and confidence—you smile!
LG1860	6012	You forget you are sick, as I forget you are sick,
LG1860	6013	You do not see the medicines—you do not mind the weeping friends—I am with you,
LG1860	6014	I exclude others from you—there is nothing to be commiserated,
LG1860	6015	I do not commiserate—I congratulate you.
LG1860	6016	BE composed—be at ease with me—I am Walt Whitman, liberal and lusty as Nature,
LG1860	6017	Not till the sun excludes you, do I exclude you,
LG1860	6018	Not till the waters refuse to glisten for you, and the leaves to rustle for you, do my words refuse to glisten and rustle for you.
LG1860	6019	My girl, I appoint with you an appointment—and I charge you that you make preparation to be worthy to meet me,
LG1860	6020	And I charge you that you be patient and perfect till I come.
LG1860	6021	Till then, I salute you with a significant look, that you do not forget me.
LG1860	6022	WHAT you give me, I cheerfully accept,
LG1860	6023	A little sustenance, a hut and garden, a little money —these as I rendezvous with my poems,
LG1860	6024	A traveller's lodging and breakfast as I journey through The States—Why should I be ashamed to own such gifts? Why to advertise for them?
LG1860	6025	For I myself am not one who bestows nothing upon man and woman,
LG1860	6026	For I know that what I bestow upon any man or woman is no less than the entrance to all the gifts of the universe.
LG1860	6027	IS reform needed? Is it through you?
LG1860	6028	The greater the reform needed, the greater the PER-SONALITYPERSONALITY you need to accomplish it.
LG1860	6029	You! do you not see how it would serve to have eyes, blood, complexion, clean and sweet?
LG1860	6030	Do you not see how it would serve to have such a body and Soul, that when you enter the crowd, an atmosphere of desire and command enters with you, and every one is impressed with your personality?
LG1860	6031	O the magnet! the flesh over and over!
LG1860	6032	Go, mon cher! if need be, give up all else, and com-mencecommence to-day to inure yourself to pluck, reality, self-esteem, definiteness, elevatedness,
LG1860	6033	Rest not, till you rivet and publish yourself of your own personality.
LG1860	6034	WHY reclining, interrogating? Why myself and all drowsing?
LG1860	6035	What deepening twilight! Scum floating atop of the waters!
LG1860	6036	Who are they, as bats and night-dogs, askant in the Capitol?

LG1860	6039	Then I will sleep a while yet—for I see that These States sleep, for reasons;

LG1860	6042	HERE, take this gift!
LG1860	6043	I was reserving it for some hero, orator, or general,
LG1860	6044	One who should serve the good old cause, the prog-ressprogress and freedom of the race, the cause of my Soul;
LG1860	6045	But I see that what I was reserving belongs to you just as much as to any.
LG1860	6046	TO The States, or any one of them, or any city of The States, Resist much, obey little,
LG1860	6047	Once unquestioning obedience, once fully enslaved,
LG1860	6048	Once fully enslaved, no nation, state, city, of this earth, ever afterward resumes its liberty.
LG1860	6049	ALL you are doing and saying is to America dangled mirages,
LG1860	6050	You have not learned of Nature—of the politics of Nature, you have not learned the great ampli-tudeamplitude, rectitude, impartiality,
LG1860	6051	You have not seen that only such as they are for These States,
LG1860	6052	And that what is less than they, must sooner or later lift off from These States.
LG1860	6053	I HEAR you have been asking for something to repre-sentrepresent the new race, our self-poised Democracy,

LG1860	6055	I SEE in you the estuary that enlarges and spreads itself grandly as it pours in the great sea.
LG1860	6056	LET us twain walk aside from the rest;
LG1860	6057	Now we are together privately, do you discard cer-emonyceremony,

LG1860	6059	Tell me what you would not tell your brother, wife,husband, or physician.
LG1860	6060	STRANGER! if you, passing, meet me, and desire to speak to me, why should you not speak to me?
LG1860	6061	And why should I not speak to you?
LG1860	6062	I WAS asking for something specific and perfect for my city, and behold! here is the aboriginal name!
LG1860	6063	Now I see what there is in a name, a word, liquid, sane, unruly, musical, self-sufficient,
LG1860	6064	I see that the word of my city, is that word up there,
LG1860	6065	Because I see that word nested in nests of water-bays, superb, with tall and wonderful spires,
LG1860	6066	Rich, hemmed thick all around with sailships and steamships—an island sixteen miles long, solid- founded,
LG1860	6067	Numberless crowded streets—high growths of iron, slender, strong, light, splendidly uprising toward clear skies;
LG1860	6068	Tides swift and ample, well-loved by me, toward sun-downsundown,

LG1860	6070	The countless masts, the white shore-steamers, the lighters, the ferry-boats, the black sea-steamers, well-model'd;

LG1860	6073	The carts hauling goods—the manly race of drivers of horses—the brown-faced sailors,

LG1860	6080	The free city! no slaves! no owners of slaves!
LG1860	6081	The beautiful city! the city of hurried and sparkling waters! the city of spires and masts!
LG1860	6082	The city nested in bays! my city!
LG1860	6083	The city of such women, I am mad to be with them! I will return after death to be with them!
LG1860	6084	The city of such young men, I swear I cannot live happy, without I often go talk, walk, eat, drink, sleep, with them!
LG1860	6085	A GREAT year and place,
LG1860	6086	A harsh, discordant, natal scream rising, to touch the mother's heart closer than any yet.
LG1860	6087	I walked the shores of my Eastern Sea,
LG1860	6088	Heard over the waves the little voice,
LG1860	6089	Saw the divine infant, where she woke, mournfully wailing, amid the roar of cannon, curses, shouts,crash of falling buildings,
LG1860	6090	Was not so sick from the blood in the gutters running —nor from the single corpses, nor those in heaps,nor those borne away in the tumbrils,
LG1860	6091	Was not so desperate at the battues of death—was not so shocked at the repeated fusillades of the guns.
LG1860	6092	Pale, silent, stern, what could I say to that long-accrued retribution?
LG1860	6093	Could I wish humanity different?
LG1860	6094	Could I wish the people made of wood and stone?
LG1860	6095	Or that there be no justice in destiny or time?
LG1860	6096	O Liberty! O mate for me!
LG1860	6097	Here too keeps the blaze, the bullet and the axe, in reserve, to fetch them out in case of need,
LG1860	6098	Here too, though long deprest, still is not destroyed,
LG1860	6099	Here too could rise at last, murdering and extatic,
LG1860	6100	Here too would demand full arrears of vengeance.
LG1860	6101	Hence I sign this salute over the sea,
LG1860	6102	And I do not deny that terrible red birth and baptism,
LG1860	6103	But remember the little voice that I heard wailing—and wait with perfect trust, no matter how long,
LG1860	6104	And from to-day, sad and cogent, I maintain the bequeath'd cause, as for all lands,
LG1860	6105	And I send these words to Paris, with my love,
LG1860	6106	And I guess some chansonniers there will understand them,
LG1860	6107	For I guess there is latent music yet in France—floods of it,
LG1860	6108	O I hear already the bustle of instruments—they will soon be drowning all that would interrupt them,
LG1860	6109	O I think the east wind brings a triumphal and free march,
LG1860	6110	It reaches hither—it swells me to joyful madness,
LG1860	6111	I will run transpose it in words, to justify it,
LG1860	6112	I will yet sing a song for you, ma femme.
LG1860	6113	OF the visages of things—And of piercing through to the accepted hells beneath;
LG1860	6114	Of ugliness—To me there is just as much in it as there is in beauty—And now the ugliness of human beings is acceptable to me;
LG1860	6115	Of detected persons—To me, detected persons are not, in any respect, worse than undetected per-sonspersons—and are not in any respect worse than I am myself;
LG1860	6116	Of criminals—To me, any judge, or any juror, is equally criminal—and any reputable person is also—and the President is also.
LG1860	6117	OF waters, forests, hills,
LG1860	6118	Of the earth at large, whispering through medium of me;
LG1860	6119	Of vista—Suppose some sight in arriere, through the formative chaos, presuming the growth, fulness,life, now attained on the journey;
LG1860	6120	(But I see the road continued, and the journey ever continued;)
LG1860	6121	Of what was once lacking on the earth, and in due time has become supplied—And of what will yet be supplied,
LG1860	6122	Because all I see and know, I believe to have purport in what will yet be supplied.
LG1860	6123	OF persons arrived at high positions, ceremonies,wealth, scholarships, and the like,
LG1860	6124	To me, all that those persons have arrived at, sinks away from them, except as it results to their bodies and Souls,
LG1860	6125	So that often to me they appear gaunt and naked,
LG1860	6126	And often, to me, each one mocks the others, and mocks himself or herself,
LG1860	6127	And of each one, the core of life, namely happiness,is full of the rotten excrement of maggots,
LG1860	6128	And often, to me, those men and women pass un-wittinglyunwittingly the true realities of life, and go toward false realities,
LG1860	6129	And often, to me, they are alive after what custom has served them, but nothing more,
LG1860	6130	And often, to me, they are sad, hasty, unwaked son-nambulessonnambules, walking the dusk.
LG1860	6131	OF ownership—As if one fit to own things could not at pleasure enter upon all, and incorporate them into himself or herself;
LG1860	6132	Of Equality—As if it harmed me, giving others the same chances and rights as myself—As if it were not indispensable to my own rights that others possess the same;
LG1860	6133	Of Justice—As if Justice could be any thing but the same ample law, expounded by natural judges and saviours,
LG1860	6134	As if it might be this thing or that thing, according to decisions.
LG1860	6135	AS I sit with others, at a great feast, suddenly, while the music is playing,
LG1860	6136	To my mind, (whence it comes I know not,) spectral,in mist, of a wreck at sea,
LG1860	6137	Of the flower of the marine science of fifty genera-tionsgenerations, foundered off the Northeast coast, and going down—Of the steamship Arctic going down,
LG1860	6138	Of the veiled tableau—Women gathered together on deck, pale, heroic, waiting the moment that draws so close—O the moment!
LG1860	6139	O the huge sob—A few bubbles—the white foam spirting up—And then the women gone,
LG1860	6140	Sinking there, while the passionless wet flows on—And I now pondering, Are those women indeed gone?
LG1860	6141	Are Souls drowned and destroyed so?
LG1860	6142	Is only matter triumphant?
LG1860	6143	OF what I write from myself—As if that were not the resumé;
LG1860	6144	Of Histories—As if such, however complete, were not less complete than my poems;
LG1860	6145	As if the shreds, the records of nations, could possibly be as lasting as my poems;
LG1860	6146	As if here were not the amount of all nations, and of all the lives of heroes.
LG1860	6147	OF obedience, faith, adhesiveness;
LG1860	6148	As I stand aloof and look, there is to me something profoundly affecting in large masses of men, fol-lowingfollowing the lead of those who do not believe in men.
LG1860	6149	NATIONS ten thousand years before These States, and many times ten thousand years before These States,
LG1860	6150	Garnered clusters of ages, that men and women like us grew up and travelled their course, and passed on;
LG1860	6151	What vast-built cities—What orderly republics—What pastoral tribes and nomads,

LG1860	6154	What sort of marriage—What costumes—What physiology and phrenology,
LG1860	6155	What of liberty and slavery among them—What they thought of death and the Soul,

LG1860	6157	Not a mark, not a record remains—And yet all remains.
LG1860	6158	O I know that those men and women were not for nothing, any more than we are for nothing,

LG1860	6160	Afar they stand—yet near to me they stand,

LG1860	6175	WHO includes diversity, and is Nature,
LG1860	6176	Who is the amplitude of the earth, and the coarseness and sexuality of the earth, and the great charity of the earth, and the equilibrium also,

LG1860	6463	The night and sleep have likened them and restored them.
LG1860	6464	I swear they are all beautiful!
LG1860	6465	Every one that sleeps is beautiful—everything in the dim light is beautiful,
LG1860	6466	The wildest and bloodiest is over, and all is peace.
LG1860	6467	Peace is always beautiful,
LG1860	6468	The myth of heaven indicates peace and night.
LG1860	6469	The myth of heaven indicates the Soul;
LG1860	6470	The Soul is always beautiful—it appears more or it appears less—it comes, or it lags behind,
LG1860	6471	It comes from its embowered garden, and looks pleasantly on itself, and encloses the world,
LG1860	6472	Perfect and clean the genitals previously jetting, and perfect and clean the womb cohering,
LG1860	6473	The head well-grown, proportioned and plumb, and the bowels and joints proportioned and plumb.
LG1860	6474	The Soul is always beautiful,
LG1860	6475	The universe is duly in order, everything is in its place,
LG1860	6476	What is arrived is in its place, and what waits is in its place;
LG1860	6477	The twisted skull waits, the watery or rotten blood waits,
LG1860	6478	The child of the glutton or venerealee waits long, and the child of the drunkard waits long, and the drunkard himself waits long,
LG1860	6479	The sleepers that lived and died wait—the far advanced are to go on in their turns, and the far behind are to go on in their turns,
LG1860	6480	The diverse shall be no less diverse, but they shall flow and unite—they unite now.
LG1860	6481	The sleepers are very beautiful as they lie unclothed,
LG1860	6482	They flow hand in hand over the whole earth, from east to west, as they lie unclothed,
LG1860	6483	The Asiatic and African are hand in hand—the European and American are hand in hand,
LG1860	6484	Learned and unlearned are hand in hand, and male and female are hand in hand,
LG1860	6485	The bare arm of the girl crosses the bare breast of her lover—they press close without lust—his lips press her neck,
LG1860	6486	The father holds his grown or ungrown son in his arms with measureless love, and the son holds the father in his arms with measureless love,
LG1860	6487	The white hair of the mother shines on the white wrist of the daughter,
LG1860	6488	The breath of the boy goes with the breath of the man, friend is inarmed by friend,
LG1860	6489	The scholar kisses the teacher, and the teacher kisses the scholar—the wronged is made right,
LG1860	6490	The call of the slave is one with the master's call, and the master salutes the slave,
LG1860	6491	The felon steps forth from the prison—the insane becomes sane—the suffering of sick persons is relieved,
LG1860	6492	The sweatings and fevers stop—the throat that was unsound is sound—the lungs of the consumptive are resumed—the poor distressed head is free,
LG1860	6493	The joints of the rheumatic move as smoothly as ever,and smoother than ever,
LG1860	6494	Stiflings and passages open—the paralyzed become supple,
LG1860	6495	The swelled and convulsed and congested awake to themselves in condition,
LG1860	6496	They pass the invigoration of the night, and the chemistry of the night, and awake.
LG1860	6497	I too pass from the night,
LG1860	6498	I stay a while away O night, but I return to you again, and love you.
LG1860	6499	Why should I be afraid to trust myself to you?
LG1860	6500	I am not afraid—I have been well brought forward by you,
LG1860	6501	I love the rich running day, but I do not desert her in whom I lay so long,
LG1860	6502	I know not how I came of you, and I know not where I go with you—but I know I came well, and shall go well.
LG1860	6503	I will stop only a time with the night, and rise betimes,
LG1860	6504	I will duly pass the day, O my mother, and duly return to you.
LG1860	6505	TO think of it!
LG1860	6506	To think of time—of all that retrospection!
LG1860	6507	To think of to-day, and the ages continued hence-forwardhenceforward!
LG1860	6508	Have you guessed you yourself would not continue?
LG1860	6509	Have you dreaded those earth-beetles?
LG1860	6510	Have you feared the future would be nothing to you?
LG1860	6511	Is to-day nothing? Is the beginningless past nothing?
LG1860	6512	If the future is nothing, they are just as surely nothing.
LG1860	6513	To think that the sun rose in the east! that men and women were flexible, real, alive! that every-thingeverything was alive!
LG1860	6514	To think that you and I did not see, feel, think, nor bear our part!
LG1860	6515	To think that we are now here, and bear our part!
LG1860	6516	Not a day passes—not a minute or second, without an accouchment!
LG1860	6517	Not a day passes—not a minute or second, without a corpse!
LG1860	6518	The dull nights go over, and the dull days also,
LG1860	6519	The soreness of lying so much in bed goes over,
LG1860	6520	The physician, after long putting off, gives the silent and terrible look for an answer,
LG1860	6521	The children come hurried and weeping, and the brothers and sisters are sent for,
LG1860	6522	Medicines stand unused on the shelf—(the camphor-smell has long pervaded the rooms,)
LG1860	6523	The faithful hand of the living does not desert the hand of the dying,
LG1860	6524	The twitching lips press lightly on the forehead of the dying,
LG1860	6525	The breath ceases, and the pulse of the heart ceases,
LG1860	6526	The corpse stretches on the bed, and the living look upon it,
LG1860	6527	It is palpable as the living are palpable.
LG1860	6528	The living look upon the corpse with their eye-sight,
LG1860	6529	But without eye-sight lingers a different living, and looks curiously on the corpse.
LG1860	6530	To think that the rivers will flow, and the snow fall,and fruits ripen, and act upon others as upon us now—yet not act upon us!
LG1860	6531	To think of all these wonders of city and country,and others taking great interest in them—and we taking no interest in them!
LG1860	6532	To think how eager we are in building our houses!
LG1860	6533	To think others shall be just as eager, and we quite indifferent!
LG1860	6534	I see one building the house that serves him a few years, or seventy or eighty years at most,
LG1860	6535	I see one building the house that serves him longer than that.
LG1860	6536	Slow-moving and black lines creep over the whole earth—they never cease—they are the burial lines,
LG1860	6537	He that was President was buried, and he that is now President shall surely be buried.
LG1860	6538	Cold dash of waves at the ferry-wharf—posh and ice in the river, half-frozen mud in the streets,a gray discouraged sky overhead, the short last daylight of Twelfth Month,
LG1860	6539	A hearse and stages—other vehicles give place—the funeral of an old Broadway stage-driver, the cortege mostly drivers.
LG1860	6540	Steady the trot to the cemetery, duly rattles the death-bell, the gate is passed, the new-dug grave is halted at, the living alight, the hearse un-closesuncloses,
LG1860	6541	The coffin is passed out, lowered and settled, the whip is laid on the coffin, the earth is swiftly shovelled in,
LG1860	6542	The mound above is flatted with the spades—silence,
LG1860	6543	A minute, no one moves or speaks—it is done,
LG1860	6544	He is decently put away—is there anything more?
LG1860	6545	He was a good fellow, free-mouthed, quick-tempered,not bad-looking, able to take his own part, witty,sensitive to a slight, ready with life or death for a friend, fond of women, gambled, ate hearty,drank hearty, had known what it was to be flush, grew low-spirited toward the last, sickened,was helped by a contribution, died, aged forty-one years—and that was his funeral.
LG1860	6546	Thumb extended, finger uplifted, apron, cape, gloves,strap, wet-weather clothes, whip carefully chosen,boss, spotter, starter, hostler, somebody loafing on you, you loafing on somebody, head-way, man before and man behind, good day's work, bad day's work, pet stock, mean stock, first out, last out, turning in at night,
LG1860	6547	To think that these are so much and so nigh to other drivers—and he there takes no interest in them!
LG1860	6548	The markets, the government, the working-man's wages—to think what account they are through our nights and days!
LG1860	6549	To think that other working-men will make just as great account of them—yet we make little or no account!
LG1860	6550	The vulgar and the refined—what you call sin and what you call goodness—to think how wide a difference!
LG1860	6551	To think the difference will still continue to others,yet we lie beyond the difference.
LG1860	6552	To think how much pleasure there is!
LG1860	6553	Have you pleasure from looking at the sky? have you pleasure from poems?
LG1860	6554	Do you enjoy yourself in the city? or engaged in business? or planning a nomination and elec-tionelection? or with your wife and family?

LG1860	6558	Your farm, profits, crops,—to think how engrossed you are!
LG1860	6559	To think there will still be farms, profits, crops—yet for you, of what avail?
LG1860	6560	What will be, will be well—for what is, is well,
LG1860	6561	To take interest is well, and not to take interest shall be well.
LG1860	6562	The sky continues beautiful,
LG1860	6563	The pleasure of men with women shall never be sated,nor the pleasure of women with men, nor the pleasure from poems,

LG1860	6566	The difference between sin and goodness is no delusion,
LG1860	6567	The earth is not an echo—man and his life, and all the things of his life, are well-considered.

LG1860	6578	The law of the past cannot be eluded,
LG1860	6579	The law of the present and future cannot be eluded,
LG1860	6580	The law of the living cannot be eluded—it is eter-naleternal,

LG1860	6595	I shall go with the rest—we have satisfaction,
LG1860	6596	I have dreamed that we are not to be changed so much, nor the law of us changed,
LG1860	6597	I have dreamed that heroes and good-doers shall be under the present and past law,

LG1860	6600	And I have dreamed that the satisfaction is not so much changed, and that there is no life with-outwithout satisfaction;
LG1860	6601	What is the earth? what are body and Soul, without satisfaction?
LG1860	6602	I shall go with the rest,
LG1860	6603	We cannot be stopped at a given point—that is no satisfaction,
